It holds one row per experiment run and model with the templates and the prompts as they were sent - the `{{variables}}` filled in the way the Prompt Builder does it, a decision run with its questions as stored - and the hash of each, taken of the whole text before it is cut to the length of its column, and with what the tracker holds of that call: its run time and its token counts (`run_time`, `prompt_length`, `output_length`) - the run time tells the request of a run from another request with the same prompts; one row per RAG setup that names a collection, with its embedding model and in `template_source` who wrote the model, `builder` or `step` (`role` `rag`); one row per run that was judged and model that judged it, with the prompts of the run, which the Prompt Builder shows a judge in the user prompt of its call (`role` `judge`); one row per optimization and model it called, with the start and the end of the job in UTC (`window_start`, `window_end`), its id (`optimization_id`) and the system prompt it started from (`role` `optimization`); and one row per LLM, embedding model and decision model (`role` `llm`, `embedding`, `decision_model`), whose `name` is the name of its container. The definition projects are found by name (`LLM Model Project`, `Embedding Model Project`, `Decision Model Project`), and each setting also takes a project id or `tag:<tag>`, several separated by `|`; a project that is not found is a WARNING, an empty setting leaves the kind out. Measured outside of SAS on files saved from the test system: the templates were read from nine manifested score codes, one of them a decision template manifested with 2.2.0 whose score code does not compile and is read statement by statement; the questions and the state template read from the score code of one decision template fit 41 of the 105 requests to its decision model in a collected log of 1,040 requests, and the questions of its tracker equalled the questions of those requests character by character. On the test system on 2026-10-11 the catalog of that day - fifteen columns, no rows for judges and optimizations - was written with 365 rows: the prompts as sent of 70 runs and models of two templates were found in the collected log by their hashes, and a setup whose properties SAS Model Manager did not return (HTTP 500) had a row without a collection. Such a setup is left out of the catalog since, with a WARNING. The six further columns, the two further roles and that rule ran on the test system on 2026-10-12: the catalog had 21 columns and 421 rows - 285 `prompt`, 47 `decision`, 24 `judge`, 35 `optimization`, 14 `rag`, 8 `llm`, 3 `embedding` and 5 `decision_model` -, its key was unique, `run_time`, `prompt_length` and `output_length` were numbers in CAS, and three setups without a collection were left out with a WARNING each. An `optimizationId` that a tracker holds on two entries gets a counter in the catalog since (see *Fixed*, with what was measured of it later that day).
- **`Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` links every log row to the model that answered and to the prompt template, decision template or RAG setup that sent the request.** A new program in `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Logging-Monitoring` fills the link columns of the log table (`model_id`, `caller_model_id`, `caller_project_id`, `caller_kind`, `caller_name`, `experiment_run_id`, `rag_model_id`, `match_method`, `match_candidates`) with SAS Model Manager ids. It reads the log table, the table `MODEL_CATALOG` that `Get-All-Prompts.sas` writes and, when it is there, `RAG_RUNS`; all three are macro variables at the top of the program (defaults `Public.LLM_LOGS`, `Public.MODEL_CATALOG`, `Public.RAG_RUNS`). The model that answered is the catalog row whose name is the name of the container. No request and no log line names its caller, so every other link is made by content, in this order: the prompts of an experiment run of the Builder, compared by their hashes, for a request a browser can have sent and whose run time is the run time the tracker holds for the run - a run is given to one request (`experiment run`); the questions of a decision template with or without a state that fits its state template (`questions and state`, `questions`); the templates of a prompt with every `{{variable}}` standing for any text (`template`); a call the Prompt Builder made for a prompt - a request that holds the text of a catalog row of an optimization or of a judge, went to the model of that row and, for an optimization, was sent inside its window (`optimization`, `judge`; a judge call also gets the run it judged); for embedding rows the query of a retrieval that an LLM row carries within `_lnk_query_window` seconds behind it (`retrieval query`, 300 seconds by default) and the window of an ingestion run (`ingestion run`). An LLM row whose prompt carries a retrieval also gets the RAG setup of its collection in `rag_model_id`, beside its prompt. A row that fits two callers equally well is `ambiguous`, a row that fits nothing is `none`; a template takes part with at least 20 characters of fixed text (`_lnk_min_literal`); where a setup saved in the Builder and the model the step *RAG - Register Setup* registered for it hold one collection, the setup of the Builder is taken. The program links the rows without a `match_method` and leaves every other row as it is; `_lnk_redo_all = 1` links all rows again, and a run that links no row leaves the table, its promotion and its saved file as they are. The twelve columns of every release and every column the program does not know stay in CAS and are not rewritten: the rows are numbered in a copy in `CASUSER`, only the link columns are uploaded and merged back by that number, and the new table replaces the old one the way the parser replaces a table - built as `<table>_NEW`, given the label of the table, promoted and saved; a table that is promoted or has a saved file stays so whatever `_lnk_promote_table` and `_lnk_save_table` say. A log table without the link columns is refused with one ERROR line that names the parser, a missing catalog with one ERROR line that names `Get-All-Prompts.sas`. A row or a catalog row whose content can not be read gets no link and is named in a WARNING, and so are the rows that no longer find the experiment run they were linked to: the id of a run is its place in the tracker, and the Prompt Builder numbers the runs again when one is deleted. The program ran on the test system on 2026-10-11 and on 2026-10-12, each time as it was that day, and was changed by what each run showed - see the next entry. The guide names what cannot be linked and why, how the tables join, and the way to exact links that is not built: a caller id that the manifested models send themselves.
- **The chain of the three programs was measured on SAS Viya.** On the test system on 2026-10-11 the parser, the pull and the link program as they were that day ran one after the other in 23 SAS Job Execution jobs of the *SAS Studio compute context*, on tables of their own in `CASUSER` and a redacted log of 1040 requests; the CAS server of that system runs without workers. For the parse see the entry of `LLM_LOGS` above and the append under *Changed*, for the pull its entry above. The link: `1040 of 1040 rows ... were linked against 365 catalog rows in 0.09 seconds`, and every row got a `model_id`. Counted in the table, the LLM rows were `experiment run` 32, `template` 144 and `none` 72, the decision rows `experiment run` 50, `questions and state` 301 and `none` 4, the embedding rows `retrieval query` 239, `ingestion run` 3, `ambiguous` 72 and `none` 123. The table kept its 36 columns with their order, types and labels, the order of its rows and every value of its other 27 columns - also a table of 3120 rows, which takes two reads. A second run linked 0 rows and a run with `_lnk_redo_all = 1` wrote the same links; the two refusals left their table untouched; tables that were saved and not in memory were loaded. For all 769 linked rows the caller was compared with the content of the row afterwards, outside of SAS, and was right. The three statements of the guide's example ran, and the joins counted what the link columns say: 527 rows to `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS`, 242 and 238 to `RAG_SETUPS`, 1040 to the answering rows of the catalog. What did not hold on 2026-10-11, each with the change that was made and with what the changed programs did on the same system on 2026-10-12, in 35 jobs: (1) the three summary lines of the link held semicolons, which ended the statement a line is written to the SAS log with, so the rest ran as SAS code - four `ERROR 180-322` in each of the seven runs that reached the summary, and a job that ended as failed although the table was written correctly; every message is now made one text on its way, and the summary is worded without a semicolon - on 2026-10-12 no log held an `ERROR 180-322`, the job of the link ended as completed, and the summary lines arrived in full: 32, 144, 49, 13 and 10 LLM rows by `experiment run`, `template`, `optimization`, `judge` and `none`, 41, 310 and 4 decision rows by `experiment run`, `questions and state` and `none`, and 138, 3, 72 and 224 embedding rows by `retrieval query`, `ingestion run`, `ambiguous` and `none`. (2) Every request with the prompts of a run got the newest run with these prompts: 82 rows for 70 runs and models, three of them with the id of another run; a row now gets the run whose run time is its own, and a run is given to one row - on 2026-10-12 73 rows for 73 runs and models, each with the run time the tracker holds for it. (3) A query embedding was linked through any prompt with its question, 18 of them through prompts that had been sent 20 minutes to two hours before; the prompt now has to follow within `_lnk_query_window` seconds - on 2026-10-12 138 of the 290 query embeddings were linked, where 239 had been; of 84 that were sent before the first prompt of their collection 83 were `none`, and one, sent 178 seconds before a run of the Prompt Builder with the same text, is still linked. (4) The 49 calls of a prompt optimization and the 13 calls of a judge were `none`; they are now linked to their prompt - on 2026-10-12 49 and 13, and 10 LLM rows stayed `none`. (5) A second run with 0 rows linked built, promoted and saved the table again; it now leaves the table as it is - on 2026-10-12 with one NOTE, the same modified time and the same saved file. (6) The label of the table was lost by the link and by an append; both now give the new table the label - on 2026-10-12 the table had its label after the append and after the link, and a label of a site's own stayed after the append; the link was not run on such a table. (7) A table name of 34 characters for an output of the pull ended in `ERROR: The table ... was not written.` for a table that had been written, promoted and saved; such a name is now refused before anything is read - on 2026-10-12 with its ERROR line, and no table was written. (8) In one SAS session `Get-All-Prompts.sas` and the second run of the link wrote `WARNING: A session with the name MYSESS already exists.`, and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` ended the session of the parser; the three programs now ask `SESSFOUND` (see *Fixed*) - on 2026-10-12 parser, pull, parser, pull, link and link ran in one SAS session without that warning, also behind a CAS session of the user, which was still connected at the end. (9) The run table of the setup the Prompt Builder had saved did not exist - its ingestion job published a ledger only -, so none of its document embeddings was linked to an ingestion run: of the 147 document embeddings 3 were, 72 were `ambiguous` inside runs of run tables that several models registered by the step share, and 72 were `none`; the pull now takes the runs a ledger still names, which is the newest run only - on 2026-10-12 one run of six seconds for that setup, in which the log holds no document embedding, so no link changed; from this release on the ingestion job publishes its run table itself (see *Changed*), which was measured later that day with a setup of its own (see the entry below). Checked outside of SAS after the second run: all 730 linked rows against the texts, run times, windows and runs of the catalog, none of them wrong; one link is doubtful - the query embedding of 178 seconds named above, which is linked through a prompt that is not its own. What did not hold in the second run, each with what the programs do since - which ran later that day, see the entry below: (a) the pull's refusal of a table name and the link's refusal of a table of twelve columns ended with `SYSCC` 0 and a completed job; a run that wrote an ERROR line and no table now sets `SYSCC` to 1012. (b) With an engine other than CAS the parser wrote four ERROR lines, the last about a log it had not read; it now ends with one line. (c) A response that held a request line with a line of text behind it gave a second row, a case that was not named before; it is named now, and the row is still written. (d) Files made by hand whose times were not in file order gave `failed` = 1 for answered requests without a NOTE, and one request the numbers of another; the next part of a file now begins where the time goes back by more than a second, and a request the lines of its container end on has `failed` missing. See *Fixed* for each. Not measured: the default table names - one chain of 2026-10-12 wrote to `Public` under names of its own -, a CAS server with workers, the *LLM Usage Report* opened on the tables, and what the programs do since the pass of the entry below. See *The Chain on SAS Viya* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide.
- **The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya, as they were after the second run.** On the test system on 2026-10-12 - SAS Viya 2026.09, with version 2.4 of the *LLM - Log Parser* step, version 2.2 of the *LLM - Get All Prompts* step, `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` and `Ingest-Documents.sas` as they were then - 46 SAS Job Execution jobs ran, six ingestions and 40 jobs of the chain, on tables of their own in `CASUSER`. (1) The ingestion job published its run history in three runs, left it alone with the history switched off and with a `rag_core` of before the change: see *The RAG ingestion job publishes the run history to CAS* under *Changed*. (2) The link found those runs. A log of the model containers of 24 hours was collected with the script of the guide - 1027 requests, every `API_KEY` value and every header value redacted - and parsed into 1027 rows; the pull wrote four rows of `RAG_RUNS` for the setup, all from its run table, with a key that was unique; and the link gave `ingestion run` to exactly four embedding rows: the three document embeddings of the first run and the one of the run with a changed document - the `chunks_written` of the two runs -, each with one candidate and the setup as its caller. The six embeddings in query mode of those minutes, one per run of the job, stayed `none`. With the run table dropped from memory the pull took one run from the ledger, the newest, and a link with `_lnk_redo_all = 1` left the four rows `none`. (3) A run that is refused ends its job as failed, and the first DATA step behind it does not run: see *A run of the log parser, of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` or of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` that is refused no longer leaves its job completed* under *Fixed*. (4) The numbers of the second run held: the collected log of 1040 requests gave 1040 rows and 36 columns, with no row that differed in any of the 26 compared columns from a parse with the parser of the second run; the pull wrote its five tables with 318, 27, 17, 107 and 421 rows; the link gave the same counts for every method; parser, pull, parser, pull, link and link in one job ran in one CAS session; and both steps were registered, updated with `PUT` under the id they had, and ran as the code SAS Studio generates for a flow. Not run in that pass: `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, which had no setting for the names of its tables. A check of the saved logs of the pass against its report carried every measurement and corrected four statements of the report: the number of pods the log was collected from is in no saved output; the log holds ten requests to the embedding model in the minutes of the runs, not nine; the compute context is reported by the service for the six ingestion jobs and was the argument of the request for the 40 others, and the tables of the ingestion runs were read back in a compute session and not in jobs; and the stored program of the job was compared with the file for the first ingestion run - for the five others the source lines of the job log are the program. What was changed after this pass was not run in SAS in it: the settings that are taken only where they are not set, versions 2.5 and 2.3 of the two steps, the counter of an `optimizationId` that passes over the id of another entry, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, the closing NOTE of `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` and the reason of a run history that could not be read. The settings, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` and the closing NOTE were measured afterwards in test content roots, see their entries; the two steps as steps, the counter and the reason are still not run in SAS. See *The Third Run* under *The Chain on SAS Viya* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide.
- **The pull, the link and the three programs in a row are tested in the repository.** Beside the tests of the log parser, `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Logging-Monitoring/tests` now runs the Python of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` against a made-up SAS Model Manager that pages its listings and can fail a request - trackers with header and model rows, a decision template with its expected answers, an optimization tracker, a `rag-setup.json`, a model registered by the step, manifested score code of both kinds, an empty system -, the Python of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` on made-up log rows and catalog rows with a stand-in for CAS - every rule and their order, ambiguity, the minimum of fixed text, a second run and a redo, the columns the program does not know -, and the three one after the other: log lines of all three kinds become rows, tracker, setup and score code files become the catalog, and the link program is given both. The tests fail when a code file and its step no longer hold the same program, when a column the *LLM Usage Report* reads changes its name or type, when the columns of a run table differ from the ones `rag_core` writes, and when the three programs no longer hand each other the same columns, kinds, roles, default table names or the same hash. 885 tests where there were 59: 394 of the parser, 12 of the collection script, 106 of the pull, 212 of the link, 11 of the chain, and 150 in three files that read all the programs at once - every text the Python hands to SAS goes through one function (119), every statement that writes an ERROR line leads to the statement that sets `SYSCC` (20), and every statement that starts or ends a CAS session stands under the check of a connected session (11). Among them: requests whose lines interleave at every position, a line that is one to thirty seconds older than the line in front of it, two experiment runs with the same prompts, the calls of a judge and of an optimization, a query embedding with and without a prompt behind it, a table name of 33 characters, the run table of a setup as its job writes it - read like the one the step writes, and with the document that was embedded inside one of its runs -, and a stand-in for the SAS object that fails a test of the link when the program hands SAS a message that would not arrive as one text. They run the Python; none of them runs SAS.
- **The release table says which part of the Prompt Builder a change concerns.** `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` has a new last column, `builder_part`, with the values `Prompt`, `Decision` and `RAG`. It is made for a button bar on the Changelog page of the report, with which a reader picks prompt templates, decision templates or RAG setups. A button bar shows the rows of one value, so an entry of the component `Prompt Builder` is now one row for each part it concerns - equal in every other column, in the order Prompt, Decision, RAG - and an entry that names no part concerns the Builder as a whole and is written for all three. A `RAG runtime` row carries `RAG`, once; the rows of every other component and the release intros have an empty value. Every other column means what it meant, and `release_rank` with `item_rank` still counts a change once; `component` itself changes for 14 entries of earlier releases (see *Changed*). The parts are read from the wording by keyword lists, as the component is, and the first of these decides: a section heading that names a part, for every entry under it; the steps of an upgrade and an entry that says it holds for every kind, which are written for the whole Builder; the parts the headline names - read whole, also where the column `summary` holds only the beginning of a long first sentence, and with a template named without its kind (*a prompt, a template or a RAG setup*) counting as both kinds of template; the whole Builder when the headline names the Prompt Builder and no part; else what the text names. By release, a part that did not exist yet cannot be concerned: no entry before 2.2.0 is filed under `Decision`, an entry that names no part is `Prompt` alone before 2.0.0 and `Prompt` and `RAG` from 2.0.0 to 2.1.x, and up to 2.2.x the name Prompt Builder names the prompt part. `mdb load-releases` prints the rows per part beside the number of changelog entries they stand for. The page needs the filter on `component` it has and a button bar on `builder_part`; the report with that page comes with the transfer package once the report is exported with it. The table was loaded into CAS on the test system on 2026-10-12, by `mdb load-releases` and by the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables`, each under a test name: 707 rows and eleven columns, `builder_part` empty in 341 rows, `Prompt` in 134, `Decision` in 54 and `RAG` in 178. What a Changelog page shows with it was read from the shipped package, not measured in SAS Visual Analytics. See *The release table* in the Model Definition Builder guide. Upgrading from 2.2.x: nothing by hand - the import of step 2 reloads the table.
- **The Administration Guide says how to recognise an execution that SAS Micro Analytic Service ended after 30 seconds, for a decision with a retrieval model, a prompt template and a decision template.** A decision with three model nodes that were all manifested in the Prompt Builder - the retrieval model of a RAG setup, a prompt template with the included LLM call that is given the retrieved context, and a decision template - returned, in its publishing validation on a SAS Micro Analytic Service destination, rows with **HTTP status 201, an empty error column and empty outputs of all three nodes**; the log of the validation job had no ERROR or WARNING line. That is no defect of the accelerator, and nothing in the decision or in a model has to be repaired for it: the service had ended those executions because they took longer than 30 seconds. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09. With the log level debug, the scheduling thread of the service logs `0x803fc006=tkWait()` exactly 30.0 seconds after *Scheduled work on worker thread*, followed by *Failure encountered during execution in revision … of module context … Return code: 0x8b3ff145* and *Micro Analytic Service failed to execute method*; a direct REST call of the step of the module answers HTTP status 201 with `"executionState": "timedOut"` and `"outputs": []`. The publishing validation sends the rows of its input table at the same time - three rows were scheduled within 12 milliseconds - and the LLM and the decision model of that decision were both self-hosted on one CPU node. In single calls the retrieval node took 0.2 seconds, the LLM node 1.3 to 4.2 and the decision model node 1.7 to 4.1, a whole call 6.5 to 9 seconds, and six single calls one after another all returned `retrieval_status` *ok*, three hits, an envelope of 1969 characters and the answer of the LLM from the context. With three rows at once the service logged 0.2 to 2.2 seconds for the retrieval node, 5 to 6 seconds for the LLM node of the first row and more than 29 for the two others, and 24 to 25 seconds for the decision model node while the LLM container was busy; with the retrieved context the prompt of the LLM is about 900 characters instead of about 50. The same module with the same table, validated twice without any change, returned all three rows empty the first time and one row complete - its three nodes took 29.65 seconds in total - and two empty the second time; three direct calls at once all answered `timedOut`, after 30.8, 31.7 and 36.5 seconds. The lengths of the model's variables have no part in it (see *Changed*). What a site can do follows from these durations and was not measured as a remedy: fewer calls at once, a faster model or models that do not share a node, a shorter prompt - a smaller `k` or smaller chunks. No setting for the 30 seconds is named, because none was found: the configuration definition `sas.microanalyticservice.system` of that system holds no timeout property, and no way to change the time was tried. *Several rows at once* on the *RAG Ingestion and Retrieval* page has the durations side by side, and the *Troubleshooting Guide* the section for the symptom.
- **`mdb credentials-add` and `mdb credentials-remove`: the credentials manifest is edited with a command, and a credential is removed through it.** `mdb credentials-add <type> <id>` - the type is `group` or `user`, the options are `--only`, `--source`, `--manifest`, `--env-file` and `--domain` - adds an identity to `credentials.yaml`. It takes the place of the placeholder a manifest of `mdb credentials-init` names; where there is no manifest, it writes the one `credentials-init` writes, with the identity in it; an identity that is listed is not written a second time, and with `--only` or `--source` its entry is updated and the change is printed; an `--only` name the source does not carry is refused with the message `mdb credentials-apply` gives for it, and nothing is written. An identity may carry `state: absent`: `mdb credentials-apply` plans the action `remove` for it, shows it in a dry run and deletes the credential in a run. A credential that is already gone is reported and is no error, such an identity needs no source and no entries, and a run that only removes does not create the domain. `mdb credentials-report` shows it as absent, and as `STILL THERE` while the credential exists. `state: present` is the default; any other value is refused when the manifest is read, with exit code 2. `mdb credentials-remove <type> <id>` writes `state: absent` on the identity, or adds the identity with it, and `credentials-add` on that identity sets it back to present. Neither of the two commands contacts SAS Viya, and nothing is removed because a line is missing from the manifest: the credentials service cannot list who holds a credential. Both commands change the lines of the one identity and leave every other byte of the file as it is - comments, blank lines, order, line ends. A comment on the `state:` line of an identity that is set back to present stays: the value is rewritten to `present` where a comment stands in what would be taken out. One comment goes: the two lines `mdb credentials-init` writes above its placeholder leave with the placeholder. The YAML library of the CLI drops comments when it writes a file, so the edit is made in the text at the positions its parser reports, and the result is read back with the loader and has to be the manifest that was there plus the one change before it is written. Refused, with the file left as it is: an `identities` list that is not written one line per identity (`identities: []` among them), an identity that is listed twice, an `only:` list that is to be rewritten and holds a comment, and any other layout whose result does not read back so (an alias, a merge key). `mdb credentials-apply` and `mdb credentials-report` refuse an identity that is listed twice as well, and a `--domain` that is not the domain of the manifest - with a manifest the run goes to its domain. `mdb credentials-apply` says once per run that plans or writes a group credential, and `mdb credentials-report` once where a group holds one: *A group credential reaches only the members of that group, and a user's own credential replaces it for that user.* Run: the test suite of the CLI, in which the credentials service is a stand-in in memory, and the two commands on files. Measured on 2026-10-14 in a credential domain and a group made for the pass, with the commands as they were before the fix-up of that day: `credentials-apply` created the domain and the credential of the group with its 3 entries, `credentials-remove` marked it absent without a request, the next apply deleted it (HTTP 204) without writing the domain, the report said `absent (as the manifest says)`, an apply once more said that there was none to remove - the DELETE was answered 404 - and ended with exit code 0, and a credential the manifest marked absent while it was there was `STILL THERE (manifest: absent)`. For a group that does not exist the service answered the write with HTTP 404 and errorCode 12310. Of what the fix-up of that day changed in these commands, the second pass of 2026-10-14 measured two: `credentials-report` printed the line about a group credential once, and `credentials-apply --domain agentic-ai-keys --dry-run` with a manifest of another domain ended with exit code 2 and its line and sent no request. The comment on the `state:` line and the refusal of an identity that is listed twice have not run against SAS Viya. See [Equipping many identities at once](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Managing-Credentials#equipping-many-identities-at-once).
- **A definition can take its provider key from the SCR container's environment: `mdb add --auth env`.** `provider.auth.mode` takes `env` beside `api_key` and `none`, with `auth.env_var` naming the variable (upper-case letters, digits and `_`); without it the provider's own name is used - `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `MISTRAL_API_KEY`, `VOYAGE_API_KEY`, `AWS_BEDROCK_API_KEY`, `AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY` for every azure adapter, `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` - and an adapter without one needs `--key-env NAME`. `key_name` is not used in that mode, and the manifest schema (`manifest.schema.json`) has the new mode and field. The `options.json` of such a definition has no `API_KEY`, and every score template that sends a key - OpenAI-compatible chat and embeddings, Anthropic, Gemini, Bedrock (bearer), Azure AI Foundry, Voyage and System One decision models - reads it through one partial, `partials/_env_api_key.py.j2`: a non-empty `API_KEY` option a caller passes still wins, otherwise the variable is read, and with neither the call fails before any request with `No API key: set the <VAR> environment variable on this container (or pass an API_KEY option).` Only the name of the variable is written into the file. A template that sends no key (self-hosted weights, Ollama and vLLM, Bedrock SigV4, `azure-foundry-env`) refuses the mode with one line. `mdb add --auth env --yes` works offline, for a decision model too (`mdb add openrouter typesafe/jev-1.13 --auth env --yes --offline`), and the description the fact sheet, `modelConfiguration.json` and the model card show ends with *The key comes from the container environment variable <VAR>.* - derived from `auth` at every `generate` and `sync`, not written into `definition.yaml`, so it follows a switch of the mode or the variable by hand; README and model card say it in their deployment notes as well. A manifest in this mode that declares an option named `API_KEY` is refused, because its default would take the place of the container's key in every call, and `mdb import` (and `mdb pull --import`) of a folder generated in this mode reads the variable from the score code and keeps the mode, where it made the definition `none` and regenerated a scorer that read an `API_KEY` no call held. `mdb test` lets the score code read the variable from the environment or `.env` and stops with one line where it is not set; `mdb validate --live`, `ship` and `radar` read the definition's variable first. The Prompt Builder, the optimization job and the SAS programs that call a model needed no change: a model whose `options.json` has no `API_KEY` is offered, run, judged, used in a council, optimized and manifested without a key and without the note about a missing credential-domain entry, and the verification of the page now writes such an `options.json` with mdb as well. A caller built while the definition still took a key keeps sending its `API_KEY` option, and a non-empty one wins over the container's key: the guide names the flows, the manifested models and the SAS code that `Create-LLM-Call.sas` and the custom steps *LLM - Create LLM Call* and *LLM - Get Options* generated - it sets `%let API_KEY=<entry name>;`, and the provider answers 401 - and says to rebuild them or take the option out; RAG ingestion asks the domain for a switched model until the maps of `rag_core/providers.py` are regenerated, which its refusal now says. Every existing definition is generated byte for byte as before.
- **`mdb deploy` gives an environment-keyed definition its key from a Kubernetes Secret, and takes the namespace and the TLS secret.** The Deployment of an `auth.mode: env` definition reads its variable with a `secretKeyRef` (`--key-secret`, default `llm-api-keys`, the Secret key named like the variable; in the Azure template it replaces the optional `azure-openai` reference), and the command prints the `kubectl create secret generic` line with `--from-literal=<VAR>="$<VAR>"` for every variable it rendered - names only, the shell fills in the values - and, for a Secret that exists, a `kubectl patch secret ... --type merge` line with `stringData` that adds or replaces those keys and keeps every other. The `--dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -` the first version suggested for that removes every key it applied before that a run does not list: after a `mdb deploy <model_id>` the keys of the other models. When `--tls-secret` names another Secret the command says that it has to exist in the namespace of the Ingress, and the guide shows how to copy the certificate of the SAS Viya ingress there; when it renders the PV variant into another namespace, it says that the claim and staging templates still name `llm`. The guide and the SCR deployment page say that the Ingress has no authentication, so a container that holds its key spends it for anyone who can reach its path, and how to restrict that. `--tls-secret` (default `llm-tls-certs`) sets the TLS secret of the Ingress and `--namespace` (default `llm`) the namespace of all three objects; the Ingress of the three deployment templates now names its namespace like the Deployment and the Service. A namespace or a Secret name Kubernetes does not take is refused with one line.
- **Six model definitions: GPT-6 Luna, GPT-6.1 Sol, Claude Haiku 5.5, Claude Sonnet 5.5, Claude Opus 5.5 and Mercury Decide.** `gpt_6_luna`, `gpt_61_sol`, `claude_haiku_5_5`, `claude_sonnet_5_5` and `claude_opus_5_5` are in `LLM-Definitions`, and the decision model `mercury_decide` (Inception, through OpenRouter) is in `Decision-Definitions`. Each has its fact-sheet row and takes its key per call under the key names of the rest of the fleet (`OpenAI`, `Anthropic`, `OpenRouter`). All six were generated by `mdb add ... --offline --yes` and called once through their generated score code on 2026-10-19, with at most 50 output tokens: every LLM answered at the default, the lowest and the highest reasoning effort - except Claude Opus 5.5 at the highest, whose thinking used all 50 tokens and left no text - and Mercury Decide routed the ticket of `mdb test` to billing with confidence 1.0. Prices per token, input / output: 1e-07 / 5e-07 (GPT-6 Luna), 2e-06 / 1e-05 (GPT-6.1 Sol), 1e-07 / 5e-07 (Claude Haiku 5.5, whose price above 100,000 prompt tokens is higher - the fact sheet holds the lower one), 2e-06 / 1e-05 (Claude Sonnet 5.5), 4e-06 / 2e-05 (Claude Opus 5.5) and 2e-08 / 0 (Mercury Decide). Mercury Decide has no measured *Evaluation* section yet.
- **An option can carry its own translation of the normalized values: `value_map` in `definition.yaml`.** `reasoning_effort` takes the same five levels for every model - `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `maximum` - and the option vocabulary translates them once per score template. A model that accepts other words now carries its own map on the option, and its score code sends that map's words instead. The keys must be exactly the five levels and the values non-empty texts; `mdb generate`, `validate` (V003) and `register` refuse a map with a missing or extra key, or a map on an option its template does not translate - a custom pass-through option and an informational option included, whose values no template translates. `mdb add` writes the map from the catalog's list of the effort words a model accepts - `minimal` goes to `minimal` where the model takes it, else to its lowest word, and `maximum` to its highest - and only where the map differs from the template's, so no existing definition changes. GPT-6 Luna sends `minimal` as `none` and `maximum` as `xhigh`; GPT-6.1 Sol, which has no `none`, sends `minimal` as `low` and `maximum` as `xhigh`. The manifest schema (`manifest.schema.json`) has the new field.
- **`reasoning_effort` for Claude models with adaptive thinking.** The `anthropic_messages` template sends the option as `output_config.effort`: `minimal` and `low` as `low`, `maximum` as `max`. Claude 5.5 takes `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max` and refuses `minimal` and `none` (measured on 2026-10-19). The option cannot be combined with `thinking_budget`, which stays for older Claude models; a definition that declares both is refused.
- **Catalog entries for GPT-6 Luna, GPT-6.1 Sol, Claude 5.5 (Haiku, Sonnet, Opus) and Mercury Decide.** The OpenAI entries are reasoning models and name the effort words each one accepts (GPT-6 Luna `none` to `xhigh`, GPT-6.1 Sol `low` to `xhigh`). Without them `mdb add openai gpt-6-luna` wrote `temperature`, `top_p` and `max_tokens`, which GPT-6 refuses - it takes `max_completion_tokens` - and the template's map sent `minimal`, which GPT-6 refuses as well; such a definition needed a hand-edited score file. Now it gets `reasoning_effort` with its own `value_map` and `max_completion_tokens`. The Claude 5.5 entries are marked for adaptive thinking and no sampling options (see *Changed*). Mercury Decide is marked as a decision model, so `mdb add openrouter inception/mercury-decide` builds a decision definition without `--kind decision`; its price and release date come from OpenRouter's endpoints API. A catalog entry can now give its own snapshot date, which the definition's provenance then shows (`static snapshot 2026-10-19`).
- **`mdb deploy --ingress contour`: model endpoints on a cluster whose ingress controller is Contour.** The Python SCR runtime answers only `POST /<model_id>`. The per-model Ingress of the templates rewrites `/llm/<model_id>/<model_id>` to that path with an annotation of the NGINX Ingress controller, and Contour has no rewrite on a plain Ingress, so on SAS Viya with Contour no model could be reached. With `--ingress contour` each `deploy-<model_id>.yaml` holds only the model's Deployment and Service, and the command also writes `llm-router.yaml` from the new template `SCR-LLM-Deployment-YAML/llm-router-contour-template.yaml`:
  - a ConfigMap with an NGINX server block: one `location = /llm/<model_id>/<model_id>` per rendered model, forwarding to `http://<service>.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local:<port>/<model_id>`, the cluster DNS as resolver (`resolver <dns> valid=30s ipv6=off`, so each Service is looked up per request: the router starts before the models and follows a Service that is created again), bodies up to 64 MB, 300-second timeouts and `/llm/healthz`;
  - a Deployment of `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged:1.27-alpine` (port 8080, readiness on `/llm/healthz`, requests 20m CPU and 32Mi, limit 128Mi) whose annotation lists the routed models, so a changed list restarts the router, and its Service (port 80 to 8080);
  - one Contour Ingress (`ingressClassName: contour`, path `/llm`, `Prefix`) for the host, with the TLS certificate the per-model Ingresses would serve and `projectcontour.io/response-timeout: 300s`. Contour merges an Ingress for the same host, so nothing of SAS Viya changes.

  The router routes exactly the models of one render: a render without `--all` warns that applying `llm-router.yaml` removes the routes of the other models. `--dns` sets the resolver (default `10.0.0.10`, the kube-dns of AKS). The last line of the command gives the `kubectl apply` line of the router and the health check. Without `--ingress contour` (or with `--ingress nginx`) the output is unchanged byte for byte: all 51 definitions the repository had before this release and seven environment-keyed site definitions rendered identically before and after. Not applied to a cluster by `mdb`: `kubectl diff` against a cluster that runs the hand-made equivalent found the router identical and, in the model Deployments, a label, `SAS_SCR_LOG_LEVEL_SCR_IO` (`TRACE` from the template, `INFO` on that cluster) and a readiness probe on `/internal/ready` that the hand-made Deployments have and the templates do not.
- **`mdb deploy` places and sizes the pods: `--node`, `--node-class`, `--requests`, `--limit-memory`, `--pull-policy`.** For both ingress kinds. `--node` adds a `nodeSelector` on `kubernetes.io/hostname` (with a note that the `app=llm` node affinity of the templates still applies unless `--node-class` is given); `--node-class CLASS` replaces that affinity and its tolerations with one toleration of the SAS node taint `workload.sas.com/class=CLASS:NoSchedule`, so the models can run on a node pool of SAS Viya; `--requests CPU,MEMORY` (instead of `1000m,4000Mi`), `--limit-memory` and `--pull-policy` (`Always`, `IfNotPresent`, `Never`) change only the model container. The router takes `--node` and `--node-class` and keeps its own requests and pull policy. A value the command cannot use - an ingress kind, a node name, a class, a quantity, a pull policy, a DNS address - ends it with exit code 2 before anything is written.
- **The Prompt Builder says on Finalize when the best prompt or the state of the Best Decision Model uses no variable.** Such a model is manifested without inputs (`def scoreModel():`, or with the provider key as its only input): it takes nothing from the data it scores, and SAS Intelligent Decisioning cannot use it - SAS Model Manager answers its Python wrapper with HTTP 412 *Python source code is not in the correct format*. A notice above **Manifest** (English and German) says so and what to do: define a variable under *Define Variables*, use it as `{{variable}}` in the prompt or the state, run again, tick that run and manifest it. The page looks at the run **Manifest** takes - the most recent one with a ticked **Best Response** or **Best Decision Model** -, at both prompts of a prompt template and at the state of a decision template; a variable that is defined but not used counts as none. Manifest stays usable, for a model that is used outside SAS Intelligent Decisioning. Prompt runs saved without a list of variables, by earlier releases, show no notice: their manifest takes its inputs from the user prompt - one per `name:value` item where it is written as `name:value;...`, and one `userPrompt` input for any other text or an empty prompt. Both manifests and the notice now read the variables by one rule, and the score code they generate is unchanged byte for byte.

### Changed

- **The RAG Builder is part of the Prompt Builder.** One bundle (`LLM-Prompt-Builder/dist/index.html`), one job definition (`SAS-LLM-Prompt-Builder-UI`), one report (`Prompt Builder`) and one Options pane serve all three kinds. The names and paths of the Prompt Builder are unchanged, so existing Data-Driven Content URLs, bookmarks and saved `builder-options.json` files keep working, and every element of both builders keeps its id. *RAG Builder* stays the name of the RAG part of the Prompt Builder; there is no report, object, package, job definition or build of that name any more.
- **One list of options for the Prompt Builder, and two compute contexts with two names.** The Options pane shows the shared options first (SAS Viya host, repository, LLM project, SCR endpoint, deployment type, judge model, model card report, credential domain), then each switch with its own options. The repository, the SCR endpoint, the deployment type and the credential domain are set once and used by every kind. `computeContext` keeps meaning the context of the optimization job; the RAG Builder's ingestion context is the option `ragComputeContext` (**RAG: ingestion compute context**), because the stock context the optimization job runs in is known not to work for an ingestion. A blank value in a URL is read as the default, and the `id` parameter is no longer read.
- **`mdb package-export` exports the content root and keeps what the manifest lists.** Every other object of the export is dropped with its entry in the requested items of the package and its mention in the connectors of what stays - a generated artifact, a report somebody parked in the folder, a dependency from outside it -, and so is every authorization rule and share. Of the files a report brings along, those stay that are its children and that its manifest entry lists (see *Changed*, the pictures of a report). In every copy of the Prompt Builder report the option defaults are reset to the neutral values - no project ids, RAG and optimization off, TLS verification on -, the per-user states are removed and the exporting host becomes `your-sas-viya-host`. The result is compared with the checkout before it is written, as `mdb package-check` does it, and the file from before stays byte for byte when a difference, a host or an object on a path that never ships is left. `--folder` is now `--content-root`, `--exclude` is gone, an export of another folder than the content root needs `--out`, and `--allow-differences` - with `--out` only - writes the export of a test folder that holds no report. Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 on the test content root. Without the two reports in the folder the command ended with exit code 1 and wrote no file, with one line per report that it is in the manifest but not in the package. With `--allow-differences` it wrote 71 objects and left nine out: the five objects of the folder that the manifest does not list, and the four folders above the content root, each as `(not below the content root)`. The export of that folder brought no rule. The file held nothing outside the manifest, no host, no rule and no share. It held the user id of the exporter 215 times - in `createdBy` and `modifiedBy` of the package, of every object and inside every body - and the path of the exported folder in `contentSourceLocation` of every object; they were left as the export wrote them, and since then the release file names no user (see *Fixed*). With copies of the two reports of the test system in the folder the export went through without `--allow-differences`: nine option defaults reset, the host replaced by the placeholder in four places, 77 objects, four of them the pictures of the usage report. After a file, a custom step and a job definition had been changed by hand it ended with exit code 1, named them and wrote nothing. In the second pass of 2026-10-12 the export of a test root without reports ended the same way - exit code 1 and four lines: the two reports, and the parameters of the two job definitions, whose `contentRoot` was the test root -, and with `--allow-differences` it wrote 71 objects and named the user id of the exporter in 298 places. The export package was deleted with HTTP 204, and the record of the export job stayed on the server; `mdb` deletes both since (see *Fixed*). Not measured: an export of the content folder itself, and whether such an export brings rules.
- **`mdb builders-import` hands only the reports to the transfer service and brings everything else in place.** The import of 2026-10-12 (see *Added*) showed two things about the SAS Viya transfer service: a file that is in the target folder under the same name with another id is not replaced - the file of the package is written into no folder, its task ends `completedWithWarnings`, and the import job still says `completed` -, and a package whose root folder is not at the root of SAS Content lands in a new folder of that name at the root. The command therefore hands the transfer service only the reports, the files that are children of a report and the folders they sit in. Every file, custom step and job definition of the package is written by the requests `mdb content-deploy` sends, with the package as the source and no checkout needed for the content: an object that is at its path is updated under the id it has on the site, whatever id the package carries, and a missing one is created. One line per entry says `equal`, `updated`, `created` or `FAILED`, for an object that was there with its id: `updated step RAG/steps/RAG - Embed Chunks.step (templates; id <its id> kept)`. Nothing of the exporting system - an id, a link, a user - is written to the site with it. New options: `--content-root`, the folder for everything but the reports; `--no-reports`, with which the transfer service is not called at all; `--compute-context`; and `--no-options-capture`. A report is imported only into the folder the package was exported from, and only when that folder is at the root of SAS Content, which is how every release so far was installed; otherwise the reports are not imported, the command says why, and it ends with exit code 1 unless `--no-reports` says that this is meant. No mapping to another folder is guessed. The reports are imported first, so that on a first install the transfer service creates the folders as it did before. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12 with `--content-root <a second test root> --no-reports --no-releases --no-files`, on a package exported from the first test root whose `tokens.py` had been changed by hand, so that the content of the package could be told from that of the checkout, into a root that held that file, a custom step and a job definition of the same names under other ids and with other content, and a file of its own. The dry run sent the sign-in and 10 GET requests, none to the transfer service, wrote nothing and said `70 entries: 3 equal, 3 updated, 64 created.` The run said the same, the three `updated` lines with the ids of the target root, and sent POST requests for the 64 new objects and one PUT each for the file content, the step and the job definition - no DELETE and no request to the transfer service. Afterwards the three objects had their ids and creation times; the file held the 76 bytes of the package, not the 1,020 of the checkout; the 37 files and 18 steps equalled the package; Start Here kept the `_contextName` the root had and got the root as `contentRoot`; the file of the root's own was untouched; nothing was created outside the root; and a second import said `70 entries: 70 equal.` with GET requests only. Two custom steps of one name in one folder, for the case of two objects at one path, could not be set up: SAS Viya refused the second registration with HTTP 409, errorCode 11552, `An item named "LLM - Get Options.step" of type "Custom step" already exists in the folder`. Not measured: the reports through the transfer service - neither pass imported a package that holds them -, and so what the service does with the folders of the reduced package on a site whose folders of the same paths have other ids, and with a report of the same name under another id - the tasks of the job are read, so a refusal ends the run with exit code 1 and its line.
- **`mdb content-deploy` writes in place and stops at the first write that fails.** As first built for this release the command replaced a file that differed by deleting it and uploading the new copy, and went on with the next entry after a failure. A review of that code against a service that is made up found what this costs: with uploads that are refused, every file that differed was gone from the folder, and with an upload that got no answer the line did not say that the file was deleted. On 2026-10-12 a file the command replaced had a new id afterwards (see *Added*). A file that differs now gets its content replaced - `PUT /files/files/{id}/content` under the ETag of the content that was compared - and stays the file it was; nothing is deleted, and the repeat of an upload that is answered 409 is gone with the delete. A write that is refused or gets no answer ends the run: the entry is `FAILED`, every entry behind it is listed as `not attempted`, and the line `Stopped at the first write that failed: ...` says what was written before it and that nothing was removed. Where the update of a file is refused, the file is left as it is. An entry that fails before anything is sent - two objects of its name in the folder, a reference of its name to an object of its kind elsewhere - alone, or beside the object itself -, an object that cannot be read - is reported and the run goes on; a folder that cannot be created stops the run like a failed write (as first built it did not, see *Fixed*). Either way the exit code is 1. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12: the update of the content of a file - one `PUT /files/files/{id}/content` with `If-Match`, answered 200 -, after which the file had its id, its creation time and the bytes of the checkout, and the run sent no DELETE. The stop at the first write that fails was measured on 2026-10-13, see *`release.json` is written last* under *Fixed*.
- **`mdb package-check` is strict for a release and lenient between two releases.** The package is exported from a system, so between two exports it holds the content of an earlier commit: every changelog entry, every fact sheet row and every fix to a program made it differ from the checkout, and the workflow and six tests of `mdb` would have failed for each of them until a maintainer exported again. `mdb package-check --release` is what the release procedure runs after the export and what the workflow runs for a version tag: every difference is an error. Without `--release` a difference in content against the checkout - a file, a custom step, a job definition, the release table, `release.json` - is a line that starts with `drift:` and does not fail. What a package must never be fails as before: an object the manifest does not list or lists once and the package holds twice, a rule, a share, a host, a per-user state, a report default that is not neutral, a missing entry, a path that never ships. With `--release` the option `--allow-missing` has no effect: a release cannot be tagged without the package. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says what a contributor sees. `release.json` names the commit the folder was deployed from, and for a release that commit has to be in the history of the checkout: a commit the clone holds on another line of history, or does not hold at all - a commit of a branch that was squashed and deleted -, fails `--release` (see *Fixed*). Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12 on two exports of a test root without reports: the lenient form printed the parameters of the two job definitions as `drift:` lines and the two missing reports as errors and ended with exit code 1; `--release` printed the same four lines without `drift:`, exit code 1; and after a file of the root had been changed by hand, the export of it was named `drift: ... file 'RAG/rag_core/tokens.py' differs from SAS-Viya-Integrations/RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (76 vs 1020 bytes)` in the lenient form and the same line without `drift:` with `--release`. The two forms sent no request to SAS Viya. The workflow was not run with the change.
- **The content manifest marks the settings of a site, and lists the pictures of a report.** A parameter of a job definition with `siteSetting: true` - `_contextName` and `contentRoot`, and of `Start Here` and the loader the CAS library and server, the credential domain, the names of the repository and the projects, the compute contexts to look for and the release table - holds a value of the site: a job definition a run creates gets the default, and so does one on the site that has no such parameter; a value a job definition on the site holds is left alone by a deploy and an import and is not counted as a difference. `--compute-context` writes a compute context into every job definition that has `_contextName`, and `contentRoot` is always written with the content root the content goes to, whether `--content-root` names it or not. As first built, every deploy set a compute context a site had chosen for the Prompt Builder job back to `SAS Studio compute context`, and a deploy into another content root left the page checking the default folder. A report entry lists the files that are children of the report under `files:`, with the SHA-256 of each: the four pictures of the *LLM Usage Report*, as the package of earlier releases and the export of 2026-10-12 hold them. `mdb package-export` keeps those and no other file a report brings along, and `mdb package-check` compares names and hashes. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12: a `_contextName` set by hand on Start Here survived `mdb content-deploy`, and the one a root had survived `mdb builders-import`; `--compute-context` wrote it; and `--content-root` wrote that folder into `contentRoot`. The import of that pass knew no compute context of options - it ran with `--no-reports` into a root without the report -, and the pictures were not compared on a real export.
- **`deploy-rag-content.ps1` / `.sh` call `mdb content-deploy --only RAG --to <the RAG content root>`.** They need the Model Definition Builder installed and sign in the way it does - a token, a password grant or the login of the SAS Viya CLI -, where they read the token of the CLI themselves before. The RAG content root they are given (`-ContentRoot` / `-r`) is the folder the runtime goes into, whatever its name: `--to` names the folder the entries below `RAG` of the manifest are deployed into. `-DryRun` / `-n` lists what would change. A site whose RAG content root is the folder `RAG` of the content folder needs neither script: the import brings the runtime there. Both scripts were checked for their syntax. Measured on 2026-10-13: `deploy-rag-content.ps1 -ContentRoot <test folder> -DryRun -Insecure`, with every stream redirected into a file and the command line saved beside it, wrote the warning of the connection that is not verified and `41 entries: 41 equal.` into that file, and the request log held GET requests only; the exit code of the script was not saved. Not run against SAS Viya: `deploy-rag-content.sh`, a run without `-DryRun`, and `-Create` / `-c`. The command the scripts call, `mdb content-deploy --only RAG --to <folder>`, created the 41 entries in a test folder on 2026-10-12, and with `--create` in one that was not there on 2026-10-13. The test that runs `deploy-rag-content.sh` is skipped where the `bash` on the PATH cannot read the path of the checkout - from PowerShell on Windows that is the launcher of the Windows Subsystem for Linux, and the test failed there.
- **Files reach SAS Content with LF line ends.** `mdb content-deploy` uploads a repository file with LF line ends whatever the checkout holds - a Windows checkout has CRLF where the repository has LF -, so that the same file has the same hash in `release.json` from every checkout. The scripts uploaded the bytes of the checkout. Measured on 2026-10-12: read back from the test content root, every repository file had the bytes of the repository with LF line ends.
- **A refused password grant is asked again.** A call of `mdb` ended with `401 Client Error` at the sign-in on the test system on 2026-10-11 and went through when it was repeated; the sign-in service refused password grants that overlapped that day. `mdb` now asks up to three times (`MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS` sets another number), 2 and 5 seconds apart - a grant that is answered `Bad credentials`, which is what a wrong password got on 2026-10-13, or `Unauthorized grant type`, is not asked again (see *Fixed*). `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS=1` switches the repeat off, and a token is not asked again. On 2026-10-12 a refused grant was not asked again and the command ended with a traceback: see *Fixed*.
- **`mdb setup` writes one seed file for the Prompt Builder.** `llm-prompt-builder.json` now also names the embedding project (`embeddingProjectID`) and holds exactly the five values an administrator copies into the Options pane - repository, LLM project, embedding project, SCR endpoint, deployment type; the keys `name`, `id`, `width`, `type` and `API_KEYS`, which no option corresponds to, are gone. `mdb builders-import` fills in the same five by itself, and a failing lookup of the embedding project no longer costs the LLM values.
- **The release table files RAG Builder changes under the Prompt Builder.** The `component` column of `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` no longer has the value `RAG Builder`: entries about it - past ones included - are `Prompt Builder` rows, which is what the changelog page of the report filters on - but for the one of 2.0.4 about the release table itself (see the next entry). `RAG runtime` stays a component of its own.
- **The release table files 14 entries of earlier releases under another component.** The value of `component` in `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` changes for them when the table is reloaded, under a report imported earlier as well. No entry of a release before 2.0.0 is filed under `RAG runtime` any more: `rag_core`, the RAG steps and the vector stores came with 2.0.0, and five older entries were filed there for a word such as *retrieves* or *enrichment*. Two of them are `Model Definition Builder` rows now (1.8.3 *Live catalogs no longer lose the kind*, 1.6.0 *Richer model attributes on register*), one is `Definitions` (1.1.0, the open-source CPU embedding definitions), one is `General` (0.1.15, `Get-All-Prompts.sas`) and one `Prompt Builder` (1.7.0 *The manifested best prompt inherits its winning LLM's attributes*). An entry about the best prompt is a `Prompt Builder` entry, which moves five more there from `Model Definition Builder` or `General`: 1.5.0 *Save vs. manifest link placement*, three of 1.0.0 (the tags a manifest writes, the toasts after saving and manifesting, *Save Experiments* and *Manifest Best Prompt* as real buttons) and 0.1.8 (the line in the language file that explains the best prompt). And an entry that says it holds for both builders is a `Prompt Builder` entry for every part its release had: the two fixes of 2.0.0 to the live run displays (*A carriage return would have made every progress milestone unreadable*, *Long progress messages were being read as their first 132 characters*) were `RAG runtime` rows and are `Prompt Builder` rows for `Prompt` and for `RAG`. An entry whose headline is about the release table - it names the release table, `ACCELERATOR_RELEASES` or `mdb load-releases` - is a `Model Definition Builder` entry: `mdb` makes the table, and such an entry names the Prompt Builder, the RAG Builder or the RAG runtime as values of the column, not as what changed. 2.0.4 *A release table for the Builder objects: `mdb load-releases`* was a `RAG Builder` row and is the one entry with that value that does not become a `Prompt Builder` row; the entries of this release about the table are filed the same way and have an empty `builder_part`. And an entry that the wording files under `Model Definition Builder` is filed under `Definitions` when its headline names the definitions and nothing of `mdb`, although its text says what `mdb` generated or renders: 2.2.0 *Shipped definitions*, and the 1.1.0 entry named above. Beside `component` and the new `builder_part`, no column of an existing row changes. Not run in SAS Viya: the table was built as CSV from this changelog and compared entry by entry with the one the rules built before, and the tests of `mdb` cover the rules. See *The release table* in the Model Definition Builder guide. Counted on the table as CSV - built from the changelog of 2.2.0 with the `mdb` of 2.2.0, and from this changelog with the `mdb` of this release: of the 453 entries of the releases up to 2.2.0, 61 were filed under `Prompt Builder` before and 98 are now - the 29 that were `RAG Builder` rows and eight of these 14, of releases up to 2.0.0 -, and they are 113 rows of the table where they were 61, an entry being one row for each part it concerns. A page filtered on `Prompt Builder` therefore lists more than those eight. Upgrading from 2.2.x: nothing by hand - the import of step 2 reloads the table.
- **`LLMCONTAINERSSLVERIFY` switches TLS verification on as well as off.** The score code of a prompt template with the included LLM call and of a decision template manifested in the Prompt Builder read the variable in one direction only: `false`, `no` or `0` switched verification off. `true`, `yes` or `1` now switch it on, also for a model that was manifested with verification off, so the variable wins in both directions on the destination it is set on. Unset, or with any other word, the model does what was written into it when it was manifested. A model manifested with the option ticked behaves as before for every value of the variable; `LLMCONTAINERCABUNDLE` and `LLMCONTAINERTIMEOUT` are unchanged.
- **`RAGEMBED_SSLVERIFY` and `RAGSTORE_SSLVERIFY` of the retrieval model switch verification on as well as off.** `retrieve_context.py` verifies its two HTTPS calls - to the embedding container and, in a compute session, to the credentials service - and read the two variables in one direction only. `false`, `no` or `0` still switch verification off; `true`, `yes` or `1` now switch it on, and unset or with any other word the new constant `SSL_VERIFY` of the MANIFEST block decides, which is `True` in the template and written per model when the model is manifested. The connection to the vector store is not affected: its TLS stays the matter of `sslmode`. The template reaches a site with the transfer package (step 2 of the upgrade), and a model has it once it is manifested again (step 8). A model manifested from a template deployed before this release has no such constant and verifies whatever the option of the report says: the manifest then says that verification is still on and that the template has to be deployed again, and the model is not tagged.
- **The retrieval model prefers the endpoint it was manifested with to `SAS_SERVICES_URL`.** `retrieve_context.py` embeds the question at the first of these that is not blank: `scr_endpoint` in the options of the call, the environment variable `RAGEMBED_ENDPOINT`, the manifested `EMBED_ENDPOINT`, and `SAS_SERVICES_URL` + `/llm`. In 2.2.x `SAS_SERVICES_URL` came before the manifested value, so wherever that variable is set - a compute session sets it - the question was embedded at `<SAS Viya host>/llm` whatever the model had been manifested for. Where the model containers are served somewhere else, that is another container than the one that embedded the documents, or none. Without `SAS_SERVICES_URL` nothing changes. The order is part of the template: a model has it once the template of this release is in the content folder (step 2 of the upgrade) and the model has been manifested again (step 8).
- **The log collection script redacts secrets and reads the model pods only.** With `SAS_SCR_LOG_LEVEL_SCR_IO=TRACE`, which the log parser needs, a container logs the options of every request - with the provider key of every model that is called with one, as `API_KEY:<key>` - and the request headers, with the `Cookie` header of every call made from the browser. The script in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide copied all of it into a file that SAS Studio users can open. It now pipes every line through `sed` before it is written and replaces the key and the values of the `Cookie`, `Authorization` and `X-CSRF-TOKEN` headers with `REDACTED`; the log parser reads a redacted log exactly like an unredacted one. It also selects the pods by the label the deployment templates give every model (`-l app`) instead of taking every pod in the namespace, where one unrelated service made up 97 % of the collected file on the test deployment. The guide gains a warning about the files collected so far, and says which lines the redaction expects: the three header expressions match a header at the start of a line, as `kubectl logs` prints it without `--timestamps` or `--prefix`. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11: one provider key and seven `Cookie` headers in the logs were `REDACTED` in the written file, and the number of lines stayed the same. The expressions for `Authorization` and `X-CSRF-TOKEN` were not exercised there, because no such header occurred.
- **Appending in the log parser writes the table again, wider, and keeps its other columns.** Appending called `table.append`, which takes rows into the columns the table has. It now writes the rows of the existing table and the rows of the log to one new table with a DATA step and puts that table in the place of the existing one, the way replace mode does: the 36 columns of the parser stand in front in their order, a column the existing table lacks is added and is missing in its rows, a column the parser does not know stays behind the others with its type and length and is missing in the new rows, and what the link wrote into existing rows stays. Text columns of the parser are VARCHAR; one that the existing table holds as CHAR is read under another name and handed to the VARCHAR column without its trailing blanks, so new rows are not cut to the old length. A column that is a number on one side and text on the other stops the DATA step, and the table is left as it is with the error `The new table could not be created, ...`. A table that was promoted is promoted again whatever the option of the run says; the saved copy is replaced when saving is on. The new table only takes the place of the existing one when the DATA step ended with a return code of at most 4 and wrote as many rows as the existing table and the log hold together - otherwise one ERROR line names the return code and the row counts, and the table stays as it is -, and it gets the label of the existing table. For this the program assigns the librefs `_llmtgt` and `_llmsrc` and clears them, keeps the column list of the table in `work._log_target_columns` for a moment, and reads the table through a libref - its name has to be one a libref can address (32 characters). Appending writes the whole table on every run, and other sessions do not see the table between the drop and the promote, as in replace mode. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11, with the program as it was that day, on a table of twelve columns and 174 rows that the parser had written before it had the further columns: appending the log of 1040 requests gave 1214 rows and 36 columns, promoted and saved again, the earlier rows with missing values in the new columns; into a copy with CHAR text columns no value was cut - the longest were those of a fresh parse -; a copy with two further columns kept them behind the 36; and a copy whose `temperature` was text gave `ERROR: Variable temperature has been defined as both character and numeric.` and `ERROR: The new table could not be created, ...`, and was unchanged. The DATA step ran in CAS. After the append the table had lost its label. The check of the return code and of the rows, and the label, were added after these runs and ran on 2026-10-12: the append gave 1214 rows and 36 columns again, with the label of the table, and a copy with a label of the site's own kept it; the copy whose `temperature` was text gave, behind the ERROR of the DATA step, the line with the return code 1012 and -1 rows written, was unchanged, and its job ended as failed.
- **`PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` gains twelve columns behind the 49 it had.** `modelID`, `projectID` and `projectName` (the ids were only inside `modelLink` and `projectLink`), `kind` (`prompt` or `decision`), `expected` (the expected answers of a decision run, JSON), `options` (the options of the run as stored, with the value of an option whose name says that it holds a key or a password replaced by `REDACTED`, by the names the log parser goes by), `variables` (the variables of the run, JSON), `resolved_system_prompt` and `resolved_user_prompt` (the prompts as they were sent), `resolved_system_hash` and `resolved_user_hash` (SHA-256 of those texts with every run of blanks as one, taken before a text is cut to the length of its column) and `judge_reasoning` (at most 2,000 bytes). The 49 columns keep their names, their order and their types. A long text column - the prompts, the response, the description - is now as wide as its longest value and at most 32,767 bytes, where it was 32,767 for every row; `description` holds up to 1,024 bytes, where it was cut at 256. `max_tokens` also takes the value of the option `max_length` when a run has no `max_tokens`. `unique_id` is still the row number and no key. The table was written on the test system on 2026-10-11 with its 61 columns and 318 rows; the 38 columns the *LLM Usage Report* reads are among them under their names, and the report was not opened on the table.
- **`Get-All-Prompts.sas` reads SAS Model Manager in PROC PYTHON.** The requests are made with the Python package `requests`, the token of the session and the CA bundle of the compute server, as the optimization job makes them; PROC HTTP and the JSON libname engine no longer read the service. The Python step hands each table to SAS as a JSON file with a map in the WORK folder. Of the WORK tables, `_gap_all_data` stays, and `_gap_all_optimizations`, `_gap_all_rag_setups`, `_gap_all_rag_runs` and `_gap_all_catalog` are new; `_gap_all_projects`, `_gap_all_models` and `_gap_all_experiments` are gone. Run on the test system on 2026-10-11: the JSON libname engine read the files and their maps as they are written.
- **The *LLM - Get All Prompts* step (version 2.2) writes the five tables.** Its output table names the table of the experiments and the CAS library, as before; the section *Further output tables* has a field for each of the four other tables, and *Where the models are found* the two tags and the three projects. A table or tag field that is left empty takes the default, a project field that is left empty leaves that kind out of the catalog. The step starts a CAS session only when the session the option `SESSREF` names is not connected, and ends the one it started. Version 2.0 of the step ran on the test system on 2026-10-11, as the code SAS Studio generated for a flow, and wrote the five tables with the content the program wrote. Version 2.1 - the table names it refuses, the further source of `RAG_RUNS`, the further columns and roles of the catalog - ran there on 2026-10-12 the same way and wrote the five tables of the program of that day, with the same three WARNINGs. What version 2.2 adds - one ERROR line without an ABORT for an output library that is no CAS library, the condition code 1012 for a step that wrote an ERROR line and left an output table unwritten, the function its messages go through, the counter for an id that a tracker holds twice - ran there later that day (see *The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya* under *Added*). Version 2.3 of the step - the counter that passes over the id of another entry, see *Fixed* - is not run in SAS.
- **German: the prompt part of the Prompt Builder says "manifestieren" too.** Eleven German texts of the prompt template part said *festschreiben* for what the decision and RAG parts already called *manifestieren*: the button (*Besten Prompt manifestieren*), the heading and the description of the manifest card, the label and the info text of the included LLM call, the label of the default outputs, the toast (*Der beste Prompt wurde manifestiert.*), the message for a manifest without an output, the two *Nicht manifestiert:* messages for a saved run with a refused variable or question name, and the explanation under the prompts on *Build & Test*. No key and no English text changed.
- **The Prompt Builder and `RAG - Register Setup` register the retrieval model of a RAG setup with the same lengths, every variable with one.** The RAG Builder wrote the two context outputs of the retrieval model, `context_dg` and `context_envelope`, without a length, for which SAS Model Manager stored 99 and SAS Intelligent Decisioning declared `in_out varchar(100)` in the code it generates for a decision; *RAG - Register Setup* registered the two outputs with 32000 and its inputs shorter than the Builder's (4096, 1024, 16 and 2048). Both now write `question` 32000, `k` 8, `filter_json` and `options` 4000, `retrieval_mode` 32, `context_dg` and `context_envelope` 32000, `retrieval_status` 512 and `run_time` 8 - the Builder with every save and every manifest, as before - and a test compares the two lists entry by entry, so that a model is the same model whichever of the two wrote its variables last. The lengths describe the model and repair nothing. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, as a control: with both context outputs set back to 99 on the model and the decision published again, single calls in SAS Micro Analytic Service returned HTTP status 201, `retrieval_status` *ok* and `context_envelope` complete with its 1969 characters, and the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning had returned the longer values complete with the length 99 as well - a value longer than its declared length neither failed the execution nor was it cut. The empty outputs that a publishing validation returned for such a decision were executions the service had ended after 30 seconds (see *Added*); the validation that returned every row after the lengths had been raised to 32000 by hand was a run that finished within that time. Measured the same day with the Prompt Builder page of this release, the bundle that ships: **Manifest setup** wrote the nine lengths, 32000 for both context outputs. On the same page, **Launch ingestion** of a setup on the runtime of this release ended as completed, with run ids of the form `run-<epoch>-<ten digits>` and the run history recorded, and three runs of one setup showed 6 documents new, then 6 changed, then 6 unchanged and 1 new. Measured earlier that day with the page as it was before its German texts were reworded (the entry above): the same nine lengths, and the code SAS Intelligent Decisioning then generated for a decision declared `varchar(32000)` for both context outputs. A model that *RAG - Register Setup* registered held the same nine lengths in SAS Model Manager. Not measured: a model that *RAG - Register Setup* registered with these lengths inside a decision, outputs longer than 32000 characters, SingleStore, and a value longer than its declared length for a model of another kind. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2 brings the page and step 8 manifests each RAG setup again, which is what writes the lengths; nothing else has to be done for them.
