The Administration Guide has the table under *The lengths of the variables* on the *RAG Ingestion and Retrieval* page.
- **A failed run of the RAG ingestion job ends as a failed job.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` never raises a SAS error, so it ended normally whatever happened and SAS Job Execution reported every run as completed; the outcome was in the log only. The program now sets `SYSCC` as its last statement when the run failed - the statement a job that *RAG - Register Setup* generates from a flow ends with, which SAS Job Execution reported as `failed` when that job was measured (2026-10-11, SAS Viya 2026.09). A schedule or a monitor that reads the state of the job now sees a failed ingestion as one. The program still runs to its last statement and nothing aborts. Every run that failed ends this way, whatever the reason: a saved ledger that cannot be loaded, the lock of another run, a store that cannot be reached. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 for `Ingest-Documents.sas`, run in SAS Job Execution in the *SAS Studio compute context*, with pgvector: the nine runs that failed - three at a configuration that had drifted from the ledger, five at a fresh lock that was not theirs (two at the lock of a flow that was running, three at a lock row appended by hand, one of them a row of the earlier kind without a run id), one at a `rag_core` folder that does not exist - ended as `failed` after 16.5 to 20 seconds, and the eight runs that went through ended as `completed`. The record of a failed job holds the error code 5713 of SAS Job Execution, *A procedure in the SAS program provided for the job completed execution with at least one error.*, and inside it an error with the code 1012 and the text of the program's ERROR line. That line is the last message of the program and not the last line of the log: the timing lines of its DATA step and the echoed source of the last statement follow it. Not run: a saved ledger that cannot be loaded (see the entry about a saved ledger under *Fixed*) and a store that cannot be reached. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **The RAG ingestion job publishes the run history to CAS.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` - the ingestion a setup is started with from its page, and the ingestion job of a setup - writes `<prefix>_RUNS` and `<prefix>_DOC_EVENTS` beside the ledger, promoted and saved, as *RAG - Load Vector Store* does: every run the vector store holds for the collection (the newest 500) and their document events (the newest 5,000), with the columns, types and labels of the step. `<prefix>` is the ledger table without its ending `_LEDGER`. The job recorded its runs in the vector store only: on the test system on 2026-10-11 and on 2026-10-12 a setup that had been ingested by the job had its `<prefix>_LEDGER` in memory and no `<prefix>_RUNS`, so the option text *publishes the first two to CAS for reporting* did not hold for it, and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` had only the newest run of such a setup, from its ledger. A run that finds nothing to do publishes too, so one run of an existing setup brings the runs the store holds into CAS. A history that cannot be read or published is a WARNING in the job log and not a failed run; with the history switched off the job touches neither table, and it leaves a table the store holds no rows for as it is, where the step drops the table and deletes its saved file. `rag_core/history.py` has the rows as the step stages them for a caller without SWAT (`cas_types`, `cas_values`, `write_cas_file`). `Get-All-Prompts.sas` reads the table of the job as it reads one of the step, with `run_source` = `run table`, and its ledger only where the run table is not in memory, so a run that both name is one row of `RAG_RUNS`. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12 with six runs of the job, in a content folder, a table prefix and a collection of their own, each run a SAS Job Execution job that ended as completed. A first run of three documents published `<prefix>_RUNS` with one row and 35 columns and `<prefix>_DOC_EVENTS` with four rows and ten columns, promoted and saved; set against a run table the step had published, no column differed in name, type or label, and two differed in length. A run that found nothing to do made it two rows and five events, and a run with one changed document three and seven. With the history switched off the log had no line about it and both tables and their files stayed as they were. After both tables were dropped and their files deleted, one run published four rows - every run the store held; the run with the history switched off is not among them. With the `rag_core` of before this change the job wrote the milestone *rag history: not read for CAS (ImportError: …) - the ingestion itself is unaffected* and the WARNING, and left both tables. The pull then read the runs from the run table, and the link found the document embeddings of the runs (see *The three programs and the ingestion job ran once more on SAS Viya* under *Added*). Changed after those runs and not run in SAS: how the reason of a history that could not be read reaches SAS (see *Fixed*). The tests run the Python of the job against a stand-in for the store, compare the file it writes with what the step stages and hold the SAS statements of the upload as text, and they follow the two macros of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` that stack the run tables with a stand-in. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 8.
- **`Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` replaces a table only with a sheet that was read.** Each sheet is read and loaded beside its table first; a file that is not in the folder, a file without rows and a sheet CAS did not hold completely leave the table as it is, with a WARNING, and the other sheets are loaded. The upload whose rows were counted then takes the place of the table - the table is dropped, the upload promoted under its name and saved, as `Load-Accelerator-Tables` does it for the release table -, and a refused promote loads the table again from the file it was saved to before. The swap ran on 2026-10-13, from a copy of the program with test table names in `casuser`: 30, 16 and 5 rows, each table global and saved, one load per sheet, no WARNING, and the same after a second run; a drop or a promote that is refused did not occur. As first built the table was dropped and the sheet loaded a second time with the statements the program had, so a failure of CAS in that second load could cost the table. The program switches the option `SYNTAXCHECK` off while it runs, so that an error for one sheet stops no sheet behind it in a batch session. Run on 2026-10-12, with the program of that day, through the job `Load-Accelerator-Tables`, with the three table names replaced by test names - fifteen lines of the program - and nothing else changed: the three sheets were loaded with 30, 16 and 5 rows, promoted and saved, and with one file missing from the folder that sheet gave its WARNING and the two others were loaded. The program with its own table names was not run: it replaces the fact sheets of a system.
- **The steps *LLM - Log Parser* and *LLM - Get All Prompts* were built from their programs again on 2026-10-12, as version 2.5 and 2.3.** No control changed. Version 2.5 of the parser differs from 2.4 in the text of its *About* tab and in one comment; version 2.3 of the pull has the counter that passes over the id of another entry (see *Fixed*). The settings that are taken only where they are not set are in the two code files and not in the steps, which get every setting from a control. Both versions were registered in a test content root on 2026-10-12 by `mdb content-deploy`, and what the service held equalled the two files in every part that is compared; neither version has run as a step. The versions this release ships are named in *A PROC CAS step that halted is no answer* under *Fixed*.
- **Start Here: a session that is refused everything gets one line.** Where SAS Viya answers every request of the session with 401 or 403, the page has one line at its top in place of those lines: that every request was refused, and that the page is opened with `&_contextName=<a compute context that runs as you>` or with that context in the parameter of the job definition. It says that the session runs under a service account, and not as you, where the user of the SAS session (`SYSUSERID`) is another one than the user SAS Job Execution started the session for (`SYS_COMPUTE_SESSION_OWNER`); the two are compared and neither is written to the page or the log. In such a session that only some services refuse, each refused line advises to open the page as yourself. Not run in SAS: no request was refused in the second pass of 2026-10-12 or in the two passes of 2026-10-13, so the line did not appear. A probe in the `SAS Job Execution compute context` printed that `SYSUSERID` was not `SYS_COMPUTE_SESSION_OWNER` there, and in the `SAS Studio compute context` that the two were equal.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables says when its session is another user's.** One NOTE: the SAS session does not run as the user who started the job, a file that is reported as not found may be one that account may not read, and the parameter `_contextName` names a compute context that runs as you. The state of the job is decided as before. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12: in the `SAS Job Execution compute context` the NOTE and its second line were in the log, and the job then found the file and loaded the table; in the `SAS Studio compute context` the NOTE did not appear.
- **Custom step *LLM - Create LLM Call* 1.1.** With *Save the code to a filename?* ticked and no path - the values the step comes with - the code is written to a temporary file and the fileref `_slo_LLM` stays assigned, as the label of the path says; the program of version 1.0 held an ERROR line and an ABORT statement for that. With a path nothing changes. Measured on 2026-10-12 with the step registered in a test root, its code run with the values SAS Studio sets for manual entry and the options file of gpt_41_mini: the NOTE about the temporary file, the fileref `_slo_LLM` assigned, 36 lines of code and `SYSCC` 0; with a path the file was written and the fileref cleared; with the box not ticked no file and no WARNING. With the options file of gpt_5_mini, which holds a `values` list, PROC TRANSPOSE failed as expected; version 1.2 reads the options another way (see *Fixed*).
- **The collection script writes one line in front of every container's lines, and the parser ends a part there (*LLM - Log Parser* 2.7).** The script of the Logging & Monitoring guide reads each container of each model pod on its own and writes `#=# llm-log-collection pod=<pod> container=<container> collected=<UTC time> #=#` in front of its lines, inside the redaction pipe; `kubectl` stays read-only. A model's response is logged line by line, so a container can write a line of that shape. The script therefore puts a backslash in front of every container line that starts like its own line (at the start of a line and behind a carriage return, with or without backslashes in front) and ends the last line of a container; in a file the script wrote, a line of that shape is a line of the script. From the first such line on, a part ends at each of them and nowhere else, whatever time of collection it carries: a request running there is cut off (`failed` missing, counted in a NOTE), nothing read before goes on in the next part, and the time of the lines decides nothing. Behind the first line of the script the parser takes the backslash off again, so a response holds the line as the model wrote it. What stands in front of that first line, and a file without one, is read by the time of its lines. On 2026-10-13 the script as it was that day - without `escape_llm_log` - read nine containers of the test system and wrote nine lines of its own with one time of collection, and `Log-Parser-Code.sas` of version 2.6 read that file and a copy without those lines into the same 1,024 rows, with no difference in any of the 36 columns. The script with `escape_llm_log` and version 2.7 ran in the tests only - the script with a `kubectl` of the test that answers from files and GNU `sed` 4.9 of Git for Windows -, not in a live pass and not in SAS. Upgrade step 12 (the collection script) applies.
- **Start Here checks the tables of the logging chain and an empty project.** `DECISION_FACT_SHEET` and the four tables `Get-All-Prompts.sas` writes beside `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` (`PROMPT_OPTIMIZATIONS`, `RAG_SETUPS`, `RAG_RUNS`, `MODEL_CATALOG`) are a section of their own. A definition project without a model is `missing`. In a session of another user the page links itself in each compute context it found - or, where the contexts could not be listed, in each one the parameter `computeContexts` names -, but the one it ran in. A page that is refused everything now has 20 lines that are not checked; the page measured on 2026-10-12 had 15, and the difference is the five new tables. Measured on 2026-10-13 for a test root: the section of the tables of the logging chain with five rows, one of them ok, and in a session of another user one link, to the other compute context. Not run in SAS: a project without a model as `missing`, and a page that is refused everything.
- **The log parser appends only where it is told to, stops where it would replace rows unasked, and appends a request once (*LLM - Log Parser* 2.8).** `_log_append_table` of `Log-Parser-Code.sas` has no default any more, and three states. `1` appends and `0` replaces, as before. Without a value the program only starts a table: where there is none, or one without rows, it writes the requests of the log; at a table that holds rows - in memory, or only as its saved file - it stops in front of the log with `ERROR: The table <caslib>.<table> holds <n> rows and _log_append_table is not set, so nothing was read and nothing was written - not the table and not its saved file. Set it in front of the program: %let _log_append_table = 1; adds the requests of the log to the table, %let _log_append_table = 0; replaces the table with them.` and ends with the condition code 1012. Any other value ends with one ERROR line that names the values of the setting. Up to now the program replaced without the setting - the file of release 2.2 held `%let _log_append_table = 0;` -, and since the settings moved out of the file, a program of a site that had lost its `1` replaced the rows of every run before it: measured on the test system on 2026-10-13 on a copy of a table of 3,630 rows, which held the 1,024 requests of one log afterwards, in memory and in its saved file, with a completed job and no line that said so. A run with `0` that replaces a table that held rows now says so, `NOTE: The table <caslib>.<table> held <n> row(s). They were replaced by the <m> request(s) of the log, as _log_append_table is 0.` The program deletes its settings at its end, as before, so a second include in one SAS session without the `%let` line stops the same way. A request is appended once: a run with `1` leaves out a request a row of the table holds already, and keeps one row of a request the log itself holds more than once - what a request is known by, and what version 2.9 does beyond that, is the next entry. Before, a second append of the same file left every request in the table twice (measured on 2026-10-13: 5,678 rows where the table had 4,654). A request without a `transaction_id` is appended every time, and so are the requests of a log whose rows a table of release 2.2 holds already, as those rows have no ID. One line names the numbers of the run; the next entry has it as the program writes it. The custom step sets `1` or `0` from its checkbox *Append new rows to existing table*, which comes unchecked as before: its two choices do what they did, with the two NOTEs and without a request twice, and its Options and About tabs say what each choice does. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-13 with version 2.8 of the step, in SAS Job Execution jobs, on copies in `CASUSER` of a table of release 2.2 - 3,630 rows, twelve columns - and with a collected log of 1,024 requests: without the setting the ERROR line, the copy and its saved file as before and a failed job; with `1` 4,654 rows and 36 columns, the 3,630 earlier rows with the values of their twelve columns; the same log once more 0 appended and 1,024 left out; a file that held every request twice 1,024 appended; with `0` the NOTE and the 1,024 rows; a table that was only its saved file the same in each of the three states; a table without rows replaced without the ERROR line; `2`, `yes`, `01` and `TRUE` refused; the program of the step with both choices of its checkbox; and a table the file of release 2.2.0 had written from that log, to which the same log added its 1,024 requests a second time. The guide has each of them: [Appending](https://sassoftware.github.io/sas-agentic-ai-accelerator/Administration-Guide/Logging-&-Monitoring#appending). Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 15 - a job or a schedule that ran `Log-Parser-Code.sas` writes that ERROR line and nothing else until its program sets `_log_append_table`.
- **The log parser writes a request once in every state of its setting, lets a row that was cut off give way to the whole one, and writes nothing where it has nothing to add or can not load the saved table (*LLM - Log Parser* 2.9).** What the pass of 2026-10-13 left open with version 2.8, and one thing the program contradicted itself in. **A request is known by three columns** - its `transaction_id`, its `model` and its `timestamp`, the time of its request line - and no longer by the transaction ID alone: the parser reads a second `Score request received` line of an ID as another request, since a client can give its requests an ID of its own, and the guard of 2.8 took such requests for one. A table that lacks one of the three columns is compared with nothing, as a table of release 2.2 is. **A request the log holds more than once** - a file that was collected twice - gets one row with `1`, with `0` and without the setting: the first row that is complete, and the first of all where none is. With `0`, 2.8 wrote 2,048 rows from a file that held 1,024 requests twice (measured on 2026-10-13). A run that replaces or starts a table says `NOTE: <r> request(s) were read from the log. <m> were written to the table <caslib>.<table> and <l> were left out, as another row of the log holds their request.` **A row that holds neither a response nor an error gives way.** A row is incomplete when its `failed` is missing, as for a request that was still running where the lines of its container end. With `1`, a complete row of the log takes the place of the rows of the table of its request when each of them is incomplete; in 2.8 the row of a request that was cut off kept its missing values when a later file held it whole (measured). A complete row of the table is never replaced, and no row of a table without a numeric column `failed`. The row that takes the place is the row of the log: what the link wrote into the row that gives way goes with it. The line of an append names these rows: `NOTE: <r> request(s) were read from the log. <a> were appended to the table <caslib>.<table> and <p> took the place of <x> row(s) of it whose failed was missing: the table held <o> row(s) and holds <n> now. <s> were left out, as another row holds their request: <t> a row of the table, <l> a row of the log.` - `r` is `a` + `p` + `s`, and `n` is `o` + `a` + `p` - `x`. **A run that appends nothing writes nothing**: where every request of the log is in the table already, no table is built and none is saved - `NOTE: <r> request(s) were read from the log and none of them was written: the table <caslib>.<table> keeps its rows, and neither the table nor its saved file was written. All were left out, as another row holds their request: <t> a row of the table, <l> a row of the log.` 2.8 wrote and saved the table again (measured: a new creation time of the table and a new time of its file). A table that is only its saved file is still loaded for such a run, and promoted where the run promotes. **A saved table that can not be loaded is not replaced.** A run with `0` counts the rows of the table before it writes; where the saved file of a table that is not in memory can not be loaded it ends with `ERROR: The saved table <caslib>.<table> could not be loaded, nothing was replaced and the saved file was left as it is.` and the condition code 1012, and writes neither a table nor the file. 2.8 ended with the ERROR lines of the action and `SYSCC` 1012 and had replaced the table and its file all the same - measured on 2026-10-13 with a copy of the program whose count asked for a file that was not there. **A request without a `transaction_id`** is appended every time it is parsed, as before, and no rule of the program can change that: in a file without the lines of the collection script, such a request and another request with the same lines are the same file line for line, and onto a table that holds the row the first has to add nothing and the second a row. In a file of the collection script the line of the script names the pod and the container, but the table keeps neither of a row: the row of such a request of one container and the row of the same request of another container are the same row. A column of `LLM_LOGS` for the pod and the container would tell them apart; the table has none. And a request read again and another request of the same container, written in the same second with the same lines, are the same file line for line with the line of the script as well: no column of the table can tell those two apart. **A table whose rows can not be counted is not started over.** A run without the setting counts the rows of a table in memory with `table.tableInfo`. Where `table.tableExists` found the table and that listing named none, the count stayed 0, and the run replaced the table and its saved file without an ERROR line (found in the review of 2026-10-14, in the made-up CAS library of the tests; whether CAS answers that way was not measured). Such a run now stops in front of the log with `ERROR: The rows of the table <caslib>.<table> could not be counted and _log_append_table is not set, so nothing was read and nothing was written - not the table and not its saved file. Set it in front of the program: ...` and the condition code 1012. A run with `0` replaces such a table as before, without the NOTE of the rows it held. **An append that can not build its table drops the table it loaded for it.** Where the table was only its saved file, the run had loaded it, and it stayed loaded as a table of the session of the run behind `ERROR: The new table could not be created, ...`. It is dropped again now, so only the saved file is left, as before the run. A table that was in memory before the run is not dropped. The step is version 2.9; its Options and About tabs and the README of the folder say the same. Measured on 2026-10-14 with the code file of 2.9 and the program of its step in SAS Job Execution jobs, on copies in `casuser` of a table of release 2.2 (3,630 rows) and a collected log of 1,024 requests. Without the setting, with `1` and with `0` each run answered as described here - at no table, at a table with rows, at a table that was only its saved file and at a table without rows -, the 3,630 earlier rows kept their values by a checksum, and `2`, `yes`, `01` and `TRUE` were refused. The same log appended a second time gave `none of them was written` and left the table and its file as they were - the creation time of the table, the size and the time of the file; where the table was only its saved file it was loaded, and the file kept its time. A file that held every request twice was written once in every state: 1,024 rows, or 1,024 appended to the 3,630. A request cut off in one file and whole in the next took the place of its row (`1 took the place of 1 row(s)`), with status 200 and `failed` 0. Three requests without an ID were appended again (6 rows, then 9), and three requests with one transaction ID gave 3 rows, which a second run with `1` did not write again. A saved file that can not be loaded could be staged only with two changed copies of the program - CAS refused a CSV under a `.sashdat` name and an encrypted file -; each gave its ERROR line and `SYSCC` 1012 and left the file as it was. In the appends that were looked at, each DATA step on a CAS table ran in Cloud Analytic Services and none wrote a WARNING; no `_TEMP_LLM_LOGS` table was left, and the link behind them linked 4,654 of 4,654 rows. Of the two changes of a later commit of this release, the drop after an append that can not build its table ran in the second pass of 2026-10-14, with the code file of that commit: at a table that was only its saved file, whose `temperature` was text, the append ended with `ERROR: The new table could not be created, ...` - the return code 1012, -1 rows written, 2 rows of the table and 1,024 of the log, 0 rows taken out -, `SYSCC` 1012, no table in memory afterwards, and the file with the size and the time it had. At a valid table that was only its saved file the append loaded it, wrote 1,026 rows, promoted and saved them. The three states gave the answers above once more on copies of the table of release 2.2. The table whose rows can not be counted has not run in SAS: it is followed in the tests of the repository against a CAS library that is made up (`log_parser_table_harness.py`). 49 of the 66 cases of `test_log_parser_output_table.py` for the code file fail on the program of 2.8.
- **Decision runs in the Prompt Builder: the box Manifest takes is called by what it records.** The box on the result of a decision run read *Correct - matches the expected answers*. It marked the run **Manifest** takes; the page ticked it where every expected answer matched, and a person could tick it on a run whose answers did not match, where the label was then untrue. It now reads *Best Decision Model* (German *Bestes Entscheidungsmodell*): it records the choice of the best decision model of a run, and a run has one. Two decision models whose answers all matched got two ticked boxes, of which **Manifest** took the first without saying so. The page now ticks the first result of a run whose answers all matched the expected ones, a tick on another result of the run unticks the one ticked before, and a run saved with more than one ticked box shows the first - the one **Manifest** took - and is written with that one alone at the next **Save Experiments**. **Manifest** takes the Best Decision Model of the most recent run that has one. Every result whose answers all matched carries the note *All expected answers matched* (*Alle erwarteten Antworten getroffen*), ticked or not; the legend of the tracker reads *Best Decision Model - one per run, ticked automatically on the first result whose expected answers all matched, yours to change* (*Bestes Entscheidungsmodell - eines je Lauf, automatisch gesetzt beim ersten Ergebnis, bei dem alle erwarteten Antworten getroffen wurden, änderbar*). What the box stores (`best_prompt`) is unchanged. Every text of the manifest of a decision template names the box the same way, in English and German: the legend, the title of the icon of a ticked model, the instructions of the tracker and its count (*Runs: n · with a Best Decision Model: m*), the heading **Manifest the Best Decision Model**, the description of **Manifest** on the *Finalize* step - it said that it takes the most recent run *with a correct response*, and it now says that it takes the most recent run ticked *Best Decision Model* (German: *bei dem „Bestes Entscheidungsmodell“ angehakt ist*) -, the button **Manifest Best Decision Model**, its hint while it is off and the line of a manifest without a ticked box (*You have to select a Best Decision Model using the checkboxes.*), and the message of a manifest that is done (*The Best Decision Model has been manifested.*). A prompt template keeps *Best Response*, **Manifest Best Prompt** and its other texts; the *Best Response* box of an LLM run is a choice of the person alone - the page ticks it nowhere. The German reading of the answer of a decision model says *Top-Antwort*, as the English says *Top answer*; it said *Beste Antwort*, the German name of the box of an LLM run. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-decision.js` in English and German - with one Best Decision Model per run: a stored run with two ticked boxes, a run of two models that both match, a tick by hand and the score code of the manifest -, `verify-outcomes.js`; `verify-locales.js` checks the two labels, that each text of the decision side that speaks of an experiment run names the box by its label, and that no text of the decision side speaks of a best prompt or a best response); not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb add` writes no sampling options and `max_tokens` 4000 for Claude 5.5.** Claude 5.5 refuses `temperature` other than 1, `top_p` and `top_k` at any value (even `top_p` 1), and `thinking {type: enabled}`; it thinks adaptively and is steered by `output_config.effort`. A definition added for such a model declares `reasoning_effort` (default `medium`) and `max_tokens` (default 4000, at most 128,000), and no sampling option; a `temperature` a caller passes anyway is not sent. The thinking counts against `max_tokens`: with 1000, a model that thinks long can end before its answer is written. `medium` is the API's own default for Haiku 5.5 and Opus 5.5; for Sonnet 5.5 the API default is `high`, so the definition sends less effort than an unset request. Older Claude models keep `temperature`, `top_p` and `thinking_budget`.
- **The Anthropic live catalog reads the context window, the output limit and the thinking capabilities of each model.** `mdb add anthropic` gave every listed model a 200,000-token context and a thinking budget. It now takes `max_input_tokens`, `max_tokens` and the `thinking` and `effort` capabilities from the Models API. A model that refuses budget thinking is treated as one with adaptive thinking and no sampling options - the Models API has no field for sampling, so this is inferred, and it holds for every current model. The bundled snapshot fills in what a live entry does not say.
- **Catalog prices per token are rounded to 12 significant digits.** A price of 0.1 per million tokens is now written to `definition.yaml` as `1.0e-07` instead of `1.0000000000000001e-07`. Existing definitions are unchanged.

### Fixed

- **LLM - Create LLM Call 1.5, LLM - Get Options 1.4 and `Create-LLM-Call.sas`: code for an options file without any option.** The options file of an LLM that reads its key from its container (`mdb add --auth env`) has no `API_KEY`, and one without any other option is `{}`. For it the three copies of the program wrote no code and ended with `ERROR: ... no code was written, because the options file ... holds no option that SAS could read` and `SYSCC` 1012: the table of the options has no row, and the DATA step that says that every option was read ended at its `SET` statement before it got there. Both DATA steps now ask for the number of rows with `NOBS=` in front of their `SET` statement - the value is known before a row is read -, and the code for no option sends an empty options object, `{}`. The code for an options file with options is the code of before, line for line. Read from the program and followed by the tests on the text of the three; not run in SAS.
- **A decision template manifested in the Prompt Builder can be scored.** 2.2.0 wrote the lines that return each question's answer at the wrong indentation, so the score code of every manifested decision template stopped with `SyntaxError: expected 'except' or 'finally' block` - the model registered, and nothing could run it, in SAS Intelligent Decisioning or anywhere else. The code is valid Python now, and the state template is escaped for the string it is placed in (backslashes, double quotes, braces); the manifest of a prompt template uses the same escaping. One visible consequence: a backslash typed in a prompt or a state stays a backslash in the manifested model, which is what the experiment sent - before, a typed `\n` became a line break. The designer also refuses, as an item to fix, a question name the score code cannot use: a Python keyword, a name the code uses itself, the name of a variable of the state, the `_confidence` output of another question, and a name longer than 21 characters (it was 32; the model also returns `<name>_confidence`, and a SAS name ends at 32). Templates manifested with 2.2.0 have to be manifested again.
- **A decision template stays in the decision view of the Prompt Builder.** SAS Model Manager drops the tags that are sent when a model is created, and the Prompt Builder told the kinds apart by a tag: after a reload a new decision template was listed among the LLM prompts and missing from the decision view. The kind is now read from the model's `algorithm` (`Decision-Template`), which Model Manager keeps, with the tag as a second sign; templates made with 2.2.0 carry the algorithm and are recognised without any change. A project's prompt-tests are listed with one request instead of two, and the tags are written after creation where the service left them out.
- **The optimization switch of the Prompt Builder shows what is stored.** **Enable prompt optimization (DSPy)** showed *Disabled* in the Options pane whatever the report stored, because Visual Analytics does not restore a dropdown whose keys are the words `true` and `false`: after every reload of the object it shows the first entry. The page itself followed the stored value, so the Optimize step was there while the pane said *Disabled*. The switch is a checkbox now, like the two new ones, and an existing report keeps its setting - a stored `true` shows as ticked. `mdb options-save` and `mdb options-restore` read and write the two words as before.
- **Prompt-tests made in the Prompt Builder record their modeler.** A misspelt field name meant that every prompt-test was created without one; new prompt-tests carry the signed-in user as modeler in SAS Model Manager. Existing ones are not changed.
- **An LLM run in the Prompt Builder keeps the results of the models that answered.** A model that failed ended the run at that model, and the answers of the models after it were discarded. Each failed model now has its own line with a hint - registered but no container answers at the endpoint (404 / 503), the endpoint cannot be reached, or the hint for the provider's status as before - a message says how many of the selected models did not answer, and the cost and the *fastest* and *fewest tokens* marks are worked out over the answers only. A run in which no model answered is taken back instead of being listed empty, also when the tracker was drawn again while its calls were out. Decision runs behave the same and name the failed models beside the skipped ones.
- **A RAG setup keeps its vector database in the Prompt Builder when the deployment no longer offers it.** A stored setup on a database that the Options pane withholds opened with an empty *Vector database* field, as if it had no store. It now opens with its database selected and marked *not offered for new setups*, and can still be read, saved, manifested, launched and tested; the embedding model and the caslib are kept the same way. A new setup starts with no vector database selected instead of the first in the list.
- **The Setup card of the Prompt Builder reads in the right order.** The question *What are you building?* was set larger than the title of the card it sits in and is now a smaller heading one level below that title, and the row of buttons under the pickers keeps a gap between its lines when it wraps on a narrow report object. Three kind cards fit in one row at 1024 px, in English and in German.
- **The RAG Builder part of the Prompt Builder survives a failed project listing.** When the RAG projects could not be listed, the whole builder failed to load; it now shows the reason with a **Try again** button and keeps the rest of the page. The note shown when no embedding model can be listed no longer says that a model name has to be typed - the field is a list, and without a listed model no setup can be saved.
- **The note about decisions that still use a prompt template, a decision template or a RAG setup appears on the first delete in the Prompt Builder.** Before **Delete Prompt**, **Delete Project**, **Delete setup** and **Delete project** ask for confirmation, the page asks SAS Viya which SAS Intelligent Decisioning decisions reference what is about to be deleted and names them in the dialog. On the first use of that service in a browser session the request was answered with HTTP 449 instead of a result, and the dialog said *Decision usage could not be verified* - or, for a RAG setup, *could not be checked* - for something a decision does use. The page now establishes the session with the service and sends the request again, once, for every SAS Viya service it calls; the *could not be verified* note is left for a service that really does not answer. The note is the only thing between the click and a broken decision, measured on 2026-10-11: SAS Model Manager does not refuse to delete a prompt or a RAG setup that a decision still uses (the delete requests were answered 204). The decision stays, and in SAS Intelligent Decisioning its model nodes then carry an error badge and **Validate** fails with *NOT_FOUND* for each deleted model. The User Guide says so where the dialogs are described.
- **An ingestion run of a RAG setup in the Prompt Builder is shown with the result it had.** The run panel took the word *failed* anywhere in the milestones as a failed run, and every step reports `0 failed` on a clean run - so a run that ingested everything was shown red with *The ingestion run did not succeed*. A run is now failed when the job did not complete or reports `Ingestion failed` with its reason. A run that completed is shown as completed, and one in which documents failed as completed with a warning that gives their number: the other documents are in the collection, and the ledger names each failed document with its error.
- **A RAG setup embeds its documents and its questions at one endpoint.** A question embedded at another container than the documents searches the collection with the wrong vectors, and three things were given the endpoint separately. The retrieval model that **Manifest setup** registers was written with `<SAS Viya host>/llm` whatever the **SCR endpoint** option of the Prompt Builder said. The Embed step of the generated SAS Studio flow got a blank endpoint whatever the option said, and embedded at the host of its compute session. And with a blank option the ingestion job worked out the host in its compute session, while the model carried the host of the page. The endpoint is now resolved once - the **SCR endpoint** option, and `<SAS Viya host>/llm` where it is blank, the host being the **SAS Viya host** option or the address the page is opened at - and the same value goes to the ingestion job, **Test retrieval**, the Embed step of the flow and the retrieval model. With a blank option that address has to be reachable from the compute session of the ingestion and from the publishing destination; where the page is opened under a name that is not, such as a port-forward or an alias of a reverse proxy, set the option: the ingestion otherwise calls an address it cannot reach, where it used to work out the host itself. The value is written when the setup is manifested, and the retrieval model needs the template of this release to prefer it in a compute session (see *Changed*): manifest every setup again, as step 8 of the upgrade says. A job definition and a flow generated before keep their old value until then.
- **Variable names of a prompt are checked like question names in the Prompt Builder.** Every variable of a prompt becomes an input of the manifested score code, and every output variable an output. A name that code cannot use - a Python keyword, or a name the code uses itself, such as `options`, `llm`, `endpoint` or `json` - was accepted and produced a model that could not be scored. *Define Variables* and the output variables now mark such a name as invalid and say why, as the question designer does for a question name, and a saved run that still has one is not manifested: rename the variable, run the experiment again and manifest from that run.
- **A prompt manifested for the Call LLM node in the Prompt Builder sends valid JSON.** The score code builds `llmBody` with `json.dumps`, as the variant with the included LLM call does: a backslash or a tab in a prompt or in an input value no longer breaks the body, and an apostrophe stays an apostrophe - 2.2.x turned it into a double quote, so `the customer's complaint` reached the LLM as `the customer"s complaint`. Prompts manifested with 2.2.x keep the old body until they are manifested again.
- **"Judge this run" in the Prompt Builder can be used right after a run.** After an experiment the button of the new run stayed disabled until the prompt-test was loaded again, because the tracker was drawn while the run still counted as running. It is enabled as soon as the run has ended. 2.2.0 had the same defect. A judgment that came back while a run was out left that run drawn without its answers; it is drawn with them when it ends. A run whose judgment is out keeps its button disabled, with the spinner, whatever draws the tracker again.
- **A manifest of a RAG setup that fails half way says so beside the button.** **Manifest setup** saves the setup and then writes the ingestion job, the SAS Studio flow and the retrieval model. When one of the later parts failed, the reason appeared at the top of the page while the green note *RAG setup saved* stayed beside the button, which read as a manifest that went through. The failure is now reported beside the button, where the result of the click is read: which stage stopped the manifest, the reason it gave, and what had been written by then. A save that fails says so beside **Save setup** as well.
- **A manifest whose score code was not stored is a manifest that failed, in the Prompt Builder.** SAS Model Manager answers the upload of a score code with a status, and all three manifests went on whatever it said: a toast said that the model was manifested, the tags were written, and for a RAG setup a model version was recorded - for a model that still held its old score code, or none. *Finalize* now stops there for a prompt template and for a decision template: no tags are written, no toast is shown, and a line in the manifest box says that the score code could not be stored, that the model keeps the score code and the tags it had, and that its input and output variables may already be those of this run. **Manifest setup** stops at the retrieval model with `SAS Model Manager did not store retrieve_context.py (HTTP <status>)` and records no model version.
- **A manifest in the Prompt Builder ends when a request fails outright.** A score code that SAS Model Manager refuses comes back as a status, and the manifest reports it, as the entry above says. A request that gets no answer, or an error page in place of an answer from SAS Model Manager - the 502 of an ingress - is another case: *Finalize* stopped where it stood for a prompt template and for a decision template, the button stayed disabled on *Saving...*, and nothing was said. The button is usable again now on every path. Where such a request stops the manifest, no toast says that the model was manifested, and a red line in the manifest box says where it stopped. *Not manifested: a request to SAS Model Manager failed before the score code was stored* - the model keeps the score code and the tags it had, and its input and output variables may already be those of this run. *The manifest was not completed: the score code of this run is stored, but a request to SAS Model Manager failed after that* - `requirements.json` and the attributes of the model may be missing or still those of an earlier manifest. In both cases manifest again, and if that fails too, check the connection to SAS Viya and the permissions on the model. Two requests after the score code do not stop the manifest, because the model holds its score code by then, and these lines are not for them. A tag update that fails, refused or outright, is named by its own line in the box while the manifest goes on, and the toast is shown when it reaches its end. The last step, which copies the attributes of the LLM or of the decision model onto the manifested model, is best effort: when it fails the toast is shown and the page says nothing. The upload of the score code itself and the save before the manifest have entries of their own below. Both manifests also write the tags of the model directly after the score code is stored, before `requirements.json` and the attributes, so `TLS-Verification-Off` matches the stored score code when a later request fails. **Manifest setup** of a RAG setup already ended every stage with a message. Both lines are there in German as well.
- **A score code whose upload gets no answer is reported as not known, in the Prompt Builder.** When the upload of the score code fails outright - the connection drops, or an ingress answers with its error page in place of SAS Model Manager - the service may have stored the code before the answer was lost, and the answer does not tell. For a prompt template and a decision template the manifest box said *Not manifested: the score code could not be stored* and *The model keeps the score code and the tags it had* all the same, which is only certain when SAS Model Manager answered with a refusal. The line for an upload without an answer now reads *The manifest was not completed, and it is not known whether the score code of this run is stored*, and goes on: the tags of the model were not updated, so `TLS-Verification-Off` may not say what the stored score code does, and manifest again. **Manifest setup** of a RAG setup gave the error text of the browser as its reason, under *The setup is not manifested*; it now stops at the retrieval model with *It is not known whether SAS Model Manager stored retrieve_context.py*, says the same about the tag, and its first line says that the manifest was not completed. The page now reads the score code back (see *A write whose answer was lost is read back*): only where it cannot be read back is the line *it is not known*, and until a manifest completes, the tag of such a model may then not match the score code it holds. A refusal with an answer keeps its wording. The three texts are there in German as well.
- **Save Experiments in the Prompt Builder ends when a request fails outright.** The save had the defect the manifest had: a request that got no answer, or an error page in place of one, left **Save Experiments** disabled on *Saving...* for good, and nothing was said. **Manifest Best Prompt** saves the experiments first, so the same failure there ended the click without a word, and a save that SAS Model Manager refused was followed by the manifest all the same. Now the button is usable again on every path, no toast says that the experiments were saved, and a red line beside **Save Experiments** - or in the manifest box when the click was on **Manifest Best Prompt** - says what happened: an upload that got no answer is read back, and the line says that it was not carried out, or, where nothing could be read back, that this is not known (see *A write whose answer was lost is read back*); for any other request that failed it says that the save did not finish. The runs are still on the page; save again. A manifest does not go on after a save that failed, refused or outright: the box adds *Not manifested: a manifest saves the experiments first, and SAS Model Manager did not confirm that save*, and the model keeps the score code, the tags and the variables it had. **Run optimization** and **Compare targets** save first as well; after a save that failed they now stop with *Could not launch the optimization job* and the reason, where a refused save was followed by a job that read the experiments saved before. The three texts are there in German as well.
- **A double click on Manifest Best Prompt in the Prompt Builder runs one manifest.** The button was disabled only after the experiments had been saved, so a second click in that moment started a second manifest beside the first: the experiments were saved twice and two score codes were uploaded, and a failure line of one run could stand under the other. The click now takes the button at once and gives it back when the run has ended, for a prompt template and for a decision template.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder show the whole name.** In the dialogs that create a project, a prompt-test or a RAG setup the label and the input field shared one line, and the field was too narrow to show a name of ordinary length while it was typed. The field now has the room for it.
- **Delete project and Delete setup of the RAG Builder act on what was selected at the click.** Both first ask SAS Viya which decisions use what is about to be deleted, and that takes a moment. A change of the selected project or setup in that moment made the dialog name one object while the delete removed another - at worst the setups of the project just left, under the name of an empty one. The project and the setup are now fixed at the click: **Delete project** lists the setups of that project again from SAS Model Manager and deletes nothing when the listing fails, **Delete setup** opens no dialog when another setup was chosen in the meantime, and a change of project empties the setup list at once.
- **The prompt flow of the Prompt Builder cannot be used while the RAG Builder loads.** The first choice of the RAG setup card loads the RAG Builder, and the prompt steps stayed usable while it did. A dialog opened in that moment - *Create a new Project*, for one - was hidden together with the prompt steps when the RAG steps appeared, and its backdrop stayed over the page, so nothing could be clicked until a reload. The prompt flow does not react while the RAG Builder is loaded for the first time, and reacts again when the load ends or fails.
- **Arrow keys on the kind cards of the Prompt Builder move without choosing.** The cards under *What are you building?* are a radio group, and an arrow key on one chose the next kind at once: the selected project was reset, runs that were not saved were dropped without a question, and a second key press could open the RAG Builder. An arrow key now only moves the focus; Space chooses the card that has it.
- **A run in flight belongs to its prompt-test in the Prompt Builder.** Opening another prompt-test or project while an experiment was still running let the late answers land in the tracker that was open by then: the page stopped with an error and **Run Experiments** stayed on *Running Experiments...*, or, when every model failed, a saved run of the other prompt-test was taken out of its tracker and missing after the next manifest. Answers for a prompt-test that is no longer open are now dropped, and **Run Experiments** stays disabled until the calls of the run have ended.
- **The Prompt Builder draws the prompt-test its picker names.** Opening a prompt-test takes several requests to SAS Model Manager, and nothing looked at the picker again after them. With another prompt-test or the placeholder chosen while the first was still loading, its runs, its link and its **Delete Prompt** button were drawn under the other name, and **Save** wrote those runs to the prompt-test the picker named. The answer to an earlier choice is now dropped - for a prompt-test, for the prompt-tests of a project and for the documentation of a prompt-test - and a change of kind and both deletes drop what is still on its way. The documentation section is emptied and switched off wherever the selection is dropped: another project, another kind, a deleted prompt-test or project. Before, **Save documentation** went on writing to the prompt-test that had been open. Choosing the placeholder entry of the project list or of the prompt-test list no longer sends its text to SAS Model Manager as an id.
- **A new prompt-test, decision template or RAG setup starts empty in the Prompt Builder.** The steps after **Setup** keep everything on the page, and opening a prompt-test only wrote its best run into them. A prompt-test that brought no run - a new one above all, an existing one without a best response, or none at all - was therefore shown with what the one before it had left behind (issue #47): both prompts, the variables, the selected LLMs with their options and the judge settings; for a decision template the state template, the questions and the decision models, the evaluation with its results, and the outputs chosen for the manifest; and on the Optimize step the settings, the result and the log of an optimization. The same happened in another project and after a change of kind. The later steps now go back to what a freshly loaded page shows - the defaults, not blanks - whenever a prompt-test is left, and an existing prompt-test shows its best run and nothing else; the line that names the models that failed in the last run goes with the runs, and so do a help text opened with a **?** in the question designer, the line beside the list of decision templates, an explanation folded away on the Optimize step and the servers, caslibs and tables the CAS pickers had listed, in the lists and in the menus of their search fields. With no prompt-test open the steps show none: after a change of project the Optimize step went on listing the optimization history of the prompt-test of the project before - whose **Load as experiment** filled **Build & Test** from it - and offered **Compare targets** for it. A RAG setup had the defect in what no setup stores: the question of the last retrieval test with its chunks, the number of chunks and the open test panel, the state and the milestones of the last ingestion run, the ledger, the note of the last save or manifest, the line under *SAS Content folder* that names the job or the Studio flow of the last manifest, the banner of a run that failed or a delete that was refused, and the reading under the chunk overlap stayed from the setup before; with no setup open the documentation of the last one stayed on the Setup step. An evaluation, a retrieval test, an ingestion run or the reading of a setup that is still out when a prompt-test or a setup is left is no longer drawn on the next one, a new one included: the result of an evaluation over a CAS table, which also asks no further row of the table once its template was left; the chunks of a retrieval test, which waited in the closed test panel of the setup opened next; a finished ingestion run, which opened its ledger there; and the stored configuration of a setup that answered after another one had been chosen, which was put into the editor of that one. An optimization job goes on, and its state, its run log and its result are shown on the prompt-test it runs for alone - again when that one is opened again while the job runs; any other prompt-test has a plain **Run optimization** button that is switched off and says *An optimization is already running.* above it. The placeholder entries of the two pickers of the RAG Builder are no project and no setup any more: *Select a RAG setup…* was sent to SAS Model Manager as a model id (HTTP 404, an error nobody caught) and opened the editor on nothing, and *Select a RAG project…* was sent as a project id. What is typed into **Build & Test** or set on the Optimize step before any prompt-test is open is kept for the prompt-test that is then created for it, or for one without a best response - also when a second prompt-test is chosen while the first is still being read. What was typed into an open prompt-test and not saved in a run goes when another one is opened or created, without a question. A create dialog opens empty after a creation that went through: all four kept the name and the description of the item just created, and the description was sent to SAS Model Manager with the next one. After **Cancel** or a refusal a dialog keeps what was typed. The verify harness compares the whole page of a new item of each kind with a new item on a freshly loaded page - every field, the entries of every list, the text that is shown and the text in what is hidden, what is hidden, disabled or open also inside a part that is hidden itself, and the classes and attributes of every element - on every one of these roads, and fails when a field of the later steps is changed by none of the roads that fill an item by hand (`verify-new-item.js`). The roads of `verify-new-item.js` manifest nothing; the line of a manifest, the banner and the create dialogs are checked by `verify-outcomes.js`. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **What is open cannot be changed while the Prompt Builder writes to SAS Model Manager.** A save, a manifest and a delete are each a row of requests, and the page read its pickers, its runs and its fields again between them, so an item that was opened in between took part in the write. With **Save Experiments** clicked on a prompt-test that had saved runs and another prompt-test opened right after it, a tracker without a single run was uploaded to the first one - its saved runs were replaced by none - and *The experiment has been saved* stood on the second. **Run optimization**, **Compare targets** and **Manifest Best Prompt**, which save first, uploaded the same empty tracker. In the RAG Builder, a setup opened right after **Save setup** received `pipeline.yaml` of the setup that was being saved and showed its *RAG setup saved* note, and **Manifest setup** wrote to both setups and stopped while generating the ingestion job. Now the controls of the Setup card are disabled for as long as such a write is out: the cards under *What are you building?*, the two lists with their filters, the create buttons and the delete buttons (in the Prompt Builder also the documentation fields, which stand in the same block). They are usable again when the write has ended - saved, refused or failed - so what the page says about a write is said about the item that is open. This holds for Save Experiments, Save documentation, Manifest Best Prompt (prompt and decision templates), Delete Prompt, Delete Project, the delete of an optimization run, Run optimization and Compare targets, and in the RAG Builder for Save setup, Save documentation, Manifest setup, Delete setup and Delete project; a delete and Compare targets lock from their confirmation on, the others from the click. A creation needs none of this: its dialog cannot be closed while it is out. The verify harness drives every one of these paths with its requests held back and looks at the page until the write has ended (`verify-write-lock.js`). A disabled control stops a person, not a script: a change of a list that a script sends while a write is out - the search field of a list sends one itself - is undone and reaches no handler of the page, where it opened the other item underneath the lock. A request to SAS Viya that gets no answer within two minutes is given up, and its write ends as one whose request failed: before, the Setup card stayed locked until the page was reloaded. **Delete Prompt** and **Delete Project** of the Prompt Builder open no dialog when another prompt-test or project was chosen while the usage - for a project, also its prompt-tests - was asked, as the RAG Builder does: the dialog came up for the one that had been left, and a confirmed delete closed the one opened in between. **Manifest setup** keeps its button off until the manifest has ended; it was usable while the new model version was asked for, and a click started a second manifest beside the first. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **A RAG setup that SAS Model Manager refuses to save is not reported as saved.** The uploads of `rag-setup.json` and `pipeline.yaml` and the updates of the attributes and the tags answer a refusal with a status and do not fail by themselves. **Save setup** did not read that status and said *RAG setup saved* after an upload that was answered with HTTP 500; **Save documentation** of the RAG Builder did not read it either. A status of 300 or more now ends the save as a request that fails outright always did: *Saving the RAG setup failed* in the banner and beside the button (*Saving the documentation failed* for the documentation), and a manifest stops at its first stage. The two further uploads of **Manifest setup** - the job reference written into `rag-setup.json` and the copy of the Studio flow on the setup - read their status as well, and so does the delete of an earlier flow of the same name: a refusal stops the manifest at its stage. A refused new model version at the end of the manifest says that recording it failed, where it said *a new model version recorded*. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **A write SAS Model Manager refuses is not reported as done in the Prompt Builder.** The uploads, deletes and updates of SAS Model Manager answer a refusal with a status, and the page went on over it: **Manifest Best Prompt** said *The best prompt has been manifested.* with `inputVar.json`, `outputVar.json` or `requirements.json` answered with HTTP 500; **Save Experiments** said *The experiment has been saved* when the new model version and the delete of the stored tracker were refused; the delete of an optimization run said *Optimization run deleted.* and the history still listed the run. A status of 300 or more now ends each of them as a request that fails outright does: *Not manifested* before the score code (the variables, `inputVar.json`, `outputVar.json`), *The manifest was not completed* after it (`requirements.json` or the removal of a stale one, and the last update of the attributes of the model, which was best effort and said nothing), the line that the save did not finish, and *The deletion failed* for the delete of an optimization run. That delete removed the stored tracker before it wrote back the runs that were left, so a refused write lost them; see *The tracker of a prompt or decision template is replaced in one upload, not deleted first*. The tag update still does not stop a manifest. A **Delete Prompt** or **Delete Project** whose request gets no answer said nothing and left an error in the page; it is now read back (see *A write whose answer was lost is read back*). Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **The build of the Prompt Builder takes source-map-js 1.2.2** (GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q) **and no longer uses vite-plugin-singlefile**, which reached braces through micromatch (GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm, which every released version of braces falls under). `vite.config.ts` inlines the one script and the one stylesheet into `dist/index.html` itself, and `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities. The same source builds the same bundle byte for byte.
- **A RAG setup shows its ingestion run again when it is opened again.** An ingestion goes on in SAS Viya when its setup is left. Coming back to that setup showed nothing of it - no **Ingestion run** card - and offered **Launch ingestion** beside a job that was still writing the collection. The card is now back with the state of the job, its milestones and the time since the launch, **Launch ingestion** stays off until the job has ended, and a job that ended while another setup was open is shown as finished, with its ledger. A run the page does not know of - on a page loaded after the launch, or a job started elsewhere - is asked of SAS Job Execution when the setup is opened: the newest pending or running job of the setup's job definition is shown and followed the same way, and **Launch ingestion** waits for that answer. The request filters on the state of the job and reads `jobRequest.jobDefinitionUri`. Sent as the page sends it in the second pass of 2026-10-14, it was answered 200 - with no job, and with a job launched by its job definition, whose item carried `jobRequest.jobDefinitionUri` and was found by the match of the page. Where the service refuses the request the page shows no run, as before. A run whose state cannot be read three times in a row (its record is gone, the service refuses it) is no longer followed: the card says so and **Launch ingestion** can be used again, where it stayed off until the page was reloaded. The page itself was driven against the mock of the harness and not run against SAS Viya.
- **No button that writes works while the Prompt Builder writes.** **Save setup** of the RAG Builder was given back after the first stage of **Manifest setup**. A save clicked while the job was being generated could land after the manifest had written the job reference into `rag-setup.json`. The stored setup then named no job, the generated job definition was orphaned, and **Launch ingestion** said *Manifest the setup first.* on the next visit. While any write to SAS Model Manager is out, these buttons are now off, together with the controls of the **Setup** card that were locked before:
  - in the RAG Builder: **Save setup**, **Manifest setup** and **Save documentation**;
  - in the Prompt Builder: **Save Experiments**, **Manifest Best Prompt**, **Run optimization**, **Compare targets…** and the delete of an optimization run.

  The three buttons of a RAG setup are also off while a setup that was just opened is still being read; a click in that moment wrote the defaults of a new setup over the stored one. When that read fails, they stay off until the setup is read again: opened again, or - after **Create setup** - by **Try again** (see *A read that fails is not taken for what is stored, in the Prompt Builder*). Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-write-lock.js`, `verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **The tracker of a prompt or decision template is replaced in one upload, not deleted first.** The delete of an optimization run deleted `Prompt-Optimization-Tracker.json` and then uploaded the runs that were left. A refused upload therefore lost every other run, and the history showed none. **Save Experiments** likewise deleted `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` before it uploaded the new one. Both now replace the tracker in one upload (the page sends every upload with `onConflict=update`), and the optimization tracker is deleted only when its last run is deleted; a tracker that cannot be read at the delete is not written at all (see *A read that fails is not taken for what is stored, in the Prompt Builder*). A dataset snapshot whose delete fails after its run left the history is named in a toast of its own. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya. That SAS Model Manager replaces these two files on such an upload was not measured; the RAG Builder has always saved `rag-setup.json` this way.
- **A read that fails is not taken for what is stored, in the Prompt Builder.** The page reads what SAS Model Manager holds of an item before it writes it, and it took a read that failed for an item with nothing stored. The delete of one optimization run whose `Prompt-Optimization-Tracker.json` could not be read deleted the tracker with every run and said *Optimization run deleted.* A prompt-test or decision template whose contents or `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` could not be read opened without runs and without a word, and **Save Experiments** then replaced the stored runs with the ones on the page and said they were saved. A RAG setup whose contents, `rag-setup.json` or - for a setup never saved - attributes could not be read opened with the defaults, and **Save documentation** wrote empty documentation over the stored one. **Save documentation** whose read of `rag-setup.json` was answered with an error wrote the attributes and said the documentation was saved, and a manifest whose ingestion job source was answered with an error made the text of that answer the code of the job definition. A file that could not be read is no longer taken for its content:
  - the delete writes nothing and says that the tracker could not be read (see *The delete of an optimization run says what failed*);
  - the prompt-test says beside **Save Experiments** and in a message that its saved runs could not be read, and **Save Experiments** - also as the first step of a manifest or an optimization - writes nothing until the prompt-test is opened again;
  - the RAG setup says so in the banner and keeps **Save setup**, **Manifest setup** and **Save documentation** off until it is opened again (after **Create setup**, the warning that the setup exists and could not be read back says it, and its **Try again** reads the setup);
  - the optimization history, the documentation of a prompt-test and the result of an optimization say that they could not be read (see *An optimization history, the documentation of a prompt-test and the result of an optimization that cannot be read say so*);
  - **Save documentation** writes nothing and says it failed;
  - the manifest stops while it generates the ingestion job. The same holds where the retrieval template of a manifest or the program of a retrieval test cannot be read.
  - A file on a later page of a listing is read as well: see *Every listing of the Prompt Builder reads every page*. A file that is read and holds nothing the page can read is named as such: see *A tracker or a setup file that is read and holds nothing the page can read is named as such*.

  Driven against the mock of the harness, with each read refused and without an answer (`verify-reads.js`, `verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **The delete of an optimization run whose answer is lost no longer says that it failed.** When the upload of the other runs, or the delete of the tracker, got no usable answer, the toast said *The deletion failed. Please ensure you have the required permissions in SAS Model Manager.*, although SAS Model Manager may have stored the change and the history read right after showed the run gone. The tracker is now read back: a tracker without the run is a deletion that went through, one that still holds it says that the tracker was not changed, and only where it cannot be read back does the toast say that the run may have been deleted or not (see *The delete of an optimization run says what failed*). A delete SAS Model Manager refuses with a status still says that it failed. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **Launch ingestion and Run test of the RAG Builder are off while the page writes.** While **Manifest setup** was out, both could be clicked: a launch ran the job definition the manifest had not yet rewritten, and a retrieval test wrote and deleted a job definition between the writes of the manifest. Both are now off while any write to SAS Model Manager is out, as **Save setup** is, and Enter in the question of the test starts nothing then. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-write-lock.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **Every listing of the Prompt Builder reads every page.** SAS Model Manager and the other services answer a listing a page at a time, and the page took the first page for the whole listing: a prompt-test whose `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` was not among its first 100 contents opened without runs and without a word, **Save Experiments** then wrote a tracker of the new runs only over the stored one, and the history and the delete of an optimization run found no tracker. Every listing now asks for the next page while the page it got says there is one - a `next` link, a `count` beyond what was read, or, with neither, a page as full as asked -, and a page that cannot be read fails the whole listing: the contents, variables and versions of a model, the projects and their models, the models of a tag, the members of a folder, the CAS servers, caslibs, tables and columns, the rows of a table up to the number asked for, the relationships of a model, the running jobs, and the log of a job. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`: a census of every text of `src` that names a page size, and a prompt-test whose two trackers are on the second page of its contents); how SAS Viya pages these listings was not measured; not run against SAS Viya.
- **An optimization history, the documentation of a prompt-test and the result of an optimization that cannot be read say so.** An optimization tracker that could not be read was drawn as a prompt-test without optimization runs - also right after a delete that had stopped because of it -, a documentation that could not be read left its section empty with a line in the console only, and an optimization job that completed while its tracker could not be read was reported as *The optimization job failed.* Where the history stands the page now says that it could not be read, the documentation section says so and keeps **Save documentation** off, and the result box says that the job has ended and its result could not be read. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **A tracker or a setup file that is read and holds nothing the page can read is named as such.** A `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` that SAS Model Manager answers with 200 and that is no list of runs - an HTML page, `{"rows":[]}` -, and a `rag-setup.json` that is no JSON object, were reported as files that could not be read, with the advice to open the item again, which reads the same file. A tracker that is a list whose entries are not rows of a run - `["a"]`, `[{"foo":1}]`, `[[]]`, `[1,2]`, and `[{"runId":1}]`, which was drawn as a run whose prompts read *undefined* - was taken for a tracker: no line was shown, and **Save Experiments** replaced the file. An optimization tracker of such entries (`[{"foo":1}]`) drew a run *failed #?* whose Delete removed the file. Files that hold the fields of a run were taken the same way: a run of its header row alone, a model named `judge` or `__proto__` and a model listed twice in a run were drawn and left out by **Save Experiments**, which replaced the file; a response that is an object was drawn as *[object Object]*; an optimization entry whose `targets` or `evaluations` are a text stopped the history with an error; a Delete of one of two runs with one `optimizationId` removed both; and an enrichment of a `rag-setup.json` that is no prompt was left out by **Save setup**. Every JSON file the page reads from SAS Model Manager is now checked against the fields the page reads from it, with one check for each name of a file (`src/api/stored-json.ts`): in `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` every run starts with a row of its own - a numeric `runId`, no model, both prompts as texts - and goes on with at least one row per model, each model once and none of a name the page reads as a field of a run (`judge`, `mode`, `systemPrompt`, ...) or `__proto__`; in `Prompt-Optimization-Tracker.json` every entry has a numeric `optimizationId` of its own and a `status`; every field of a row of the tracker and of an entry of the optimization tracker, at every depth, is null, absent or of the kind the page reads (`STORED_SHAPES` - a manifest configuration must hold its outputs); `rag-setup.json` is a setup of version 1 whose every field, at every depth, is null, absent or of the kind the page reads (`RAG_SETUP`: the sections of a setup and the `promptModelId` of each enrichment prompt must be there), and whose enrichment is a list of prompts, or the one prompt of a setup saved before the enrichment could chain; in the `options.json` of an LLM every option is checked as the page draws it and sends it (`isModelOptions`; see *Every option of an LLM is drawn and sent as its `options.json` writes it*; an enum option whose values were numbers stopped the page from being built, and it showed *Could not load the Prompt Builder* in place of every prompt-test); and in the `options.json` of a decision model the one field the page reads of it, `API_KEY.default`, where the file has an `API_KEY`, is a text that is not empty (`isDecisionModelOptions`). A file that fails is treated like the other files that hold nothing the page can read. The page now says that SAS Model Manager answered with a file that holds nothing it can read, that **Save Experiments** - for a RAG setup **Save setup**, **Manifest setup** and **Save documentation** - writes nothing so as not to replace that file, and that the file is to be repaired or removed in SAS Model Manager. The optimization tracker says the same of such a file in the history, which then shows no run and no Delete, and at the delete of a run, which then writes nothing. An LLM or a decision model whose `options.json` fails is shown disabled with a note that says so and is offered nowhere - not as a judge, a member or the chairman of a council, a target of **Compare targets…** or of an optimization (see *An LLM that cannot be used is no chairman*); an `options.json` that cannot be read leaves the model offered without options, as before. Every `options.json` of the repository passes the check of its kind of model. `verify-reads.js` takes every `.ts` file under `src` and holds every read of a stored file to this check or to a read of text; it compares each shape with the type of the sources it mirrors, field for field, and sends each check, for every field at every depth, a value of another kind, null and no value. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`, `verify-outcomes.js`, `verify-round-trip.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **Prompt Builder: every option of an LLM is drawn and sent as its `options.json` writes it, or the model is not offered.** The first check of `options.json` in this release took options the page then drew as something else: an `API_KEY` whose default was a number or `true` named a provider *5* or *true* in the note about the credential domain, and one that was a list was taken as its one entry; an enum without `values` - which mdb wrote for an inline option `type: enum` without values - was drawn as a number field, lost its default and was sent empty; a bool whose default was `1` was drawn unticked and sent as `false`. It also refused what the page never reads: a number in the label of `temperature`, values that are no texts of an option that is no enum, and every field but `API_KEY.default` of a decision model, whose card then said that its `options.json` holds no options the page can read. The check of an LLM's `options.json` now asks of each option what the page reads of it: an object that holds its default, under a name without a comma or a colon; of `API_KEY` its default alone, a text that is not empty; of an enum a non-empty list of texts without a comma that holds its default; of a bool a default that is true or false, or that text; of any other option a default that is a text without a comma, a number, true or false - null, a list or an object cannot be drawn as written; its type, where given, a text, and its label and description, where given, texts - not read of a number in one of the page's own fields (`temperature`, `top_p`, `top_k`, `max_length`, `max_tokens`, `max_completion_tokens`, `max_new_tokens`).
