That is the rule of mdb's `check_options_json`, word for word; beyond it the page takes a bool's default `"true"` / `"false"`, and of `API_KEY` and of a number in one of its own number fields it does not read the other fields. mdb refuses a default of null, a list or an object as well: it wrote one for `base_url` of a self-hosted model from `provider.params`. The page draws what it takes as it is written: an option of no type has a number field for a number default and a text field otherwise, sent as text, and one of the page's own number fields is drawn as its type says when it is no number. A decision model is checked for its `API_KEY.default` alone. The note reads *The options.json of this model is not one this page can read, so the model cannot be used here. Repair the file in SAS Model Manager.* - it said the file held no options the page can read, which is untrue of a file with one option the page cannot read beside others it can. `verify-reads.js` probes each rule; `verify-round-trip.js` has mdb's own generator write every option shape of `OptionSpec` (`options-writer.py`): mdb writes 3926 of the 8413 shapes and refuses the other 4487, every file it writes is taken, and an LLM of each of the 217 kinds of file draws each of its options (418 in all) with its default as written and sends it so in a run - but for an option of type `string` whose default is empty, which a run leaves out so that the score code takes its own default; the 36 `options.json` of `LLM-Definitions` and `Decision-Definitions` pass the check of their kind. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`, `verify-round-trip.js`); not run against SAS Viya. In the tenth pass, on 2026-10-15, the page with that first check - served by SAS Job Execution from a test content root, with the `options.json` of a throw-away LLM answered by a stand-in in the browser - was built with an enum option whose values are numbers and showed that LLM disabled, where the bundle before it showed *Could not load the Prompt Builder*; the check of this entry was made after that pass.
- **`mdb` refuses an option the Builder would draw or send differently from what its `options.json` says.** mdb could write seven such options:
  - an inline option `type: enum` without `values`: the page drew it as a number input and sent it empty;
  - an enum whose default is not one of its values;
  - a `bool` option whose default is `1` or a text other than `"true"` and `"false"`: drawn unticked and sent as false (mdb refuses the texts `"true"` and `"false"` as well, which the Builder draws and sends as true and false);
  - the option of the provider key declared by hand in a manifest with `auth.mode: none`, whose default was not a text;
  - a default of `.nan` or `.inf`, written as `NaN` / `Infinity`, which is not JSON;
  - the option `base_url` of a self-hosted model, written from `provider.params`, whose default was null, a list or an object: the page cannot draw such a default as written;
  - an option whose name holds a comma or a colon (an informational option, which mdb writes under any name), or whose text default or enum value holds a comma: the Builder sends the options as `{name:value,name:value}`, and the score code read the default `a,b` as `a`.

  The rule is written once, in `check_options_json` (`mdb/core/generator.py`), and the Builder reads the `options.json` of an LLM by the same table (`isModelOptions`, with the differences *Every option of an LLM is drawn and sent as its `options.json` writes it* names):
  - the name of an option holds no comma and no colon, and a text default or a value of an enum holds no comma (the default of the provider key, which a run does not send, may);
  - an enum has a non-empty list of texts as its values, and its default is one of them;
  - a bool option's default is true or false;
  - the default of the option of the provider key is a non-empty text;
  - the default of any other option is a text, a number, true or false;
  - type, label and description are texts where given;
  - no option holds NaN or Infinity.

  Beyond the rule, the parser mdb generates into the score code (`_options_parser.py.j2`) takes the blanks and then the quotes off the ends of a value of the options string, reads `true` and `false` in any case as true and false, and reads a value as a number where `float()` reads one that holds a point or `int()` one that does not. The Builder also leaves an option of type `string` whose field is empty out of a run, and the score code uses its own default for it.

  `mdb generate` refuses a manifest that breaks the rule with one line naming the option, before anything is written. `add`, `retire` and `validate` (rule V003, also run by `ship`) do the same. Each prints the line on one line, also for an option whose name holds a line break; `validate` and `add` printed such a name over two lines. `mdb import` and `mdb pull --import` refuse a hand-maintained `options.json` in the same way when what import reads of it breaks the rule: the name and the default of each option (1 where none is given) and the key name of the option of the provider key where one is given. A default that is null, a list or an object, and a provider key whose default is no text, used to end `mdb import` in a traceback that, for the provider key, did not name the option; `mdb import` now says that nothing was written and to repair the `options.json`. A `definition.yaml` whose option holds a default that is null, a list or an object, or another field its schema does not take, never reaches the rule: the schema of `definition.yaml` refuses it first, as it refuses every `definition.yaml` it cannot read - `generate` and `register` print its message on one line naming the field (`options.<name>.default`), `validate` reports it under rule V001, and `retire` ends with a traceback. Nothing is written. `mdb register` checks the options of the manifest and the `options.json` it uploads (also one listed under `generation.overrides`) before it writes anything to SAS Model Manager. `mdb retire` now renders before it saves the manifest with its `deprecated` tag, so a manifest that cannot be rendered no longer ends in a traceback with the tag saved and the files not regenerated. It also refuses a generated file edited by hand before it saves the tag: until now the tag was saved first, and the rerun the refusal asks for (`mdb generate --force`, then `mdb retire`) stopped at *already deprecated.* without retiring the model in SAS Model Manager. All 53 `options.json` files of the repository keep the rule, and `mdb generate --all --check` is clean. Run on the workstation (`tests/test_mdb_26.py`, `tests/test_builder_26_other_default.py`, `tests/test_fix_mdb_26.py`, `tests/test_fix_26_options_string.py`, with SAS Viya replaced by a stand-in in the register, retire and pull tests); not run against SAS Viya. In the eleventh pass, on 2026-10-16, `mdb generate`, `validate` and `retire` of a manifest with an enum without values, an enum whose default is not one of its values, a bool whose default is `1`, a provider key whose default is 5 and a default of `.nan` each printed the line - `validate` behind `[V003]` - and ended with exit code 1 without changing a file, on the workstation, as they were before the changes of this paragraph made after that pass.
- **Prompt Builder: each option of an LLM is found by an id of its own, whatever its name.** The control of an option had the id `<name><index>`, which for some names was the id of another element: an option named `model` was found as the tick box of its LLM, so a run sent the name of the model as that option, and a saved run, loaded, unticked the LLM; one named `options` was found as the box that holds the options, and a run sent `undefined`; one named `__proto__` was not sent at all. mdb writes all three names. The control of every option that is not in one of the page's own number fields now has the id `llm<index>-option-<name>` (`optionControlId` in `src/api/stored-json.ts`; `%` and `-` of the name are written `%25` and `%2D`), and the options of a run and of a saved run are kept in objects without a prototype. `verify-round-trip.js` has mdb write an option named like every element of the page (the id with the digits at its end and without them), like every field of an object, and like names that test how an id is built from a name - 175 names, written as informational options, which mdb writes under any name. Each file is taken by the check of the page; on one page three LLMs of the file that holds all of them show each option inside its own LLM with no id twice, a run sends each as written, and the run loaded - from the tracker in memory and from the one **Save Experiments** wrote - chooses each as it was sent. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **Prompt Builder: a judge is sent temperature 0 only where its temperature is a number.** A judge, a member of a council and the chairman were sent `temperature:0` whenever their `options.json` held a `temperature` - also one that is an enum of texts or a text, which mdb writes and of which 0 is no value. They are now sent it only where the temperature is a number; otherwise the score code keeps its own default. `verify-round-trip.js` names every call that sends options to a model (`SENDERS`) and has a council judge a run, with one LLM of each of the 217 kinds of `options.json` mdb writes (36 with a `temperature`, 4 of them a number): each member is sent its provider key, and temperature 0 only where its temperature is a number. On the bundle before, 32 members were sent something else. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **Prompt Builder: an option the score code would read otherwise than its `options.json` says is refused - a name with a comma or a colon, a text with a comma.** A run sends the options of an LLM as one text, `{name:value,name:value}`; the score code splits it at each comma, and each name from its value at the first colon. A default `a,b` was offered, shown as written, and read by the score code as `a`. The check of an LLM's `options.json` (`isModelOptions`) now refuses an option whose name holds a comma or a colon, and one whose default - or, for an enum, one of its values - is a text with a comma; the model is then not offered. The default of `API_KEY`, which a run does not send, is not affected. mdb's `check_options_json` refuses the same options with one line naming the option; it wrote them for a text default, for an enum value and for an informational option of any name. `verify-reads.js` probes each rule, and `options-writer.py` gives mdb a text and an enum value with a comma and the names `a,b` and `a:b`: mdb refuses each, and every file it writes is taken. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **A write whose answer was lost is read back.** A write that got no usable answer - no answer at all, an answer that cannot be read (the error page of an ingress), or a 502, 503 or 504, which a gateway answers in place of a service - was taken for one that failed: a **Delete Prompt** that SAS Model Manager had carried out said *The deletion failed. Please ensure you have the required permissions in SAS Model Manager.* and kept the deleted prompt-test in the list and open, and **Delete setup** did the same. Every write of the page now reads back what SAS Viya holds when it gets no usable answer: a delete reads whether the item is still there, an upload reads the file of its name and compares it with what was sent, an update reads the model or the job definition, a creation looks for its name (a job definition for its name, code and parameters), a new model version for a version that was not listed before the request, and a launch for its job among the jobs that are running, by the name, the job definition and the arguments that the job's request (`jobRequest`) carries. A write that was carried out goes on as if it had been answered: a deleted prompt-test, setup or project leaves the page, a save is a save, a manifest goes on after a score code that is stored, a created prompt-test is opened. One that was not says *A request to SAS Viya got no usable answer, and reading back what SAS Viya holds shows that it was not carried out.*, with the name of the button in front and no word of permissions; where nothing could be read back, the page says that it is not known whether the request was carried out - for the score code of a manifest that is the earlier *it is not known whether the score code of this run is stored*. A launch that is not found among the running jobs is not known either, never not launched; the page says so - *A request to SAS Viya got no usable answer, and the job it was to launch is not among the jobs that are running: whether it was launched is not known, as it may have ended already.* - for **Launch ingestion**, **Run test**, **Run optimization** and **Compare targets…**. It said that what SAS Viya holds could not be read back, which it still says where the list of the running jobs cannot be read. A refusal with a status still says that the write failed. Two neighbours of the same defect: a **Delete setup** or **Delete project** of the RAG Builder that went through and whose list could not be read afterwards said *Deleting failed*; it now says that the item was deleted and that the list could not be refreshed. And a retrieval test whose transient job definition could not be deleted said so in the console only; its panel says it now. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify.js`, `verify-decision.js`, `verify-rag.js`, `verify-outcomes.js`, with each write aborted before and after the mock carried it out, and a census of every write of `src/api` and every call of one in `src/objects`); not run against SAS Viya. A launch of **Launch ingestion**, of **Run test** and of **Run optimization** whose request never reached the mock, with the running jobs listed, says that its job is not among them, and one of **Launch ingestion** whose list fails as well says that nothing could be read back (`verify-outcomes.js`). A launch of **Launch ingestion** and of **Run test** whose answer is lost after the mock carried it out is followed to its end (`verify-outcomes.js`); it was looked for by a name outside `jobRequest`, where neither the mock nor the documented job representation of SAS Job Execution has it, and was not found. That SAS Job Execution lists a job launched through its definition among the running jobs with its name and its arguments in `jobRequest` was not measured; the seventh pass saw `jobRequest.jobDefinitionUri` there.
- **The delete of an optimization run says what failed.** A delete whose tracker could not be read said *The deletion failed. Please ensure you have the required permissions in SAS Model Manager.*, also where that read got no answer. It now says that the run was not deleted because the optimization tracker could not be read, or holds no runs the page can read, and that nothing was written. A request without an answer before the tracker was written, and an upload of the tracker that reading back shows not carried out, say that the tracker was not changed; an upload that reading back shows carried out is a run that was deleted; only where the tracker cannot be read back does the toast still say that the run may have been deleted or not. A refusal still says that the deletion failed, and a dataset snapshot whose delete was refused or got no answer is named without a word of permissions. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **RAG Builder: Launch ingestion waits for every question about a running ingestion of its setup.** A setup opened, left and opened again while the first question to SAS Job Execution was out had two questions out, and the answer to the first let **Launch ingestion** be used while the second was still out. The page now counts the questions of each setup. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **RAG Builder: Try again reads a setup that was created and could not be read back.** After **Create setup**, a setup that could not be read back kept **Save setup**, **Manifest setup** and **Save documentation** off; the warning did not say so, and its **Try again** listed the setups again without reading the setup, which left the buttons off and nothing saying why. The warning now says that the three buttons stay off until the setup is read and that **Try again** reads it, and **Try again** reads it: the buttons follow what that read gets. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-outcomes.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **An action of the Prompt Builder whose button is off starts once.** Enter in the question of a retrieval test started a second and a third test while **Run test** was off - each with its job definition and its job -, and a click event that a script sends to a button that is off reached its listeners in Edge, where a click of the mouse or of the keyboard and `button.click()` do not. Enter is now refused for what **Run test** is off for, and a click on any control of the page that is off is stopped on the window, before every listener of the page - also a click sent to a node inside such a control, such as the text of a button. That includes Bootstrap's listener on the document, which opened the dialog of **Create a new Project**, **Create a new Prompt**, **Create project** and **Create setup** while they were off during a write. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-outcomes.js`: three Enters and a scripted click write one job definition. At three moments - a retrieval test running, **Save setup** in progress and **Save Experiments** in progress - every control that is off, and every node inside it, is sent a scripted click; a probe that sees every click listener of the page finds that only the guard runs and that no dialog opens. A census covers every listener of a key in `src`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **Prompt Builder: the prompts and the model names of a saved run are drawn as text.** The experiment tracker drew the system prompt and the user prompt of a run of a prompt template, and the name of each model, as markup: a `Prompt-Experiment-Tracker.json` whose prompt held `<img src=x onerror=...>` ran script in the page of whoever opened the prompt-test, and a prompt that held `<` lost what followed it (*Compare a<b and c>d* read *Compare ad*). They are now escaped, as the texts of a decision run already were. `verify-reads.js` reads every value the sources give to `innerHTML`, `outerHTML` and `insertAdjacentHTML` and holds each text in it to a literal, `escapeHtml` or a named text of the page. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **Prompt Builder: an LLM that cannot be used is no chairman, and a loaded run does not tick it.** The chairman list of a council listed every LLM, also one whose `options.json` is not one the page can read and one without a key in the credential domain; opening a prompt-test whose saved run used such an LLM, or loading an optimization run made for it, ticked its disabled box, and **Run Experiments** called it. The chairman list now leaves such an LLM out, as the judge list and the council members do, a saved run puts back its judges and its chairman only where they can be used, and neither a saved run nor an optimization run ticks a box that is off. `verify-reads.js` names every use of the lists of the models in the sources with what it does with a model that cannot be used. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`, `verify-new-item.js`); not run against SAS Viya.
- **The Prompt Builder no longer refuses the experiment trackers it writes itself, and no longer takes a `rag-setup.json` whose fields are of another kind.** An LLM or a decision model registered as `constructor`, `toString` or another name of a field every object has was run and saved, and the prompt-test opened again then said that its saved runs cannot be used. A model named like a field of a run (`mode`, `judge`, `systemPrompt`, ...) overwrote that field of the run, or its result was neither drawn nor saved. Now a model named like a field every object has is a model of its own. A model named `systemPrompt`, `userPrompt`, `author`, `variables`, `manifest`, `judge`, `mode` or `expected` - names the page reads as fields of a run, `author` among them although no run holds it - or `__proto__` is shown disabled with a note that says why (*This model cannot be used here: a run keeps the result of each model under the name of the model, and a run cannot keep the result of a model under the name …. Register the model under another name to use it here.*) and is offered nowhere else. A model named like a field every object has, such as `valueOf`, that answered nothing in a run judged by a council no longer shows the rank *#function valueOf() { [native code] }*. A `rag-setup.json` whose documentation field held an object opened without a warning, and **Save setup** wrote *[object Object]* back; a policy stored as the text `no` went into `pipeline.yaml` as true. Such a setup is now named as a setup the page cannot read, and nothing is written over it. `verify-round-trip.js` checks, for every writer of every JSON file the page reads, that what it writes is read back: **Save Experiments**, **Save setup**, **Save documentation**, **Manifest setup**, the delete of an optimization run, the optimization job `Optimize-Prompt-DSPy.sas` in ten ways it ends (its Python run with stand-ins for SAS, SAS Viya and dspy), and every `options.json` of the repository. Driven against the mock of the harness (`verify-reads.js`, `verify-round-trip.js`); not run in SAS or against SAS Viya but for this: in the tenth pass, on 2026-10-15, the page was served by SAS Job Execution from a test content root on SAS Viya 2026.09, with LLMs named `author` and `mode` added to the listing of the models by a stand-in in the browser, and showed both disabled with this note, in English and in German, and offered them in none of the lists.
- **The verify suites of the Prompt Builder give one answer for one bundle.** Under load, three suites failed for reasons that had nothing to do with the page:
  - `verify-new-item.js` stopped when it emptied the name field of a create dialog that had faded out;
  - `verify-write-lock.js` stopped on a request of the suite to its own mock that got no answer (*fetch failed*), and failed checks that read the page before it had drawn an item;
  - `verify-outcomes.js` stopped on Playwright's navigation time-out of 30 seconds.

  The fixes: the field is emptied by script. Every request of a suite to its mock goes through `mock-fetch.cjs`, which sends a request that got no answer again, and `run-all.cjs` refuses a suite without it. The checks after a write wait for what they read. The pages of the suites that run roads wait up to 180 seconds for a navigation.

  A failure of the check of the line under the SAS Content folder after a manifest came from a click on **Manifest setup** before the stored setup was read: the defaults were manifested, and the validation refused them. Since *No button that writes works while the Prompt Builder writes*, the button is off until the setup is read, and the check now names what the manifest said. Two runs of `run-all.cjs` at the same time on one machine passed every suite, both of them: 438, 237, 343, 89, 79, 46, 27 and 641 checks, and the locale and token checks.

  Under load two roads still gave two answers: `verify-outcomes.js` gave up after 30 seconds on a save of a setup that was still out, and `verify-new-item.js` clicked the Save button of a create dialog that was still moving in (*element is not stable*). Every wait of a fixed time that stood in for a state - 147 in the harness - now waits on the state itself (`page-waits.js`): the request answered and the page with no request out, also none a route holds back; a dialog faded in, standing still and holding the focus, or every dialog gone; a confirmation that is answered waits for its own dialog to go, not for every dialog (a second one follows it in a Delete project); a held answer released once the suite has looked at the page while the request is out, where a hold of a fixed time ended before the look on a loaded machine. `run-all.cjs` waits until the mock answers or its process has ended (it gave the mock ten seconds); the polls that ran out first under load now look for up to a minute - a create dialog that answers (it gave up after 4 seconds), the fields of a dialog given back (6), a link that says it was copied and shows its text again (3 and 5), a run shown again (8) -, and the page at rest (15 and 20 seconds) for up to two; every other poll on a state keeps its bound, most of them 8 to 15 seconds; `verify-options.js` waits until every request of the page is answered in place of 600 ms without a new one; and `verify-write-lock.js` judges an attempt made after a write by what is open then - on a loaded machine its click threw while the other prompt-test was opened. `verify-waits.js` reads the harness and holds every call of `sleep`, `setTimeout` and `waitForTimeout` to the definition of a sleep, a poll, or the one answer held for a time that is a condition of its own, and refuses a wait on the `show` class of a dialog. Measured on the bundle with the sha256 `b94715d0…`, before the last changes of the page in this release: ten passes of `run-all.cjs`, two at a time, beside four processes that each kept a processor busy - 20 runs of the twelve suites, 0 failures (each run: verify 439 OK, verify-decision 251, verify-rag 345, verify-new-item 89, verify-write-lock 79, verify-outcomes 76, verify-reads 141, verify-round-trip 32, verify-options 641, verify-locales 9, verify-tokens 2, verify-waits 3).
- **Prompt Builder: a poll of an optimization job or an ingestion is not sent again while it is out.** The optimization job and the ingestion are polled every five seconds. A poll whose answer took longer went out again at each tick, and each of them acted on the end of the job: with the polls of an optimization job held for a minute, 12 were out at once, and once they were answered the log of the job was read 12 times and its tracker 15 times. Each timer now calls its poll through `oneAtATime` (`src/util/one-at-a-time.ts`), which sends none while the one before is out. `verify-outcomes.js` names every timer of `src` that repeats (`TIMERS`) and holds each one that polls SAS Viya to `oneAtATime`; two roads hold the first poll of an optimization job and of an ingestion and run the clock of the page on by a minute: 1 poll sent each, 13 each on the bundle before. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **A Delete project that stops part of the way shows what is left.** Both builders delete a project item by item. When a later item's delete was refused or got no answer, the list kept showing items that were gone (opening one drew an empty prompt-test or setup), and the message said only that the deletion failed. The page now reads the project back from SAS Model Manager: the list shows what is there, an open item that was deleted is closed, and the message names the project, what was deleted from it and what is still in it. A project that turns out to be gone ends the delete as one that went through. Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **In the Prompt Builder, the line of a manifest of a prompt or decision template that stopped names every state of the variables.** *Not manifested* said that the model's input and output variables may already be those of this run. They are deleted one by one before `inputVar.json` and `outputVar.json` are uploaded, so the line now says they may be the earlier ones, those of this run, a part of either, or none (English and German). Driven against the mock of the harness; not run against SAS Viya.
- **Create Prompt and Create Project in the Prompt Builder report a refused creation.** When SAS Model Manager refused to create a prompt-test - a name that exists already, no permission to write - the dialog closed, an entry named `undefined` was added to the list and selected, and every later request went to a model with that id. The dialog now stays open, a message says that nothing was created and what to check, and nothing is added to the list; the dialog for a new RAG setup reports a refusal in the same way. The message stands inside the dialog, in all four create dialogs: the RAG Builder wrote a missing name and a refusal to the banner at the top of the page, behind the backdrop of the dialog, so the click seemed to do nothing. **Create** is disabled while a creation is out - a double click created two RAG projects, or a setup and a refusal for its name.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder send nothing without a name.** *Create a new Project* with the name field empty sent the request all the same, and the dialog then said that the project was not created because a project of this name may already exist, or you may not be allowed to write there - neither of which was the matter. All four create dialogs - *Create a new Project*, *Create a new Prompt*, and *Create project* and *Create setup* of the RAG Builder - now refuse a name that is empty or blanks only themselves: no request is sent, the dialog says *A project name is required.* (or *A prompt name is required.*, *A setup name is required.*), the name field is marked and has the cursor, and the button stays usable. The two new texts are there in German as well.
- **A name that shows nothing is no name in the create dialogs of the Prompt Builder.** A name made of a zero-width space, or of other characters that are not displayed, with or without blanks, passed the check for an empty name and was sent to SAS Model Manager. All four create dialogs now treat such a name as empty: the characters Unicode sets apart as not displayed - the zero-width space, the zero-width joiner and non-joiner, the word joiner, the byte order mark, the soft hyphen, the direction marks and the others of the property `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` - count like white space. A name can look empty without any of them: the braille pattern blank (U+2800) is drawn as nothing, is neither white space nor in that property, and a name made of it was still sent. The dialogs now ask whether a name has a visible character at all. Not visible are white space, the characters of `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` - the Hangul fillers, the combining grapheme joiner, the Mongolian vowel separator, the Arabic letter mark, the tag characters and the variation selectors are among them -, the control characters, and three characters whose shape is defined as empty: the braille pattern blank, the musical null notehead (U+1D159) and the Khitan small script filler (U+16FE4). Every other character makes a name - a letter of any script, a digit, punctuation, a symbol, an emoji, also one put together with zero-width joiners or a variation selector. A name that has a visible character is sent as before: as typed in the two dialogs for a project and a prompt, with the blanks at its ends removed in the two RAG dialogs.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder create what stood in them at the click.** *Create a new Project* asks SAS Model Manager for the repository before it creates the project, and read the name field again after that answer: a name cleared in that moment was sent as an empty one, past the check of the dialog. The dialog now hands the name and the description of the click to the creation, in all four create dialogs, and the two fields cannot be edited while a creation is out; they can again when it has ended.
- **A create dialog of the Prompt Builder says so when a request on the way to a creation fails.** *Create a new Project* asks SAS Model Manager for the repository before it creates the project. When that request got no answer, an error page where JSON was expected, or a refusal, the click ended without a word: nothing in the dialog, an error in the console of the browser. The dialog now says that the project was not created because the repository could not be read, and that nothing was sent to create it; the text is there in German as well. An error nobody expected ends the same way in all four create dialogs - the dialog says that the creation did not finish, and to look in SAS Model Manager before trying again. *Create setup* of the RAG Builder lists the setups and reads the new one after it has created it. A failure there was reported in the dialog as *Creating the RAG setup failed* although the setup existed - an invitation to create it a second time - and when it was the listing that failed, the new setup was missing from the picker. The dialog now closes, the new setup is selected with its editor open, and the page says that the setup was created but could not be read back, with **Try again** - as *Create project* does for its project list. The rule is the same in all four dialogs: until SAS Model Manager has answered the creation nothing exists, and what fails is said in the dialog, which stays open; from then on the thing exists and the dialog closes, and what fails after it is never reported as a creation that failed - in the two RAG dialogs it is a warning on the page; in the two dialogs for a project and a prompt the new entry is listed and selected, and a request after it that fails (the tags of the new prompt-test, the listing of what the new project holds) is written to the console of the browser.
- **The create dialogs of the Prompt Builder keep the focus, and Escape closes them.** The button that creates is disabled while a creation is out. That took the focus away from it - after Enter or the space bar on the button, and after a click - to the page behind the dialog, where it stayed after a refusal, and Escape closed nothing from there. The focus now waits on the dialog while the creation is out and is back on the button when the creation was refused or failed; with a name missing it is in the name field, as before. Escape closes an open dialog wherever the focus is. While a creation is out the dialog cannot be closed: its closing button and the X are disabled, and Escape and a click beside the dialog do nothing until SAS Model Manager has answered. Before, a dialog closed in that moment left the creation running, and a refusal was written into the closed dialog. All four create dialogs behave this way.
- **Links in the Prompt Builder copy their address when Visual Analytics blocks a new tab, and only then.** The sandbox Visual Analytics puts around the page does not allow it to open a tab, so these links did nothing on a click: the link to the saved prompt-test after **Save** and in the manifest box, the link to a prompt-test that an optimization of an earlier release produced, the user-guide link of the Optimize step, and in the RAG Builder the links to a decision in the delete dialogs and **Open in SAS Model Manager**, the button as well as the link in the note after **Save setup**. Like **Open in SAS Model Manager** of the prompt flow, they now copy the link and say so for a moment. That link in turn copied on every click, also where the tab had opened - with `allow-popups` in the sandbox setting, or on the job URL opened in a browser tab - and so overwrote the clipboard each time. All of these links now copy only where no tab opened, and a second click while the note is shown no longer leaves the note as the text of the link. *Open link in new tab* from the right-click menu works as before.
- **The Prompt Builder sends no `Referer` to another site.** The page is served from the SAS Viya host, and a browser tells the site a link leads to where the click came from: the user-guide link of the Optimize step goes to `sassoftware.github.io`, and where its tab opened, that site was told the SAS Viya host. The page now carries the referrer policy `same-origin` - a `meta` element in its head, which the single-file build keeps - so a link or a request to another origin than the page's carries no `Referer`: the user-guide link, and a call to a model container on another host. That keeps the SAS Viya host from the site a link leads to, not from a host the page calls: a call to a model container on another host still names the origin of the page in its `Origin` header, as every cross-origin call of a browser does, whatever the referrer policy. A tab on SAS Viya itself, such as SAS Model Manager, is still told where the click came from and opens as before, and the calls to SAS Viya keep their `Referer` - where the page is served from the SAS Viya origin, as it is in the deployment the guide describes. With the `viyaHost` parameter naming another origin, SAS Viya is another origin to the page as well, and neither its tabs nor its calls get a `Referer`. The user-guide link also says `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, for the middle mouse button and *Open link in new tab*, which do not go through the click.
- **`mdb package-export` says which host it still found.** When an exported package still named a host after the rewrite, the command stopped with an `AttributeError` instead of the message it was about to print; it now lists every finding with its report, host and place.
- **The run lock of a RAG ingestion follows the column types of the ledger.** The ingestion job (`Ingest-Documents.sas`) and the SAS Studio flow that are generated for one setup keep the same ledger and take the same run lock on it, a row appended to the table. The job loads the ledger from a SAS data set, which makes its text columns `CHAR`; the steps *RAG - Load Vector Store* and *RAG - Purge Documents* upload it through SWAT, which makes them `VARCHAR`; and each of the two declared its lock row the one way it stores the ledger itself. CAS does not append a row whose column is the one where the table has the other. Seen on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09: the flow of a setup that the job had ingested stopped in *RAG - List Documents* with `ERROR: The variable doc_id has been specified with incompatible data types` and *the run lock could not be written to … - refusing to ingest unguarded*, and the ledger kept its rows; the append the other way round, a `CHAR` row to a `VARCHAR` table, was refused with the same text in a scratch caslib - the job itself was not run on a ledger a flow had written, and by its code it would have gone on without a lock, because it did not look whether its lock had been written. It is one more place where the two were not the same pipeline (see 2.0.0), and it has been there since 2.0.0. The job, *RAG - List Documents* and *RAG - Purge Documents* now ask the table for its column types (`table.columnInfo`) before they take the lock, declare the lock row to match - the same two macros in all three - and say in the log which they found: *NOTE: the ledger … holds its text columns as char - the run lock is written the same way*, or *as varchar*. The four steps that read the ledger compare its values without the blanks a `CHAR` column is padded with. A ledger of either type is used as it is and nothing is migrated; the ledger still takes the type of the writer that last completed a run, because the two were not made to store it alike. Measured on 2026-10-11 with the job and the generated flow of one setup, in both directions: the job on the `CHAR` ledger it had written logged *holds its text columns as char - the run lock is written the same way*, the append added one row and the run completed with 3 documents unchanged; the flow on that `CHAR` ledger - the case that had failed - ended with all five steps green, and the ledger was `VARCHAR` afterwards, with 3 rows *unchanged* and no lock row; the job on the `VARCHAR` ledger the flow had left logged *as varchar*, appended one row and completed with 3 unchanged. Measured again the same day with pgvector and with SingleStore 9.0.40, the job and the flows run as SAS Job Execution jobs: *RAG - Purge Documents* in the mode purge, on a `CHAR` and on a `VARCHAR` ledger, appended its lock the way the table holds its text, logged that 1 chunk row was removed, and left 2 documents and no lock row in the ledger; a failing *RAG - List Documents* on both types handed its lock back, and the next run was not refused; and the job on a project without a ledger wrote a `CHAR` ledger and took the lock on it in its second run. Added after the first measurements: the job reads its lock back. *RAG - List Documents* refuses to go on when the lock it appended is not in the ledger; the job never looked, so a lock CAS had refused went unnoticed there. It now exports the ledger a second time after the append - with the statements of its first export - and ends with *the run lock could not be written to the ledger … - refusing to ingest unguarded* when that export holds no lock row the first one did not hold; the message names where to look - the SAS log above it, for an ERROR of the append or of the export after it - and not one cause. Measured for it: every job run on a ledger that existed showed two `PROC JSON` steps before the Python program and completed. Not measured in those runs: the refusal - a lock that CAS refuses was not provoked -, a first run of the flow without a ledger, the fallback for a table whose column types cannot be read, two runs at the same time, and a lock older than 30 minutes. Changed after these measurements: the job looks for the fresh lock of another run before it appends its own, and its lock row carries the id of the run (see the entry about a run of the job that failed). Measured with that job and the steps of this release, later on 2026-10-11 with pgvector: the job and the flow, each on a `CHAR` and on a `VARCHAR` ledger, logged the type they found, appended one row and completed; a first run of the flow without a ledger found its 3 documents new and completed; two runs at the same time and a lock older than 30 minutes are in the entries about a run of the job that failed and about the newest lock row. *RAG - List Documents* wrote *the run lock could not be written to … - refusing to ingest unguarded* once, for a ledger in a caslib of the CAS session, which cannot hold a promoted table (see the entry about a saved table that could not be looked for). Still not measured: the refusal of the job, which reads its lock back, and the fallback for a table whose column types cannot be read. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8 - the import brings the steps and the job, and manifesting the setup again writes the job into its job definition.
- **`RAG - Register Setup` creates a SAS Model Manager project that does not exist yet.** The step registers the retrieval model in the project it names, *RAG Engineering* unless changed, and creates that project when it is missing. With a new name it stopped with `POST /modelRepository/projects returned 400` and *You must specify a value for the "folderId" parameter* (seen on 2026-10-11): SAS Model Manager places a project in a folder, and `rag_core` named the repository only. Nobody had met it, because the project of the default name existed wherever the step had run. `rag_core` now creates the project in the default repository - the one SAS Model Manager marks as default among the first 100 it lists, else the first of them, where it used to take whichever came first on a page of one - and sends the `folderId` of that repository with the request; on that system the same request with the folder added was answered 201. A repository that names no folder ends in a message that says so and names the repository, and a project that exists is used as before, without a request that creates anything. To have the project in another repository, create it there first. Measured on 2026-10-11 with a project name that did not exist: the step ended green, the project was created in the repository marked as default (*Public* on that system) with the folder of that repository and the tags `LLM` and `RAG-Engineering`, and the retrieval model was registered in it. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2.
- **The retrieval model works on the question without the blanks a decision pads it with.** A decision hands a character input to a Python model padded with blanks to the length the variable is declared with: in a test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning a question declared with 1000 characters reached `retrieve_context.py` with about 900 trailing blanks, and in SAS Micro Analytic Service with one (seen on 2026-10-11). The model returned the padded text as `query` in `context_envelope` - 1134 characters for a short question - and sent it to the embedding model as it was. It now drops the blanks around the question before it embeds it and before it returns it. A question of blanks only is still answered with *question is empty*. A `filter_json` of blanks only is read as no filter; by the code it ended in `retrieval failed: JSONDecodeError`, which was not seen in that test because the model had stopped earlier, at the credentials of the vector store. The other inputs are read the same way: a `retrieval_mode` of blanks only is the default mode - it was taken for the name of a mode, and the status read *ok (retrieval_mode  not available yet - used vector)* - and a `k` of blanks only is the default number of chunks, where it failed the call with *could not convert string to float*. A `k` that is no number falls back to the default as well and says so in `retrieval_status`, *ok (k … is not a number - used 4)* - a missing number and an infinity, also as the texts `nan` and `inf`, are the default without that note; the notes quote at most 40 characters of what was sent, so that the status stays within the 512 characters it is registered with. Checked with the test suite, in which the two renderers of the model still write the same text, and measured on 2026-10-11 with the model as this release manifests it: a question followed by 200 blanks arrived as 56 characters in `query` in SAS Micro Analytic Service, and in the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning the three questions arrived with 103, 91 and 56 characters. A blank `retrieval_mode`, a `k` of 0, a missing `k` and a blank `filter_json` each gave `retrieval_status` *ok*, in single calls of SAS Micro Analytic Service and in the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning; the collection has three chunks, so the default number of chunks cannot be told from 3 in those runs. Not measured in a decision: a `k` that is no number. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8, then publish the decisions that use the model again.
- **The summary of a RAG step no longer stands in a quoted string, and *RAG - Register Setup* prints it without the NOTE about a quoted string of more than 262 bytes.** Every run of *RAG - Register Setup* wrote *NOTE: The quoted string currently being processed has become more than 262 bytes long. You might have unbalanced quotation marks.* twice, above the summary of its log (seen on 2026-10-11, with its checkbox ticked and not ticked). The cause was read from the code. Each of the nine RAG steps wrote its summary into the program inside two quoted strings, and SAS writes that NOTE for a quoted string that is longer than 262 bytes once its text has been put in. The two strings have stood in all nine steps since 2.0.0, and the shortest summary *RAG - Register Setup* can write was 339 bytes before the checkbox of this release; the WARNING lines of the checkbox are strings of at most 200 bytes each. The steps now read the summary with `symget`. The step file held a second pair of strings of that length - two docstrings of its Python program, of 362 and 438 bytes, the first of them new with the checkbox. Nothing in the code suggests that SAS counts those: *RAG - List Documents* could not run if SAS paired quotation marks across the lines of its Python, and no line of a Python program in the steps is longer than 95 bytes. They were cut to under 262 bytes all the same, in all nine steps. The ERROR line of a step that fails and the closing line of `Ingest-Documents.sas` held their text in a quoted string as well; see the entry about the ERROR line. Every RAG step names the change in its *About* tab, under version 1.3 - 1.4 for *RAG - Embed Chunks*, 1.1 for *RAG - Enrich Chunks* - which is where SAS Studio shows which step a site has. Measured on 2026-10-11 for *RAG - Register Setup*: the log of a run of the new step goes from `%_rgrg_status;` straight to *NOTE: ----- RAG - Register Setup -----*, with 0 errors and 0 warnings, and the two NOTE lines every earlier run had are gone; which of the two changes removed them was not separated. The other eight steps got the same change. Measured later the same day with SingleStore: the log of a flow of *List*, *Extract*, *Chunk*, *Embed* and *Load* holds no such NOTE. The logs of *RAG - Enrich Chunks*, *RAG - Purge Documents* and *RAG - Retrieve Context* were not read for it. The *About* tab of each step says what was measured for it. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2.
- **The run history of a RAG ingestion is recorded again.** A run counts the documents the deployment excludes - a source file with code ingestion off - and writes that count to `rag_runs` as `docs_excluded`, but the column was missing from the table definition and from the migrations that widen an existing table. By the code, every write to `rag_runs` therefore failed on an unknown column - in the flow and in the job, for pgvector and for SingleStore, on a new store and on an upgraded one: the run logged `rag history: not recorded (...) - the load itself is unaffected`, `rag_runs` gained no rows and `rag_doc_events` kept receiving events for runs that have no run row. On PostgreSQL the failed statement also left the transaction of the connection aborted, so the read with which *RAG - Load Vector Store* publishes `<project>_RUNS` and `<project>_DOC_EVENTS` to CAS was refused as well (`history not published to CAS: ...`) - the *Seeing what happened* tables of the user guide and every cost figure built on them. The column is now created with the table and added to an existing `rag_runs` by the next run (earlier rows read 0), and `<project>_RUNS` gains it as a measure. A run now reads which columns `rag_runs` has (`information_schema.columns`, in the schema the connection writes to) and adds only the missing ones, where it used to try every `ALTER TABLE` on every run and ignore the failures; a store that refuses to add a missing column is therefore named in the log (`rag history: not recorded (... must be owner of table rag_runs`) and nothing is written for that run, instead of document events without a run row. A column that a concurrent run added in between is not an error, and where the store does not say which columns the table has, every `ALTER TABLE` is tried as before. A history write that fails is rolled back where it fails, so a store that refuses one no longer takes the CAS publish that follows down with it. The runs made while this was broken are not backfilled: their document events are in `rag_doc_events`, their run rows do not exist. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with pgvector and with SingleStore 9.0.40: every run wrote its row, and *rag history: not recorded* stood in no log; on SingleStore `rag_runs` had 34 columns before the first run and 35, with `docs_excluded`, after it; and with one source-code file in the documents folder and code ingestion off, `docs_excluded` was 1 for the two runs that followed and 0 for the 17 before them (pgvector). The column stays: `rag_runs` is shared by every collection in the database, and the accelerator does not take it out again. Not measured: a store whose user may not alter `rag_runs`, and a store that does not say which columns a table has; the tests run both against a stand-in for the connection that keeps the columns of the tables, refuses an unknown column and a second `ADD COLUMN`, and refuses every statement after a failed one until it is rolled back. Two limits of the run history were seen in the same runs: a run of `Ingest-Documents.sas` wrote its row to the store and did not publish `<project>_RUNS` and `<project>_DOC_EVENTS` - only *RAG - Load Vector Store* did, so the CAS copy showed a job run after the next run of the flow; the job publishes both since, see *Changed* -, and *RAG - Purge Documents* writes no row to the run history, which this release does not change. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 10.
- **A saved ledger survives a restart of the CAS server.** The RAG pipeline promotes *and saves* `<project>_LEDGER` and `<project>_EMBEDDED`, but every reader looked only at the in-memory table and nothing loaded the saved one back. After a restart of the CAS server - or for any ledger that was simply never loaded again - *RAG - List Documents* found no ledger, started one that held only its run lock, classified the whole corpus as new, every chunk went through the enrichment prompts and the embedding model again, and the saved ledger was overwritten with the result; a List step that failed in that state saved an empty ledger over it. *RAG - List Documents*, *RAG - Purge Documents* and *RAG - Register Setup* now load a saved ledger, *RAG - Embed Chunks* and *RAG - Enrich Chunks* the saved embedded chunks, *RAG - Load Vector Store* both, and `Ingest-Documents.sas` its ledger - one macro, worded the same in all seven, that runs as soon as the CAS session is there. With the run lock that follows the column types of the ledger (see the entry about it) the order is: load the saved table back, ask it for its column types, append the lock, read the lock back. Each of the last three asks the in-memory table, and a ledger that came from its saved file holds its text columns the way its last writer left them. The log says `NOTE: RAG: <caslib>.<table> was saved but not in memory and has been loaded from its saved file.` A table that is in memory is never replaced by its saved copy, a project with no saved table starts as before, a load that a concurrent run got to first is not an error, and a saved file that cannot be loaded fails the step - *the saved table ... could not be loaded into memory (CAS messages above) - stopped, because an empty table would re-embed the corpus and overwrite the saved file* - before it takes a lock or starts a ledger; in the job the Python program then writes the milestone *Ingestion failed: …* and returns, and the job ends as failed. Where the files of the caslib cannot be listed, the run goes on with a WARNING (see the entry about it). The `.sashdat` is matched by name without regard to case, since the file keeps the case the project name was typed in and a CAS table name does not. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with pgvector, with the saved tables dropped from memory while their files stayed, and not with a restarted CAS server: the job and *RAG - List Documents* loaded a `CHAR` and a `VARCHAR` ledger back, appended the lock with the type of the table and found 3 documents unchanged; *RAG - Embed Chunks* loaded `<project>_EMBEDDED` back and embedded nothing. Not measured: a restart of the CAS server, a saved file that cannot be loaded, the reload in *RAG - Purge Documents*, *RAG - Register Setup* and *RAG - Enrich Chunks*, the reload of *RAG - Load Vector Store* itself (both tables were in memory by the time it ran), and SingleStore. The milestone and the failed state of the job were added after these measurements. Measured with the job and the steps of this release, later on 2026-10-11: with the ledger dropped from memory and its file kept, the job and *RAG - List Documents* wrote the NOTE, loaded the ledger and found every document unchanged. A saved file that cannot be loaded - the ERROR of the step, the milestone of the job and the untouched file - is still **not run in SAS**: no file that CAS cannot load could be put into a caslib of the test. The compute server did not see the directory of the caslib, CAS refused to save another format under a `.sashdat` name (`ERROR: Save exportoptions file type and name extension do not match.`), and the caslibs whose directories the compute server does see belong to others. That the job ends as failed was measured for other failures (see *Changed*), and what a run leaves behind that stops before it takes a lock for a run that another lock refused. For a caslib whose files cannot be listed see the entry about it. The six steps name the reload in their *About* tab, under the version of 11OCT2026. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **A job that *RAG - Register Setup* generates from a flow ends, and ends as failed, when a step fails.** The job the step creates was the code SAS Studio generates for the flow, unchanged, so a failing step ran `abort 42` as it does in an interactive flow. In a job that statement ends the session before SAS Job Execution gets its completion handshake - the reason the steps have the switch `RAG_ABORT_ON_ERROR` at all -, which leaves the job reported as running and its compute server counted against the limit of the context. The job now begins with `%let RAG_ABORT_ON_ERROR = 0;` and ends with a check that writes `ERROR: RAG ingestion failed - a step of this run reported an error (its ERROR line is above) and the steps after it were skipped.` and sets `SYSCC` when a step failed; without the second half the first would have turned a failed run into one that ends like a completed one. The check reads `RAG_JOB_FAILED`, which every failing step now sets and no step clears; the job sets it to 0 when it starts. `RAG_PIPELINE_FAILED`, the flag that makes the later steps of a chain skip, is no use for it: *RAG - List Documents*, *RAG - Purge Documents* and *RAG - Retrieve Context* clear it when they start, so a flow with a *RAG - Retrieve Context* node beside its ingestion chain, or with two chains, could reach its end with the flag at 0 after a step had failed. The check reads that flag as well, which is all a step deployed before this release sets. Both parts are added by *RAG - Register Setup* and are not part of the flow: a job that SAS Studio itself deploys from the flow does not have them, and running the step again puts them back. `Ingest-Documents.sas`, the single-program job, never aborted; it now ends with the same statement (see *Changed*, with what was measured for that program). Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with two jobs the step generated: with a failing *RAG - List Documents* the job ended as `failed` in SAS Job Execution after 31 seconds, with both ERROR lines in its log and the steps after it skipped; without a failure it ended as `completed`. Not measured: the job that does not end, which the change is made to prevent, was not reproduced; a step other than *RAG - List Documents* that fails; a flow with a second chain. Every RAG step names the flag in its *About* tab, and *RAG - Register Setup* what the job begins and ends with. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2, then *RAG - Register Setup* run again for every setup whose job it generated (step 8).
- **The RAG ingestion job and the retrieval test job say so when they could not start.** The Builder reads a run of the ingestion as failed from the state of the job or from the milestone `Ingestion failed: <reason>`. Two ways out of `Ingest-Documents.sas` left neither: a saved ledger that could not be loaded into memory, and a compute context without the `requests` package. The Python program returned before its first milestone, the job ended as completed, and the page said *Run finished. The ingestion ledger is below.* for a run that had done nothing; the reason stood only in the last line of the SAS log. Both now write the milestone, and the job ends as failed (see *Changed*). The retrieval test had the same gap: in a context without `requests`, `Test-Retrieval.sas` wrote a line without the word `FAILED`, and the page showed a probe that returned no chunks; it now says *The probe failed: …*. The page itself is unchanged. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 for the `requests` package - not in a compute context that lacks it, which the test system does not have, but in a compute session in which a module named `requests` that raises `ImportError` stood first on the path of Python. `Ingest-Documents.sas` wrote *NOTE: Python-Subprocess - RAGINGEST Ingestion failed: The Python environment of this compute context lacks the requests package - see the RAG administration guide.* and the same text behind *ERROR: RAG ingestion failed:*, and handed back the lock it had taken before the Python program started. `Test-Retrieval.sas` wrote *NOTE: Python-Subprocess - RAGRETRIEVE FAILED the Python environment of this compute context lacks the requests package - see the RAG administration guide*, and no `ROW` and no `DONE` line. Not run: the milestone for a saved ledger that cannot be loaded - no such file could be staged, see the entry about a saved ledger -, and the two texts of the page, which was not opened on a run that failed. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **The RAG ingestion job stores a ledger only for a run that went through.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` stored `rag_ledger_out.json` whenever the file existed in WORK. In a SAS session that had run the job before, a later run that stopped early - at a saved ledger that could not be loaded, at the lock of another run - found the file of the earlier run and stored that ledger again: over the saved file, and without the lock row of the other run. A job started by SAS Job Execution has a WORK of its own and was not affected. `rag_ledger_in.json` and `rag_ledger_lock.json`, from which the job reads the ledger and its lock, are deleted at the start of the next run of the session (see the entry on the exports of an earlier run). Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with the program run several times in one compute session: after runs that had gone through, a run that the lock of a running flow refused wrote nothing behind its Python program - no promote and no save of the ledger -, and the ledger, its saved file and the one lock row, the flow's, were as before. Not run: the same after a saved ledger that could not be loaded.
- **The RAG ingestion job reads no export of an earlier run.** The job exports the ledger to `rag_ledger_in.json` and, after its lock, to `rag_ledger_lock.json`, and both files stay in WORK. In a SAS session that had run the job before, a run that found no ledger in memory exported none, and its Python program read the two files of the earlier run: that ledger as the ledger of this run, and its lock as the lock that was written. Both files are now deleted before anything is exported, with *NOTE: RAG ingestion: rag_ledger_in.json of an earlier run of this SAS session was deleted from WORK* (and the same for `rag_ledger_lock.json`); a file that is found and cannot be deleted stops the run. A job started by SAS Job Execution has a WORK of its own and was not affected. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 in one compute session: after a run for one setup, a run for a second setup that had no ledger wrote the two NOTE lines, ran no `PROC JSON` step before its Python program, logged *ledger has 0 documents*, found its 3 documents new and ended with *RAG ingestion succeeded*. A line is written for each file that is there: both after a run that took a lock, the one for `rag_ledger_in.json` alone after a run that was refused, and none after a run that had no ledger. None of the 17 logs of the job in SAS Job Execution holds such a line. With `rag_ledger_in.json` replaced by a directory that held a file, the run ended with *ERROR: RAG ingestion failed: rag_ledger_in.json of an earlier run of this SAS session could not be deleted from WORK - stopped, because the run would read the ledger of that run as its own*, took no lock and stored nothing. One thing is not as it should be there, and is left as it is: the export that follows the delete still runs, so three ERROR messages of `PROC JSON` stand above the milestone of that run, on six log lines - *ERROR: Invalid file, …/rag_ledger_in.json.* and two about the JSON output. And by the code, not measured: where another run holds a fresh lock at that moment, the last line of the log names the lock and not the file. A file that cannot be deleted for another reason, a permission for one, was not provoked. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **No RAG step writes the NOTE about a quoted string above its ERROR line.** The ERROR line of a failed RAG step was `putLog "ERROR: <step> failed: %superq(…)"` with a message of up to 500 characters, and SAS reads a quoted string after the macro processor has put its text in. Seen on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09: a failing *RAG - List Documents* wrote *NOTE: The quoted string currently being processed has become more than 262 bytes long* directly above its ERROR line. The closing line of the RAG ingestion job, `Ingest-Documents.sas`, was written the same way, and its message for a run lock that was not read back is longer than 262 bytes with any table name. The nine steps and the job now put the line together in a variable, its text read with `symget`, as the summary already is. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09: a *RAG - List Documents* whose documents folder does not exist, run as a job and in a compute session, wrote an ERROR line of 311 characters, and the line above it was not the NOTE; no log of that day's runs of the steps and of the job holds the NOTE. A line written from a variable is still typed as an error, and so is what continues it: the log of that system is 132 characters wide, this line, which the step writes with `PUT`, was cut there - inside a word, and with no prefix on what follows - while a long NOTE milestone and the WARNING lines of the steps were broken at a blank before that column, and all three log lines of that message were of the type error, as were the four of a 413-character ERROR line of the job. SAS Studio was not opened: that it shows such a line as an error is read from the type of the lines.
- **The prompt optimization job writes its ERROR line without a quoted string of open length.** `Optimize-Prompt-DSPy.sas` wrote `putLog "ERROR: Prompt optimization failed: %superq(_opt_error)"` with a message of up to 500 characters - the form that made the RAG steps write the NOTE about a quoted string of more than 262 bytes above their ERROR line. The message is read with symget and the line is put together in a variable, as in the RAG runtime. The job definition holds a copy of the program: replace its code to get the change. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with a target model whose provider key the identity of the job does not hold: with the program of 2.2.0 in the job definition, the log of the failing run held the NOTE five lines above its ERROR line; with the program of this release it held *ERROR: Prompt optimization failed: The model gpt_41_mini needs an API key for provider OpenAI but the credential domain provided none …*, typed as an error like the line that continues it, and no NOTE about a quoted string. Upgrading from 2.2.x: step 2 - the job definition `Optimize-Prompt-DSPy` of the package holds the program of this release.
- **A RAG run that holds no lock of its own leaves the ledger alone.** A RAG step that fails hands the run lock back by rewriting the ledger without that row - copy, drop, promote, save. The macro did that for runs that held no lock as well: a run that the fresh lock of another run had refused (the saved file then held the lock row of the other run, which a ledger loaded back from its file brings into the next run), and a step that failed after *RAG - Load Vector Store* had already written the ledger. *RAG - Purge Documents* ran it after every run. Seen on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with pgvector and with SingleStore: a purge that had gone through, a preview and a failing *RAG - Register Setup* each rewrote the ledger and wrote *the run lock was released so the run can be retried*; the preview promoted and saved the ledger under a summary that says *PREVIEW - nothing was changed*. All eight steps that have the macro now ask the ledger for the lock row of their own run and do nothing without it, and the NOTE is written only when that row was taken out after a failure. *RAG - Purge Documents* hands a lock back only after a failure, like the other steps, and takes no lock for a preview: a preview is still refused while another run holds a fresh lock, it no longer starts a ledger for a project that has none, and an ingestion can start while it reads. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with pgvector, the flows run as the code SAS Studio generates for them. A flow started while another flow of the same ledger ran ended with *ERROR: RAG - List Documents failed: another ingestion run holds the lock on this ledger - wait for it to finish, or retry in 30 minutes if it crashed*; its log held no rewrite of the ledger and no line about a released lock, the ledger kept the lock row of the first flow, and that flow completed. A *RAG - List Documents* that failed with its own lock rewrote the ledger without that row and wrote the NOTE, on a `VARCHAR` and on a `CHAR` ledger, and the run after it was not refused. A preview of *RAG - Purge Documents* appended no lock, promoted and saved nothing, and the saved file of the ledger kept its size and time; under the lock of a running flow it was refused with the same message, and on a project without a ledger it failed with *RuntimeError: no ledger table for this RAG project - check the project name and caslib* and left no ledger behind. A purge that went through promoted and saved the ledger once and wrote no line about a released lock, and a *RAG - Register Setup* that failed after *RAG - Load Vector Store* left the ledger alone. Not measured: an ingestion that starts while a preview reads.
- **A run of the RAG ingestion job that failed, or that the lock of another run refused, no longer locks the ledger for 30 minutes.** `Ingest-Documents.sas` appended its lock row before its Python program looked for the lock of another run, and only a run that went through took the row out again, by storing the ledger. Every run that failed left a fresh lock row behind, a run that was refused left a second one beside the lock that refused it, and the next run was answered *another ingestion run appears active for this ledger (run lock held) - retry later* with nothing running - the retry of a run that this release shows as failed. The row could not be handed back either: its `run_id` was empty. The job now makes the run id in SAS, with the lines *RAG - List Documents* makes it with, writes it into the lock row and hands it to the Python program as the id of the run. It looks for a fresh lock of another run before it appends: a run that is refused appends nothing and leaves the ledger as it is. And a run that failed takes the lock row with its own id out of the ledger - copy, drop, promote, save, as the steps do - and writes *NOTE: RAG ingestion: the run lock was released so the run can be retried*. A lock row with an empty `run_id`, which a job definition from before this release still writes, is the lock of another run to every reader: it refuses while it is fresh, is ignored after 30 minutes and is never taken out by a run that failed. A refused run ends with the message without the prefix *RuntimeError:*, because SAS refuses it before the Python program runs. Manifest the setup again to get the job. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with the job run in SAS Job Execution in the *SAS Studio compute context* and pgvector. A run that failed after it had taken the lock - one parameter of the configuration was changed, so that it stopped at *RuntimeError: configuration drift: …* -, on a `CHAR` and on a `VARCHAR` ledger: the log held the append of the lock, the milestone, a table `<ledger>_R` with the rows the ledger had before the run, its promote and its save, *NOTE: RAG ingestion: the run lock was released so the run can be retried* and the ERROR line; the ledger then held the same document rows, no lock row and the column types it had; and the job, run again five seconds later, was not refused and completed. A run launched while the flow of the same ledger ran: no append and one export, the milestone *Ingestion failed: another ingestion run appears active for this ledger (run lock held) - retry later* and the same text in the ERROR line, without *RuntimeError:*; the ledger held one lock row, the flow's, and its saved file kept its size and time; the flow completed, and the job run again after it was not refused. Read from another session while a job ran, the lock row carried the id of the run - the id of its milestone and of its row in `rag_runs`. A lock row with an empty `run_id`, appended by hand as a job definition from before this release writes it: 60 seconds old it refused the job and the flow and was still the only lock row afterwards; older than 30 minutes it was ignored, was kept by a job run that failed (`<ledger>_R` held it beside the documents) and was gone after a run that went through. The line about a lock that is ignored is worded by its writer: the job logs *RAGINGEST stale run lock ignored (held > 30 minutes)*, the steps *RAG - List Documents: a stale run lock (held longer than 30 minutes) is ignored* and *RAGLIST stale run lock ignored (held longer than 30 minutes)*; a job run that fails at the configuration drift does not write it, because the drift is looked at first. Not measured: a lock row left by a job definition of an earlier release that really failed. A limit, read from the code and not measured: a lock row carries the time of the SAS clock, and the Python programs of the job and of the steps compare it with their own clock, which is UTC. In a compute session whose SAS clock is not UTC (the option `TIMEZONE`) the two differ by the offset, and east of UTC the Python program of the job still takes for fresh a lock row that SAS took for stale: that run has taken its own lock, hands it back, and its message carries *RuntimeError:*. The sessions of the test system run in UTC. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **The newest lock row decides whether a RAG ledger is locked.** A run that crashes leaves its lock row in the ledger until a run that goes through writes the ledger; after 30 minutes the next run ignores it and appends its own beside it. Whoever then looked at the ledger found two lock rows and took one: the lock macro of *RAG - List Documents* and *RAG - Purge Documents* the row its DATA step read last, the Python of *RAG - List Documents*, *RAG - Load Vector Store*, *RAG - Purge Documents* and of the ingestion job the first row CAS returned. With the stale row in that place the lock of the run at work was not seen. All six now take the newest row. A lock row whose time cannot be read is no lock in the macro and a stale one in the Python, as before. No such ledger had come about on a live system by itself; the case is read from the code. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with ledgers made for it - a lock row older than 30 minutes and a fresh one with the id of another run, appended by hand in either order: the job and *RAG - List Documents* were refused both ways, a preview of *RAG - Purge Documents* with the old row appended first, and both rows were in the ledger afterwards. With a flow that really ran beside an old row: the flow ignored the old row and took its lock, and the job and a second flow launched while it ran were refused. CAS does not fix the order of the rows of a table; what was varied is the order of the two appends. Upgrading from 2.2.x: steps 2 and 8.
- **A saved table of the RAG pipeline that could not be looked for is a WARNING.** When the files of the caslib cannot be listed, or PROC CAS ends before it says what it found, the reload of a saved table in the RAG steps and in the ingestion job wrote *NOTE: RAG: could not look for a saved copy of … - the run continues with what is in memory* and the run went on - with no table in memory, which is the case the reload exists for: every document counted as new, the corpus embedded again, the saved file overwritten. The run still goes on, and says so in two WARNING messages: *WARNING: RAG: <caslib>.<table> is not in memory, and the files of the caslib <caslib> could not be listed (CAS messages above) - a saved copy could not be looked for.* and *WARNING: RAG: the run continues without the table and saves over a copy that may be there. A ledger that is not in memory means that every document counts as new and is embedded again.* Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 for the wording, in *RAG - List Documents*: with the ledger in a caslib of the CAS session whose files CAS could not list, the log held the two WARNING messages word for word, on four log lines below the CAS errors. That the run goes on to its end was not measured: the step went on to its run lock, and a caslib of a session cannot hold the promoted table the lock is appended to, so the run ended with *ERROR: RAG - List Documents failed: the run lock could not be written to … - refusing to ingest unguarded*. A global caslib whose files cannot be listed could not be made - the user of the test may add no path caslib. Not run: the two lines in the job and in the other steps, and the line for a `PROC CAS` that ends early.
- **The run id of a RAG run tells two sessions apart.** The id is `run-<epoch>-<digits>`, and `rag_runs` is keyed on it and shared by every project in the database. The digits were `&sysjobid` in the RAG steps and the process id in the ingestion job. Seen on 2026-10-11: twelve flow runs, each in a compute server of its own, ended in `-344` (the four other runs in `-343` or `-345`), and the job runs in `-400` to `-408` - so two runs that started in the same second could get the same id. No collision was measured. The steps and the job now take ten digits from a UUID, in SAS (`&sysjobid` where UUIDGEN returns none); the Python program of the job makes ten random digits only where SAS hands it no id; the id keeps its form and fits the 32 characters of the ledger column, and ids written before are read as before. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09: the 28 ids in the logs of the flows, the purges and the job of one pass, and the 15 of the runs of the job in another - 8 in SAS Job Execution, 7 in compute sessions -, each ended in ten digits, with no two endings alike and none of three digits; the ledger held the 25 characters of the id, and `rag_runs.started_at` was the time the id names. A limit, read from the code and not measured: the epoch in the id is the time of the SAS clock - in the steps as before, and now in the job, whose Python program made the id from its own clock up to 2.2.x - and `started_at` is read from the id as UTC. In a compute session whose SAS clock is not UTC (the option `TIMEZONE`), `started_at` of a job run is therefore off by the offset to UTC, as that of a flow run is; the sessions of the test system run in UTC.
- **Smaller corrections in the logs of the RAG steps.** A skipped *RAG - Load Vector Store* wrote *WARNING: Apparent symbolic reference _RGLD_NRUNS not resolved* and the same for `_RGLD_NEVENTS` (seen on 2026-10-11 behind a failed *RAG - List Documents*); the two counts now have a start value. *RAG - Extract Text* counted every document it did not extract as *skipped as unchanged* - `4 skipped as unchanged` where *RAG - List Documents* of the same run said `excluded=1, unchanged=3` (seen on 2026-10-11); it now writes `3 skipped as unchanged, 1 excluded`.
