A part of a step's summary that is longer than the log line was printed as an empty `NOTE:` line followed by the text without the prefix (seen in *RAG - Register Setup*); it is written with its prefix. *RAG - Register Setup* said *the tag TLS-Verification-Off was not updated* and, two lines on, that the run had written that tag, for an upload without a usable answer on a model whose tag was written unseen; it now says *was not updated after the upload*, as `rag_core` does. A new document in which the extractor found no text - an empty file, a PDF that holds only scans - was still counted under *documents extracted* and under *extractors used*, although `rag_core` hands it back as skipped; it is counted as skipped: `0 documents extracted, 0 failed, 3 skipped as unchanged, 1 skipped` for three unchanged documents and one empty file, with no line *extractors used*. Measured on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09 with pgvector, in flows run as the code SAS Studio generates for them. A *RAG - Load Vector Store* that was skipped behind a failed *RAG - List Documents* wrote neither WARNING, and that log held no WARNING line at all. *RAG - Extract Text* wrote `0 documents extracted, 0 failed, 3 skipped as unchanged, 1 excluded` for three unchanged documents and one code file. *RAG - Register Setup* without a flow wrote the list of its artifacts with the prefix, continued in the next log line, and no empty `NOTE:` line. With an empty file beside three unchanged documents, *RAG - Extract Text* logged `rag extract: 0 extracted, 1 skipped, 0 failed` and `rag skipped: 1 - plaintext found no text (empty or scanned-only document)` and no line *extractors used*; the summary of that run read `0 documents extracted, 0 failed, 3 skipped as unchanged, 1 deleted, 1 skipped`, because a file had been taken out of the folder before it, and with one more new document the summary read `1 documents extracted, 0 failed, 3 skipped as unchanged, 1 skipped` with `extractors used: plaintext=1`. Not run: the line *was not updated after the upload* - it needs a model that SAS Model Manager does not return and an upload without a usable answer, faults of the service that could not be staged on the test system.
- **The log parser no longer fails on a log without requests, and replacing no longer costs the loaded table.** An hour without traffic leaves a log that holds no request - the collection script of the guide produces one every such hour. The log parser then built a table without columns, stopped with `KeyError: 'timestamp'`, and its SAS code went on regardless: in the default replace mode it dropped the promoted `LLM_LOGS` before finding nothing to put in its place, so the usage report was empty until somebody reloaded the table. `Log-Parser-Code.sas` and the *LLM - Log Parser* step now always describe the twelve columns, report a log without requests with a note and leave the table as it is, and leave it as it is with an error when the log can not be parsed at all. Replacing builds the new table as `<table>_NEW` beside the existing one and drops the old table only once the new one exists; the staging table in `CASUSER` is uploaded with `replace`, so a leftover of an interrupted run can not get in the way. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11: a file of 0 bytes and a file with start-up lines only ended with the note in replace and in append mode, the table kept its 174 rows and its saved file was not written. Measured later that day for a log file that is missing, in replace mode: the run ended with `ERROR: The log could not be parsed, the table … was left as it is.` below the error of Python, `SYSCC` was 1012 and the job ended as failed - no table of that name existed before the run, so that an existing table is left as it is was not seen there. A replace that can not build `<table>_NEW` was not run.
- **Appending in the log parser no longer saves the rows of one log over the saved table.** In append mode the log parser asked CAS whether the output table exists, which only reports tables in memory. After a restart of the CAS server - or in every new CAS session when the table is not promoted - `LLM_LOGS` was on disk only, so the log parser started a new table from the rows of the current log and saved it over `LLM_LOGS.sashdat`: the rows of every run before were gone and the SAS log showed no error. `Log-Parser-Code.sas` and the *LLM - Log Parser* step now look for the saved file when the table is not in memory, load it, append to it and then promote and save as configured. A saved file that can not be loaded ends the run with an error and is not overwritten. Replace mode is unchanged. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11: with the table dropped from memory and its `.sashdat` in place, an append loaded the saved table, added the 7 requests of the new log to its 195 rows and promoted and saved it again. A saved file that can not be loaded was not run.
- **The log parser keeps what a decision model was asked and what it decided.** A call to a decision model was parsed into a row with tokens and run time only. Its questions now arrive in `system_prompt`, its state in `user_prompt` and the answers - the JSON with every answer, its confidence and its probabilities - in `response`.
- **The log parser keeps the tokens of an embedding call.** The containers of embedding models log `tokens:` where the others log `prompt_length:`, so every embedding row had an empty `prompt_length` and per-token priced embedding models could not be costed from `LLM_LOGS`. The tokens are now read into `prompt_length`. The embedded text is deliberately not copied into the table - one run that embeds a document collection would put all of its text there.
- **In the log parser a request ends where its response is logged, and only log lines count.** The log parser took any line that contained `Request: POST`, `run_time:` and so on, so a prompt that quoted such a line split one request into two rows, and the lines at the top of the next container's log - cut off in the middle of a request by the collection interval - were added to the last request of the container before. Lines are now matched from their start (`<timestamp> - <logger> - <level> - `), a request ends at its `Response: Status Code` line - or, when the container's part of the file ends before that line was logged, at the first line that is older than the request, which is where the next container begins - and a logged value that is not a number (`prompt_length: None`) or an exponent (`run_time: 2e-05`) is read correctly instead of stopping the run or being cut at the `e`. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11: a request that was still running where its container's part of the file ended gave one row with its prompts, and the numbers of another container's finished request that followed in the file were not added to it.
- **The log parser says so when it finds requests it can not read.** Because only lines that start as a log line count (see the entry above), a log whose lines carry a prefix - the timestamp of `kubectl logs --timestamps`, the pod name of `--prefix`, a JSON wrapper of a log pipeline - yields no rows, and with the note for a log without requests, which is new in this release as well, such a log would pass as an hour without traffic. The log parser therefore writes a warning that names the number of `Request: POST` lines it found in lines of another shape and the line start it expects. No release reported such a log as one without requests: the parser of 2.2.0 matched `Request: POST` anywhere in a line. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-11 with a log collected with `--timestamps`: the warning named its 26 lines and the table was left as it is. What this entry fixes of 2.2.0 is one thing: a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is replaced instead of failing the whole file, which cost that hour's requests; a log with such a byte was not among the measured ones.
- **The *LLM - Log Parser* step no longer takes the name that an ended CAS session left behind for a CAS session.** The step started a CAS session of its own (`cas llmLogMySess;`) only where the macro variable `_CASNAME_` did not exist, and ended that session as its last action. A session that is ended leaves its name behind. Seen on 2026-10-11 on SAS Viya 2026.09, with the code SAS Studio generated for two flows of the step, run one after the other in one SAS Job Execution job whose session had no CAS session: the first run started `LLMLOGMYSESS`, wrote its table and ended the session; after it `_CASNAME_` existed and the option `SESSREF` named `LLMLOGMYSESS`, while `cas _all_ list;` listed no session. The second run found `_CASNAME_`, started no session and wrote `ERROR: There is no server connection to execute the action 'session.sessionId'.`, `ERROR: Unhandled Python exception.` (with `Session reconnect failed: Could not find the specified session.`), `ERROR: The log could not be parsed, the table <caslib>.<table> was left as it is.` and `WARNING: Attempt to delete macro variable _CASSESSIONUUID failed. Variable not found.`; no table was written, `SYSCC` was 1012 and the job ended as failed. What was run is one job with two runs in it; the step was not clicked twice in SAS Studio. The step and `Log-Parser-Code.sas` now ask the `SESSFOUND` function whether the session that the option `SESSREF` names is connected - `%if %sysfunc(sessfound(%sysfunc(getoption(sessref)))) NE 1 %then %do;` - and start their own session when it is not. The option names the session the CAS actions of the step run in, and it goes on naming a session that was ended: SAS wrote `NOTE: The default CAS session LLMLOGMYSESS identified by SAS option SESSREF= was terminated. Use the OPTIONS statement to set the SESSREF= option to an active session.` after the step had ended its session. A connected CAS session of the user is used as before and is not ended: the one `terminate` of the program names `llmLogMySess` and runs only after the step started that session. One thing differs beside the fix, by the code: where the option names a session that is not connected while another session of the user is, the step starts and ends its own and leaves the other alone; it started none there and failed. After a run with a session of its own the option still names `LLMLOGMYSESS`, as before: statements of your own that follow the step in the same SAS session and need CAS have to start a session or set `SESSREF` to one. The step of 2.2.0 holds the old check. Measured later that day on the same system, with the check that asks `SESSFOUND` and the step as it was then (version 2.1, twelve columns), in seven more SAS Job Execution jobs: `SESSFOUND` returned 0 in a session without a CAS session, 0 for a session that had been ended and 1 for a connected one, and no log held an ERROR or a WARNING at a line that calls it; the step run twice and three times in one SAS session without a CAS session started and ended `LLMLOGMYSESS` in every run and wrote its table each time; behind a CAS session of the user it ran in that session and wrote no `Deletion of the session` and no `Request to TERMINATE` line, and afterwards the session had the same id, was listed as ACTIVE and still held the session table of the user; behind a session that had been ended it started and ended its own. All seven jobs ended as `completed` with `SYSCC` 0 and without a line of the type error or warning from source line 1 on, and each table, read from a new CAS session, was global with the 174 rows. `Log-Parser-Code.sas` with the same line, run in one of the seven jobs, used the `mysess` it starts and wrote the 174 rows. An observation of those runs, not caused by the check: after the step or the program had run in a CAS session of the user, the action `session.sessionId` returned that session under the name `py-session-1` where it had returned `MYSESS` before, with the same id, while SAS went on listing the session as `MYSESS`, ACTIVE. Not measured: the step clicked in the SAS Studio UI, a session of the step's own or of the user in append mode, and the condition code SAS Job Execution keeps for a job (the job API returned none, `SYSCC` 0 was written by the jobs). The tests of `Logging-Monitoring` take the condition from the program and decide it for the three states the job logs showed. The step reaches a site with the transfer package (step 2 of the upgrade); from a checkout, `mdb content-deploy` updates the registered step in place with a `PUT`, which keeps its id.
- **The *LLM - Log Parser* step and the README name the columns the log parser writes.** The step announced the output columns `llm`, `systemPrompt`, `userPrompt` and `run_time` to a flow, and the README of `Logging-Monitoring` listed the same names; the table has always had `model`, `system_prompt`, `user_prompt` and `runtime`. Both are corrected, and the README says what each column holds for an LLM, a decision model and an embedding model.
- **In the log parser a request that failed is a failed row, with its error.** A request whose model raises an exception writes no `Response:` line: the container logs the traceback and `Error occurred in the model scoring function`. Such a request gave a row without numbers and response that nothing told apart from a request still running. It now ends at that line and has `failed` = 1, no `status_code` and the first ERROR line in `error_text` - for the line the container writes for an exception, with the last line of the traceback added. In the log of 1040 requests named under *Added*, the 253 requests without a `Response:` line were all of this kind: 251 with an `HTTP 401` that the model logged and 2 with a `KeyError`. On the test system on 2026-10-11 the table the program wrote held these 253 rows with `failed` = 1 and their error texts.
- **In the log parser the options `max_completion_tokens` and `max_length` fill `max_tokens`, and options sent as JSON are read.** A request that named the limit of new tokens `max_length` or `max_completion_tokens` left `max_tokens` empty (5 of the 1040 requests of the log named under *Added*). `max_tokens` is taken first, then `max_completion_tokens`, then `max_length`. The options are read as JSON first and as `name:value` pairs otherwise, as the models read them; before, options in JSON filled no option column. On the test system on 2026-10-11 the table of the program and the table the parser wrote for the same file before it had the further columns differed in `max_tokens` of these 5 rows, and in nothing else of the twelve columns.
- **In the log parser a request is given only what the log decides.** The first lines of a request, the lines the models log for their numbers and their answer, and the `Response:` line name no request; the lines of the `transaction_<id>` logger and the `Response Data:` line do. The parser gave a line that names no request to the newest request, so two requests of one container that ran at the same time got each other's numbers and response. Now the first line of a transaction belongs to the one request of its model that has all its first lines and no transaction yet, the lines a model logs for its numbers and its answer and the `Response:` line are the lines of the one transaction that is running, and a line the log leaves two requests for is given to neither: the values stay missing, `failed` stays missing and is never 1 for that reason, and the SAS log has a NOTE with the number of such requests. A request line for another model no longer ends a request that is running. The lines of each container have to stay together in the file all the same: in a file that orders the lines of all containers by their time, a request that ran while another container was answering loses its numbers and its response, and the NOTE counts it. No collected log has shown two requests at the same time (0 in 1040 + 207 requests of six collections), so the rules are read from the line format. On 2026-10-12 they ran on the test system on files put together by hand from blocks of a collected log: two requests of one model with their first lines interleaved gave two rows with their prompts, without a status and with `failed` missing, and the NOTE; two requests of two models gave two rows with status 200 and `failed` = 0, the first without its numbers, and the NOTE. That holds for a log whose times do not go back inside the lines of one container: what the parser does where they go back by a second, and where a part of the file ends, was changed after that run - see the entry about a log line that is one second older, below.
- **In `PROMPT_EXPERIMENTS` a run without a system prompt showed the prompts of the run before.** `Get-All-Prompts.sas` took a row of the tracker as the header of a run only when its system prompt was not empty, so the model rows of a run that has none carried the system prompt, the user prompt, the judge and the counts of the run before it. The header of a run is now the row with its run id and an empty model. Covered by the tests; no such run was looked at when the program ran on the test system on 2026-10-11.
- **The *LLM - Get All Prompts* step wrote a table the *LLM Usage Report* could not read in full.** The step held the program of an earlier release: its table had 26 columns - no `cost`, no judge column, no count of variables, and of the options only `temperature`, `top_p`, `top_k` and `max_tokens` - and lacked 12 of the 38 columns the report reads. The step now runs the same core as `Get-All-Prompts.sas`, and the tests fail while the two differ. On the test system on 2026-10-11 the step of that day (version 2.0), run as the code SAS Studio generated for a flow, wrote the table with 61 columns, the 38 the report reads among them.
- **In the log parser, lines of a response that start like log lines no longer make a request of their own.** A response that quoted a request line and the lines behind it - a model asked about a log - gave a row for a request that never was, and the request it belonged to lost what followed. While a request is the only one that is running, the lines of its response up to the next line of its transaction are now text, whatever they start with. Two cases are not told apart from a request: a response that holds a request line with a `Request Headers:` line directly behind it, each at the start of a line, and a response that holds a request line with no further line that starts like a log line behind it. Both are still read as a request, and one more row is written. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12: a response with a `prompt_length:` line and a request line in its middle, and one more line of that shape behind them, gave one row with the whole response; a response whose third of four lines was a request line, with a line of text behind it, gave 2 rows - the request with its response cut in front of the request line, and a row with the model of the quoted line and nothing else - and the NOTE. The second case was not named before that run. Since then a request whose response quotes lines of an earlier or a later time has no status and `failed` missing, where it had `failed` = 1. On the hand-made file of that run the parser of later that day gave the same two rows, with the NOTE ending *failed is missing for 1 request(s).*
- **In the log parser one request no longer stops the file.** A request body with half of a surrogate pair in a prompt - a character that has no UTF-8 bytes - stopped the upload of all rows. It is now a question mark in the table, the way its hash counts it, and a NOTE counts such requests. A `Request Data:` line that can not be read keeps its row with what was read, with a NOTE; options nested deeper than Python decodes are kept and read as names and values, without one. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12: a request whose user prompt held half of a surrogate pair was in the table with a question mark in its place, and the NOTE counted 1 request; a request with options of 100000 opening brackets was in the table with its options as they were sent and status 200.
- **`Log-Parser-Code.sas` names the table in its message for a table that is not in CAS.** The message printed the name of a macro variable that only the custom step sets. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12: `ERROR: The table casuser.<table> is not a CAS table.`, with the names of the run. The run did not end there - see the entry about a refusal of the log parser, below.
- **`Log-Parser-Code.sas` and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` run one after the other in one SAS session.** Both started `mysess` whatever the session had: the second of them wrote `WARNING: A session with the name MYSESS already exists.`, and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` ended the session at its end - the one the librefs of the parser were bound to. Seen on the test system on 2026-10-11 with the parser, the pull and the link in one job. Both code files now ask the `SESSFOUND` function, as the steps do, and start a session only when neither the session the option `SESSREF` names nor one of their own name is connected; `Get-All-Prompts.sas` ends only a session it started, and `Log-Parser-Code.sas` leaves its session and its librefs, as before. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-12: parser, pull, parser, pull, link and link in one job wrote no `already exists` line and no error on the job, one session was connected after every program, and the librefs of the parser were usable; alone in a fresh session the pull and the link each started a session and ended it; and behind a CAS session of the user none of the three started one of its own.
- **A run of the log parser, of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` or of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` that is refused no longer leaves its job completed.** On the test system on 2026-10-12 `Get-All-Prompts.sas` refused a table name of 34 characters with two ERROR lines and `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` a table of twelve columns with one, both with `SYSCC` 0: SAS Job Execution reported the jobs as completed, so a schedule saw no failure. The three programs and the steps *LLM - Log Parser* (2.4) and *LLM - Get All Prompts* (2.2) now set `SYSCC` to 1012 as their last statement in front of the macro variables they delete, for every run that wrote an ERROR line and no table. Parser: a file that is not on the SAS server, a table that is no CAS table, a log that could not be parsed, a saved table that could not be loaded, a new table that could not be created. `Get-All-Prompts.sas`: experiments that could not be read - also a table name that was refused -, a table that was not written, and in the step an output library that is no CAS library. `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas`: a log table or a catalog that was not found, a table the program refused, rows that could not be linked, a new table that could not be put in place. A WARNING, a NOTE, a log without requests, a run with nothing to link and a setup that is left out leave the condition code as it was. Measured on the test system later on 2026-10-12, in SAS Job Execution jobs: each of eight refusals ended with `SYSCC` 1012 and its job as failed - the parser with a table that is no CAS table, a path without `sasserver:`, a file that is missing and a refused append, the pull with a table name of 34 characters and its step with an output table in WORK, the link with a table of twelve columns and with a catalog that is missing. A run with a WARNING only, a log without requests and a run with nothing to link ended as completed with `SYSCC` 0. A refusal behaves like any SAS error: `%put` and PROC CAS behind the program ran, and the first DATA step behind it in the same SAS session wrote *NOTE: Due to ERROR(s) above, SAS set option OBS=0, enabling syntax check mode.* and did not run. With `%let syscc = 0;` behind the program that DATA step ran and the job ended as completed - so a job that runs a second program behind a refused one has to reset `SYSCC`, and then no longer ends as failed. In the code SAS Studio generates for a flow, the first DATA step behind the step is the flow's own `%_flw_action_end`: the log of the refused pull step held `_FLW_ACTION_START_` and neither `_FLW_ACTION_TABLE_` nor `_FLW_ACTION_END_`. What SAS Studio shows for such a node was not looked at, and no schedule was run. The *About* tabs of both steps, the comment in front of the statement and the README said that such a run keeps a flow, a job or a schedule from ending as completed; since versions 2.5 and 2.3 of the steps they say what was measured, and no statement changed.
- **The log parser ends a refusal with its one line.** With `_log_target_table_engine` other than `CAS` the program wrote two ERROR lines and an ABORT, SAS went on in syntax check mode, and a fourth line said that the log could not be parsed - about a log that was never read (test system, 2026-10-12; no table was written and the job ended as failed). The two checks now set a macro variable in place of the ABORT: the Python reads no log, and the macro of the output table returns before it says or drops anything. The line is `ERROR: The table <lib>.<name> is not a CAS table. For this step to run the target table needs to be in CAS, no log was read.` The step *LLM - Get All Prompts* says an output library that is no CAS library the same way, in one line without an ABORT. Measured on the test system later that day: with `_log_target_table_engine = V9` that one ERROR line and no other line of the program, no table, `SYSCC` 1012 and a failed job; and for the step *LLM - Get All Prompts* in the code of a flow with its output table in WORK, *ERROR: The selected output library does not point to a CASlib, nothing was read or written.*, no ABORT line and no request to SAS Model Manager.
- **One function for every text the Python of the log parser, of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` and of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` hands to SAS.** The function of `Get-All-Prompts.sas` let both marks of a comment, tabs and other control characters and a parenthesis without its partner through, in messages that carry names from SAS Model Manager - a model, a project, a setup, a file, the reason of an error -, and three messages of the log parser went to `SAS.logMessage` as they were. What SAS does with such a name in a message was never run; on 2026-10-12 the NOTEs of the parser arrived whole. The three programs and both steps now carry the function of `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` word for word, and each has one place that writes to the SAS log and one that sets a macro variable, both through it. A quote in a message of the parser shows as a backtick. Measured on the test system later that day with models whose names held the start of a comment, quotes, a semicolon, a percent sign and `%put`: the WARNINGs of the pull arrived whole, with the comment mark split, the quotes as backticks, `%` as `pct` and the semicolon as a comma, the closing NOTEs followed with `SYSCC` 0, and the tables held the names unchanged; the NOTE of the link program about two models of one such name arrived the same way, and the NOTEs of the parser whole with their backticks. SAS Model Manager refused a model name with a parenthesis without its partner (HTTP 400), so that such a parenthesis shows as a bracket was not measured.
- **In the log parser a log line that is one second older than a request line in front of it ends no request, and a request the lines of its container end on is not called failed.** A line is stamped when it is made and written a moment later. Such a line was taken for the first line of the next container, and everything that was running was dropped: an answered request became `failed` = 1, at two places without a NOTE, and a late `Response Data:` line behind the next request line cost that request every value. And on the test system on 2026-10-12, files made by hand whose times were not in file order gave `failed` = 1 for three answered requests without a NOTE, and one request the token count, the run time and the response of another. The next part of a file now begins where the time goes back by more than one second. A line that is older by a second is the line of its transaction when it names one; a line the models log for their numbers or a `Response:` line there is given to no request, and the requests running then are counted in the NOTE. A transaction is over at a request line only when every later line that names it is more than a second older. A request that was running where the lines of its container end - at the end of a part or of the file - has `failed` missing and is counted in a NOTE of its own, where it was `failed` = 1 without a word; so has a request whose first lines broke off at the request line of such a request. A request without a response whose container went on stays `failed` = 1. That a container writes a line at most one second late is read from the line format: no collected log holds requests that ran at the same time. Measured on the test system later that day, on the files made by hand for the second run and on three new ones put together from two real blocks: a request that was running where the lines of its container end had `failed` missing and was counted in its NOTE, two requests that ran at the same time gave one complete row and one without a status, with the NOTE that counts them, and two blocks one behind the other gave both requests their status 200 and their own numbers without a NOTE. A reading of the parser after that found three limits the made-up logs below do not reach - a line that is two or more seconds older between the first lines of two requests of one model, a part that ends on a running request in front of a part that starts later than that request did, and a part whose reading depends on the parts in front of it: see *Requests that run at the same time* in *Logging & Monitoring* of the Administration Guide. Outside of SAS the collected log of 1040 requests parses to the same 1040 rows in all 36 columns, the two files of 2026-10-12 give `failed` missing with the NOTE and each request its own values, and 24,000 made-up logs whose requests were interleaved at random gave no row with a value of another request, no answered request with `failed` = 1 and no missing value without a NOTE.
- **`Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` and `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` run in the CAS session that is connected.** `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` ran `cas mySess;` and `cas mySess terminate;` whatever the SAS session held - behind `Log-Parser-Code.sas` in one SAS session that is the WARNING of a session that exists, and the end of the session the librefs of the parser are bound to. Both programs now ask `SESSFOUND` of the session the option `SESSREF` names and of their own session, start their own session only when neither is connected, and end only a session they started. `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` was measured on the test system later that day: behind a connected session it started none and left that one connected, alone it started a session of its own and ended it, and it wrote its table both times. `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` ran on 2026-10-13 where no session was connected: it started a session of its own and ended it. Behind a connected session it is not run in SAS.
- **A program uses its own CAS session where the session `SESSREF` names was ended, and no longer ends it.** Where the option `SESSREF` named a CAS session that had been ended while the session of the program's own name was still connected, `Get-All-Prompts.sas` ran `cas mySess;`. SAS only wrote `WARNING: A session with the name MYSESS already exists.` and left the option on the ended session, so every PROC CAS step of the pull halted with `ERROR: There is no server connection to execute the action 'table.caslibInfo'.`. At its end the pull ran `cas mySess terminate;` and ended the parser's session, which it had not started. Measured on the test system on 2026-10-14 with version 2.5 of the pull, and on 2026-10-15 with version 2.6 of the pull and of the step *LLM - Get All Prompts* (`gapMySess`), with the link (`lnkMySess`) and with Load-Accelerator-Tables (`_latSess`): each wrote the WARNING, its PROC CAS steps halted, and at its end it ended the session the staging had started. The programs and steps that ask `SESSFOUND` of the session the option `SESSREF` names had the same check: `Log-Parser-Code.sas` (`mysess` and `llmLogMySess`), `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas`, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas`, `Load-Accelerator-Tables.sas` and the steps *LLM - Log Parser* and *LLM - Get All Prompts*. In front of that check, each of them and `Get-All-Prompts.sas` now asks `SESSFOUND` for its own session; in that state it sets the option to that session, runs in it and leaves it connected. In the steps that is *LLM - Log Parser* 2.12 and *LLM - Get All Prompts* 2.7; the ninth pass ran 2.11 and 2.6, which do not have it. `Create-Optimization-Dataset.sas` had no session check: it ran `cas _optbuild;` and `cas _optbuild terminate;` whatever the SAS session held. It names `_optbuild` in each of its CAS statements; it now starts that session only where it is not connected, and ends it only then. Its `proc casutil session=_optbuild;` steps still halted with `ERROR: No active Cloud Analytic Services session.` where the option `SESSREF` named a session that was ended while `_optbuild` was connected. This was measured on the test system on 2026-10-16: the program started no session, the DATA step could not promote over the table that was not dropped, and the run ended with `SYSCC` 1012. In that state the program now sets the option to `_optbuild`, as the other programs do for their own session. `Optimize-Prompt-DSPy.sas` (`_optdata`), `Ingest-Documents.sas` (`_ragcas` to `_ragcas4`) and the nine RAG steps are unchanged: each starts a session of its own name without a session check and ends it. `tests/test_fix_content_23.py` takes every statement that starts or ends a CAS session from the programs and the steps, and runs the check and the clean-up for every state of the sessions a program names, a foreign session and the option. In the eleventh pass, on 2026-10-16, the programs ran on the test system from a test content root with test tables in `casuser` - `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` and `Create-Optimization-Dataset.sas` as copies with test table names, the two steps through their programs with their settings as macro variables - with no session (the option on `CASAUTO`, as in a job), with the session of the program's own name connected and the option on a session that was ended (`cas <own>; cas zzs; cas zzs terminate;`), and with both connected and the option on the other (`cas <own>; cas zzs;`). Where the option named the ended session, the parser, `Get-All-Prompts.sas`, the link, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas`, `Load-Accelerator-Tables.sas` and the steps *LLM - Log Parser* 2.12 and *LLM - Get All Prompts* 2.7 set the option to their own session, wrote no WARNING that a session exists, had no PROC CAS step halted, and left that session connected; where it named a connected session, the option stayed on it (the steps did not run in that state). With no session the programs but the parser, which keeps its session, started their own and ended it. `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` ended with an ERROR of its own in each state, because the fact sheet tables of `Public` were saved and not in memory on the test system, and `Load-Accelerator-Tables.sas` with the ERROR of its end check for the three fact sheet tables the test root lacked, as in the passes before. `Create-Optimization-Dataset.sas` ran clean with no session, where it started `_optbuild` and ended it, and where the option named a connected session, where it left `_optbuild` connected; it failed where the option named a session that was ended, as said above. Its change made after the pass has not run in SAS or against SAS Viya.
- **A log table that `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` loaded and did not link stays in memory.** After a restart of the CAS server the program loads `LLM_LOGS` from its saved file. A run with nothing to link builds no table, and the loaded one was then a table of the CAS session of the program and gone with it. It is now promoted as `_lnk_promote_table` says, without writing the saved file again, and one NOTE says so - a WARNING where the promotion did not take. Measured on the test system later that day with the table dropped from memory and its saved file left: with `_lnk_promote_table` 1 the NOTE *… was not in memory. It was loaded from its saved file and promoted as it was loaded, the saved file was left as it is.*, and the next job found the table in memory, promoted, with its 1040 rows and 36 columns and the saved file as it was; with 0 the NOTE that it is a table of the CAS session, and the next job found no table. The WARNING was not provoked.
- **The NOTE of the log parser for content that can not be read names what it counts.** It gave options that are nested deeper than Python decodes as its example; those are read as names and values and are not counted - on the test system on 2026-10-12 a request with such options was in the table and the NOTE was not written. The example is now a request body that is nested deeper than Python reads, which is counted. Measured on the test system later that day: a `Request Data:` line nested 100000 deep gave a row with its status and its response, without prompts and without a kind, and the NOTE; options of 100000 opening brackets gave the row and not this NOTE.
- **An `optimizationId` that two entries of a tracker hold gives no two rows of `MODEL_CATALOG` with one key.** The rows of the second entry carry the id as `<id>#2`, of the third as `<id>#3`, and a WARNING names the model; no entry is left out, as each has a window of its own that its calls are found by. `PROMPT_OPTIMIZATIONS` holds the entries as the tracker has them, so its key is not unique for such a model. Measured on the test system later on 2026-10-12 with a copy of a tracker that held its entry twice under the id 1: the WARNING, the catalog rows with `optimization_id` 1 and `1#2`, a catalog key that was unique in its 424 rows, and two rows with the id 1 in `PROMPT_OPTIMIZATIONS`. Changed after that and not run in SAS (version 2.3 of the step): a counter that would make the id of another entry is passed over - a tracker with the ids 1, 1 and `1#2` gave two rows with one key again, and gives 1, `1#3` and `1#2` now -, an id longer than the 16 bytes of the column makes room for its counter, and entries without an `optimizationId`, which the WARNING listed as an empty id, are counted as before (`#2`, `#3`) while the WARNING says how many there are.
- **The order in which the retrieval model looks for the credentials of the vector store is documented as the code has it.** `retrieve_context.py` takes the user and the password from the `options` input of the call first, then from the environment variables (`<BACKEND>_RAG_USER` / `<BACKEND>_RAG_PW`), and asks the SAS Viya credentials service only for what is still missing after both. Its docstring listed the credentials service before the environment, and *Managing Credentials* said the model "tries the credential domain first and falls back to the environment variables". Both now state the order of the code; where the two variables are set, the credential domain is not asked. The code is unchanged, and so is its MANIFEST block. The environment variables are now measured for SAS Micro Analytic Service (2026-10-11): with `PGVECTOR_RAG_USER` and `PGVECTOR_RAG_PW` set on the containers of the deployment `sas-microanalytic-score` from a Kubernetes secret - `kubectl set env deployment/sas-microanalytic-score --containers=* --from=secret/<name>`, after which the pod restarts - and `{}` as the `options` of the call, the retrieval model returned `retrieval_status` *ok* and three hits. Not measured: the same variables for the test of SAS Intelligent Decisioning, which runs in CAS, and for a SAS Container Runtime container.
- **The closing NOTE of `Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` names the table it wrote.** It said `RAG_RUN_COST` whatever `_rcv_out_table` was - seen on the test system on 2026-10-12 with a table of another name. Measured later that day with one run table and an output table of another name: the NOTE named that table, the table had 15 rows and 30 columns, `SYSCC` was 0 and `Public` held no `RAG_RUN_COST` before or after. With two run tables the program failed - see the entry about two run tables.
- **The reason of a run history that could not be read reaches SAS as text.** The RAG ingestion job handed the reason the store or the file system gave to SAS through a weaker escape than the logging programs have, and its Python held both marks of a SAS comment. It takes the function of the logging programs now: in the WARNING and in the milestone a quote shows as a backtick, `%` as `pct`, `&` as `+` and a semicolon as a comma, a comment mark is split, parentheses that do not pair up show as brackets, and the reason ends after 200 characters. Nothing else in `Ingest-Documents.sas` changed. Not run in SAS.
- **`mdb builders-import` stops when a report that is on the site cannot be read.** The command reads the options of the Prompt Builder report before the import so that it can write them back. A report that was on the site and could not be read at that moment was passed over: the import replaced the report, the new one was configured from discovery, and the run ended with `Done.` and exit code 0 - the loss the capture exists to prevent, found by a review against a service that is made up. The command now stops before anything is uploaded, imported or written, also in a dry run and for the retired report `RAG Builder`, and names the two ways on: `--options <file>` with the options to write back, or `--no-options-capture` to import without them. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb builders-import` reads the tasks of the import job.** The job of the import of 2026-10-12 ended `completed` with `errorCount` 0 while three of its 71 tasks had ended `completedWithWarnings`, and the command said `imported 71 object(s).` and `Done.`. The tasks are now read to the end; one that did not end `completed` is printed with its message, the run ends with exit code 1, and the options are not written into a report the command cannot be sure was replaced. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb builders-import --dry-run` uploads nothing.** It uploaded the package to read the import mapping and deleted it again. It now sends GET requests only - the sign-in aside - and reads the `mapping:` lines off the package: the tables and the substitutions of its reports. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12 with `--no-reports`: the dry run sent the sign-in and 10 GET requests and nothing else. A dry run of a package that holds reports was not run.
- **A refused sign-in is asked again as it really arrives, and ends as one line.** On 2026-10-12 a call of `mdb content-deploy` ended with a Python traceback and exit code 1 at a sign-in the test system answered with `401 Client Error` from `/SASLogon/oauth/token`; the same command ran a minute and a half later. Read from the code: the repeat of this release took only the `AuthenticationError` of sasctl for a refused sign-in, and this refusal arrived as an `HTTPError`. A 401 from the token address is now asked again like the other, and a refusal that stays ends the command with one sentence and exit code 2 - `SAS Viya at <url> refused the sign-in of <user> ... (<n> sign-ins made): HTTP 401 from /SASLogon/oauth/token ...` (with what the answer says, see the entry on the wrong password) - without a traceback and without the password. Not run against SAS Viya: no sign-in was refused in the second pass of 2026-10-12 - the request logs hold 108 requests to the token address, each answered 200.
- **`mdb content-deploy` follows no reference, and of two objects of one name it writes neither.** A folder member that is a reference to a file elsewhere was compared like a file, and when it differed, the file it points at was deleted - an object outside the folders of the manifest. With two registered steps of one name in a folder only the first was updated. Both were found by a review against a service that is made up. A reference now fails its entry without a request to the object it names, and two objects of one name are both named with their ids and neither is written; the rest of the run goes on, and the exit code is 1. An update of a job definition keeps a property a site added. In the second pass of 2026-10-12 two custom steps of one name could not be put into one folder - SAS Viya refused the second registration with HTTP 409, errorCode 11552 (`An item named ... of type "Custom step" already exists in the folder`) -, so two objects at one path are not run against SAS Viya. On 2026-10-13 a reference was put at a path of the manifest: the entry was `FAILED` with GET requests only, and `release.json` kept its time stamp; beside the file itself the reference was passed over that day, see *One name, one object* below.
- **`mdb package-check` sees what it did not look at.** A second object at a listed path; a file that names a listed report as its parent, whatever it holds - the package that passed on 2026-10-12 held the four pictures of the usage report unseen; a second `form` property or a further property of a job definition; `properties` and `arguments` of a custom step; a body with another name or type than its entry; another release, kind or field in `release.json`, or an entry listed twice; and a field at the top of the package that an export does not write. Not compared, by decision: a description and the fields the service derives for display, ids, links, users and time stamps, and CRLF for LF.
- **`mdb package-export --allow-differences` no longer ends with the sentence about committing.** On 2026-10-12 the line `Review the diff, then commit it with the CHANGELOG entry that explains the change.` followed the line that said the file is not a package of the release. That sentence is printed for the release file only; the last line of such an export now says to keep the file out of the repository, and the command names the user ids the file holds in `createdBy` and `modifiedBy`. In the second pass of 2026-10-12 the export with `--allow-differences` ended with `Keep this file out of the repository. ...` and named the user id of the exporter in 298 places. The release file names no user since (see the entry of `mdb package-export` and the user ids).
- **Start Here and Load-Accelerator-Tables run in a compute context that runs as the person who opens them.** Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12: opened as the installation text of that day said, without `_contextName`, Start Here ran in the `SAS Job Execution compute context`, which runs its sessions under a service account on that system. SAS Viya answered every request of the page with 401, and the page read `13 ok, 0 missing, 15 not checked`, twelve of the fifteen lines with the advice to ask an administrator. With `&_contextName=SAS Studio compute context` the same page had 97 lines that were ok. Both job definitions now carry the parameter `_contextName`, with the default the job definition of the Prompt Builder has, `SAS Studio compute context`, as a setting of the site. `mdb builders-import` gives a job definition it creates the ingestion compute context (`ragComputeContext`) of the options it writes back - not `computeContext`, which is the context of the optimization job and may be the one the page failed in -, and writes `--compute-context` into every job definition that has the parameter; a context a site set stays (see *Fixed*). To open the page in another context, add `&_contextName=<a compute context that runs as you>` to its address. In the second pass of 2026-10-12 the job definition of Start Here named the `SAS Studio compute context`, and opened without the parameter the job ran there with `97 ok`. Opened in the `SAS Job Execution compute context` the page was built completely that time - no request was answered 401 -, by a session whose user was not the user it was started for: what a session of that context may read depends on how the system configures the context.
- **`Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas` failed with two run tables and still said that the table was built.** Measured on SAS Viya 2026.09 on 2026-10-12 with two tables in `_rcv_runs_tables`, separated by a comma as the comment of the setting prescribes: `ERROR: A character operand was found in the %EVAL function ...`, `ERROR: Argument 2 to macro function %SCAN is not a number.`, `ERROR: The macro _RCV_STACK_RUNS will stop executing.` - and then the NOTE that the output table was built in its CAS library, for a table that did not exist. The list stood in `%scan` as a reference with an ampersand, so its own comma ended the argument. It is handed over through `%superq` now; one, two and many tables work, with or without blanks around the commas. The output table is dropped and loaded only when the run tables were stacked without an error, so a run that fails on the way leaves the table of an earlier run. The closing NOTE is written when CAS holds the table with the rows of this run; otherwise the run writes a line that starts with `ERROR: Build-RAG-Cost-View:` and ends with `SYSCC` 1012. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12 with the program of that day: one run table gave `NOTE: ... built in casuser.` and a table of 15 rows and 30 columns; two gave 34 rows (19 and 15), also with blanks around the comma, and no message of `%SCAN` or `%EVAL`; a run table that was not there gave SAS's error for it, errors and WARNINGs for the WORK tables behind it, and then the program's ERROR line, `SYSCC` 1012 and the table of the run before with its rows and its creation time; an empty setting gave the program's ERROR line and `SYSCC` 1012, followed by SAS's errors for two WORK tables and two WARNINGs - the program went on behind its own ERROR line, which it no longer does (see *Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas stops at the first refusal*).
- **Start Here: the advice for a custom step that is missing no longer leads to an upload.** It names the import of the package and `mdb content-deploy`, which register a step, and says that uploading the `.step` file does not. Measured on 2026-10-12: a `.step` uploaded in the place of a step is a file and no step, and `mdb content-deploy` then registers the step and leaves that file beside it. The page now finds a registered step whichever of the two members the folder lists first, and its line says to delete the file. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12: with the registered step deleted and its `.step` uploaded as a file, the row said `missing | a file of that name is there, and no registered step` and named the import and `mdb content-deploy`; after an import registered the step, `ok | present, and a file of that name lies beside it` with the advice to delete the file; after the file was deleted, `ok | present`.
- **Start Here asks for `release.json` at the Files service only.** The uri its folder member names is held to `/files/files/<id>` before it is requested, as the uri of a folder and of a report already were. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-12: the page found and read `release.json` of the test root through that check (`70 of 72 entries of release.json are present in this content root`).
- **What was said of `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` was not what it does.** `SAS-Viya-Integrations/Setup/README.md` and the header of `Load-Accelerator-Tables.sas` said that it replaces a table the way the release table is replaced. It tries each sheet beside its table, counts it and drops that trial, and then drops the table and loads the sheet a second time, so a failure of CAS between the drop and that load can cost the table. The sentence said that, and since then the program does what the release table does (see *`Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` replaces a table only with a sheet that was read*); the README and the header say so.
- **`mdb builders-import`: a compute context a site set survives the import.** The import wrote the ingestion compute context (`ragComputeContext`) of the options it restores into `_contextName` of every job definition that has it, `SAS-LLM-Prompt-Builder-UI` included, so a context an administrator set was replaced on every upgrade whose report holds one - and the shipped report does. Now only a job definition the import creates, or one that lacks the parameter, gets that context; an existing value stays, and the run says which: `Compute context: <name> (the ingestion compute context of the options) - given to a job definition the import creates and to one on the site that has no _contextName; a _contextName a job definition on the site holds is kept.` `--compute-context` writes into every job definition that has the parameter. `_contextName` and `contentRoot` are values of the site wherever a job definition of the package has them, also where the checkout of the importer does not list that job definition, and `contentRoot` is always written with the content root the content goes to, by the import and by `mdb content-deploy`. The site settings now also cover the CAS library and server, the credential domain, the names of the repository and the projects and the compute contexts Start Here looks for, and the CAS library and the release table of the loader; the address of the guides, `_action` and `_output_type` stay the release's. Measured on 2026-10-13, with the line as it read that day: an import whose options named an ingestion compute context left the `_contextName` a test root had set on `Start Here`, gave the job definition `Load-Accelerator-Tables`, which it created, the context of the options, and with `--compute-context` updated `Start Here`. A job definition on the site without the parameter and the other site settings were not part of that pass.
- **`mdb package-check --release`: only a commit in the history of the checkout passes.** A `release.json` whose commit the clone does not hold, or whose text is no commit, passed both forms (git answers 128 for both). Each answer other than *HEAD or an ancestor of it* is now a sentence of its own - not in the history of this checkout (the clone holds it on another line of history), not held by this clone (a commit of a branch that was squash-merged or deleted after the deploy, or a clone too shallow), or git cannot say -, drift between releases and an error with `--release`. A text that is no commit id in full - an abbreviation included, see the entry on the commit id -, and a date that is not the date of the commit, fail both forms. A test runs the squash-merge sequence in a real scratch repository.
- **`mdb content-deploy` and `mdb builders-import`: `release.json` is written last.** A run that stopped left a `release.json` that named the new release over content that was partly old. `release.json` is now written only when every entry in front of it was written, and only by a run that covers every entry of the manifest; behind a stop or a failure its line says `not attempted` and why. After a run that stopped, the folder's `release.json` is the one an earlier run wrote - Start Here shows that release and checks the entries that file lists -, or there is none, and Start Here shows it as missing. A folder that cannot be created stops the run like any failed write, and a content root made with the folders above it names what was created before the failure. A report behind a stop is `not deployed (not looked up after the stop)`, not `not attempted`. Measured on 2026-10-13 in two test content roots: `release.json` was the last line of an object and the last write of a deploy that created 70 entries, and of an import in place; with a reference in the place of a file the entry was `FAILED`, `release.json` `not attempted (not written: 1 entry in front of it FAILED ...)` and unchanged in its time stamp and its bytes, with GET requests only; and a write that SAS Viya refused with HTTP 412 stopped the run - 30 entries `not attempted`, the usage report `not deployed ... (not looked up after the stop)`, `release.json` not attempted and unchanged, exit code 1. Not run against SAS Viya: a folder that cannot be created.
- **`mdb`: the update of a file is guarded by the version that was compared.** `If-Match` carried the ETag of a second read right before the write, so a file changed by someone after the comparison was written over. It now carries the ETag of the content read that the comparison used, and such a file is refused (412) and left as it is. The pass of 2026-10-12 measured that a file and its content answer with the same ETag. On 2026-10-13 the `If-Match` of an update was the ETag of the `GET .../content` in front of it, answered 200, and a file that another writer changed between the read and the write of the command was refused with HTTP 412 and kept what that writer wrote.
- **`mdb content-deploy --to` says what it writes, and a new folder needs `--create`.** With an id or a URI the object is read for its name and its folder is asked for (`GET /folders/ancestors`, which no pass has sent yet); the line ends with `- the registered step '<name>' in <folder>`. An object whose name is not the entry's is refused unless `--force`: `--to X names the registered step 'Y' in /F, not 'E': nothing was written - pass --force to update it all the same`. The folder form of `--to`, and a `--content-root` other than the manifest's, create a missing folder only with `--create`; without it nothing is written: `there is no folder P, and nothing was created: pass --create to create it (with every folder above it that is missing)`. `mdb builders-import` takes `--create` as well, and `deploy-rag-content.ps1 -Create` / `.sh -c` pass it on. Measured on 2026-10-13: the id of a step of another name was refused with the name and the folder of that step in the line and GET requests only - `GET /folders/ancestors` was answered 200 -, `--force` updated it with its id kept, a path of a step of the entry's name said `equal`, and `--only RAG --to` a folder that was not there ended with the sentence above and, for a folder of another name, created the 41 entries with `--create`. Not run against SAS Viya: `--create` of `mdb builders-import`, and `-Create` / `-c` of the scripts.
- **`mdb builders-import`: what it takes from a package.** A name that cannot be part of a path - empty, `.`, `..`, a slash, a blank at either end - is not taken, and neither is anything below a folder of such a name, each with a `not imported:` line and its reason. An object of a kind `mdb` does not import is named for that, not as an object outside the root. An empty folder of the package is created and has its line. The root of a package is the folder its one requested item names, so an export made from a folder below others no longer takes the topmost folder. A field at the top of the package that an export does not write is not handed to the transfer service, and a file a report shows from a folder gets a `note:` line. Measured on 2026-10-13 with a copy of an export that held an empty folder and a folder named `..`: `not imported: folder '..' (...)`, and the empty folder was created. Not run against SAS Viya: the other names, an object of a kind `mdb` does not import, a field at the top of a package, and the `note:` line.
- **`mdb` options: the report is the one at its place in the content root.** `mdb builders-import`, `mdb options-save` and `mdb options-restore` (both now with `--content-root`) look the report up as a member of `<root>/Prompt Builder` - and the retired one in `<root>/RAG` -, no longer by its name anywhere on the system; two reports of that name in the folder are an error. A report that is read and holds no option at all stops the capture like an unreadable one: `<report>: it is in <folder> and holds no option at all - it is not the report of the Builder, or its content is not what mdb reads.` and `Nothing was imported.`; `mdb options-save` passes such a report over. Measured on 2026-10-13 for a root without the report: `mdb builders-import` and `mdb options-save --content-root <root> --reports-only` asked for the folder of the report at its place (`GET /folders/folders/@item?path=`) and sent no request to `/reports/reports`; the import said `Prompt Builder: no such report in <root>/Prompt Builder - skipped.`, the save `Nothing captured.` with exit code 1. Not run against SAS Viya: a report that is found there, one that cannot be read and one that holds no option.
- **`mdb builders-import`: the release table is the package's, and a run leaves less behind.** The import loads `Data/ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv` of the package where the package holds it, else the release table made from the changelog of the checkout. Its line, and the lines of `mdb load-releases` and `mdb setup`, name the latest dated release and an unreleased newer one (`latest dated release 2.2.0; 2.3.0 is in it as unreleased`), where they said `Current release: 2.2.0` next to a `release.json` of 2.3.0 on 2026-10-12. The part of the package uploaded to the transfer service is deleted once the import job has ended, and a failed delete is a line with the id and how to delete it. A run that writes every option back removes its `builder-options-captured-<time>.json`. A repository or project id the captured report holds no value for is discovered for that kind only, so a 2.2.x site without the report `RAG Builder` no longer ends with an empty `embeddingProjectID`. After a failed run without reports, the command to repeat no longer promises a report. Measured on 2026-10-13: `Release table: casuser.<table> loaded from Data/ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv of <file> (755 rows, latest dated release 2.2.0; 2.3.0 is in it as unreleased).` for a file of 756 lines; a second import that was all `equal` and sent no write; and behind a failed run with `--no-reports` the line `(it brings again what did not arrive in place; with --no-reports no report is imported or configured)`. Not run against SAS Viya: the delete of the uploaded package, the kept options file, and an id that is discovered for one kind.
- **`mdb package-export`: a failed delete no longer costs the file.** On 2026-10-12 a delete of the export package that raised - provoked by the harness of the pass, which stood in for a dropped connection - ended the command with a Python traceback, without the file it had downloaded, and the package stayed on the server. The file is now written and checked whatever the delete does; a delete of the export package or of the export job record - which is deleted too now, where that record stayed on the server - that is refused or gets no answer is a line with the id and how to delete it, and the command ends with exit code 3. Measured on 2026-10-13: the DELETE of the export package and of the export job record were each answered 204, and the counts of both on the server were what they were before the export. A delete that is refused and exit code 3 have not run.
- **`mdb package-export`: the release file names no user.** `createdBy`, `modifiedBy` and `userName` - of the package, the summaries, the bodies and the report wrappers - become `sas-agentic-ai-accelerator`, and nothing else changes; `--keep-user-ids` works with `--out` only. `mdb package-check` fails a package that names a user, and `--fix` rewrites the ids. `contentSourceLocation` stays: for the content root of the release it names no one, and `mdb` reads it to refuse a package of a nested folder. The four packages of earlier releases were read for this: every user id in them is in one of these fields or in what the export removes. Measured on 2026-10-13 on a test content root: the export ended with exit code 0, all 143 `createdBy` and 143 `modifiedBy` of the file were `sas-agentic-ai-accelerator`, the 12 `userName` of the folder bodies were not (see the entry on `userName`), and the DELETE of the export package and of the export job record were each answered 204, with the counts of both back at their start. Exit code 3 and a refused delete have not run. What the transfer service does with neutral fields on an import of the reports has not been measured.
- **`mdb package-check`: what an object says about itself.** A file, a custom step, a job definition or a folder that carries a connector other than its `parentFolder`, a substitution, or a host in its summary, description or links fails both forms - the shape read from the real exports of 2026-10-12 and of earlier releases. `never_ship` refuses a folder whose name holds `generated` in any form. `--allow-missing` is no longer honoured once the package files of earlier releases are gone from the checkout. The workflow runs `mdb package-check --release` on a branch whose change replaces the package file.
- **`deploy-rag-content.ps1`: a warning on the error stream no longer stops it.** In Windows PowerShell 5.1, with the error stream of the script redirected, the first line `mdb` wrote to it - urllib3's `InsecureRequestWarning` - ended the script under `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` with `NativeCommandError`, and the output of `mdb` was lost. Reproduced with a stand-in for `mdb` and fixed: the call runs under `Continue` and passes the exit code of `mdb` on. The bash twin was checked the same way and had no such defect.
- **LLM - Create LLM Call reads every option of an options file (1.2).** Measured on 2026-10-12 with version 1.1 and the options file of gpt_5_mini, whose option `reasoning_effort` lists the values it can take: PROC TRANSPOSE wrote `ERROR: The ID value "col_values" occurs twice in the same BY group.` four times, its table held 2 of the 3 options, the job ended with `SYSCC` 108, and the code file was written without that option, with the NOTE of a good run. The options are now read with one PROC SQL step that takes the default, the range and the description of each option; a member that is a list is not read. A run that cannot read the default of every option writes no code and no file and ends with `ERROR: LLM - Create LLM Call: no code was written, because <why>.` and `SYSCC` 1012. *LLM - Get Options* (1.1), an earlier copy of the step, and `SAS-Code-LLM-Calls/Create-LLM-Call.sas` read the options the same way. The code these programs write for an options file version 1.1 could read is unchanged. Measured with version 1.2 on 2026-10-13, with the values of manual entry: for gpt_5_mini a code file of 34 lines with `%let reasoning_effort=medium;` and `SYSCC` 0, for gpt_41_mini 36 lines and `SYSCC` 0, and for an options file that is not there the ERROR line with `(status: 404)`, `SYSCC` 1012 and a failed job. *LLM - Get Options* 1.1 failed that day in the state it comes with, see *LLM - Get Options 1.2* below. The tests follow the read on a model of the table the JSON engine makes of the `options.json` of every LLM definition. Not run in SAS: `Create-LLM-Call.sas`. The step with a table for its selector ran in the second pass of 2026-10-13, see *the `optionsFileURI` of a model table* below.
- **Build-RAG-Cost-View.sas stops at the first refusal.** Measured on 2026-10-12: with an empty `_rcv_runs_tables` the program went on behind its own ERROR line, and SAS wrote ERROR lines for `WORK._RCV_RUNS` and `WORK._RCV_COST`, which did not exist, and two WARNINGs; with a run table that is not there, the same for `WORK._RCV_COST` and three WARNINGs. A run now writes one ERROR line and runs no further step: after a list without a table, after run tables that could not be stacked, and after a pricing step that ends with an error. WORK tables left by an earlier run in the same session are deleted at the start. Measured on 2026-10-13: with an empty list exactly one ERROR line of the program, no `File WORK._RCV_...` line, no WARNING and `SYSCC` 1012; with a run table that is not there the line of SAS that says so, one WARNING and exactly one `ERROR: Build-RAG-Cost-View: <table> was not built ...`, `SYSCC` 1012; with one run table 15 rows, with two 34, and `SYSCC` 0. A pricing step that ends with an error did not occur.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.6: a row never takes the transaction of another request.** The time of the newest request that could not be told apart was kept after the last such request. In a part of the file that began while nothing ran, a line then looked older by more than a second, a part began that was none, the requests that were counted were forgotten, and a waiting request took the transaction, the token counts, the run time and the status of another request - 10 of 600 files of a review generator that mixes the first lines of three or four requests. This time now counts only while such a request is counted. The Python runs in the tests and ran on the collected log of the test system outside SAS (1040 rows, all 36 columns identical); the code file of this version ran in SAS on 2026-10-13, on a file the collection script wrote (1,024 rows, see *Changed*).
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.6: a request line quoted in a response.** One more row was written for it, and the response ended in front of it. Such a line is now read as a request first and decided at the end of the file. It stays a request where it gets a transaction, an error, a status or a response of its own. Where it gets none of them and a later request of its part got its transaction, it is read as a line of the response - no row, the response whole, the status kept whatever time the quoted lines carry - and since version 2.7 a NOTE counts the request lines read that way: a request whose container wrote nothing but its first lines there is the same file line for line. Where nothing came later in its part, it can also be a request that the end of the part cut off, and the two files are the same line for line: no row is written, the response is missing, and a NOTE counts it. A `Response Data:` line of another transaction ends a response. Not run in SAS on a file that is known to hold such a line.
- **Start Here says what was measured in each compute context, and what to do in one's own session.** On 2026-10-12 one run in the `SAS Job Execution compute context` of the test system had every request answered 401 (15 lines not checked), and a later run the same day built the whole page (97 ok) in a session whose user differs from its owner. The page, its header and the README give both runs and say that what such a session may read depends on how the system configures the context. A user who is refused everything in their own session is told to ask for the rights (403) or about the sign-in (401), not to open the page in another context. One person written with a domain behind `@` or in front of a backslash is one person, also in the NOTE of `Load-Accelerator-Tables`. Measured on 2026-10-13: opened in the `SAS Job Execution compute context` the page carried the note that what a session may read depends on how the system configures its compute context, and a link to the `SAS Studio compute context`; opened without the parameter it had no such link. Not run in SAS: a session that is refused everything, and a user who is written with a domain.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables replaces only a release table.** The parameter `releaseTable` could name any table of the CAS library, which the job then dropped and replaced. A table of that name that lacks one of the ten columns every release table has (the table of 2.2.0, which has no `builder_part`, still counts as one) is left as it is, and the job ends with an ERROR line and `SYSCC` 1012. Measured on 2026-10-13 with a table made from `sashelp.class`: the ERROR line, the table unchanged (19 rows, 5 columns, the same time stamps), no `_NEW` table left, `SYSCC` 1012.
- **Start Here links the guides only on the website of the accelerator or on this SAS Viya.** The parameter `guideUrl` can come with the request, so the page linked any http(s) address it named. Any other address is now shown as text, with a line that says why. Measured on 2026-10-13 with a `guideUrl` of another site: the five guide entries were text, without a link.
- **`Get-All-Prompts.sas` and `Link-Logs-To-Models.sas` pass names to the macro language as one text.** Column names handed to `INDEXW` and the six settings handed to `%_lnk_ensure_loaded` now go through `%superq`, so a comma in a value no longer splits an argument (*LLM - Get All Prompts* 2.4). Both code files ran on 2026-10-13 - the pull wrote its five tables, and the link linked 1,024 of 1,024 rows - with names that hold no comma; a name with one is not run in SAS.
- **`mdb package-export` and `mdb package-check`: `userName` is a user field, and one list serves both.** The exports SAS Viya 2026.09 made on 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13 carry a user id in `userName` of each of their folder bodies (12 of 12), beside `createdBy` / `modifiedBy`. `mdb package-check` counted that field; the export rewrote only the other two. Measured on 2026-10-13 on a test content root: the export replaced all 143 `createdBy` and 143 `modifiedBy`, left the 12 `userName`, printed `left as the export wrote them (--keep-user-ids)` without that option and no `Replaced the user id(s)` line, and `mdb package-check` - with and without `--release` - failed the file with `names the user id(s) ... in createdBy / modifiedBy (12 place(s))`, which `--fix` did not change. An export of the content root in that shape ends with `Nothing was written`. Now `createdBy`, `modifiedBy` and `userName` are one list: the export rewrites these fields and the gate counts them, in the same places (the package, the summaries, the bodies, the report wrappers). The lines name the three fields, the `--keep-user-ids` line is printed only with that option, and `mdb package-check --fix` rewrites what its sentence says. A user id that is part of a path - `contentSourceLocation`, and the `contentRoot` default of a job definition, in an export of a folder below `/Users/<id>` - is left as it is, and the line names the folder; the release file is written from the content root of the release only, also when `--out` names it. Read for this, in place: the three exports of the two passes and the four packages up to 2.2.x, every value searched for the ids the three fields hold - no other field of an object that ships holds one. Applied in memory to an export of each day, the changed code leaves no id in a field and the gate prints no user line. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with an export of a test content root below the folder of a user: the export printed `Replaced the user id(s) <id> in createdBy / modifiedBy / userName with 'sas-agentic-ai-accelerator' (298 place(s))` and named the folder `contentSourceLocation` keeps, the file held that text in its 143 `createdBy`, 143 `modifiedBy` and 12 `userName`, and `mdb package-check` printed no user line for it. With `--keep-user-ids` the export printed the line of that option, `mdb package-check` failed the file with `names the user id(s) <id> in createdBy / modifiedBy / userName (298 place(s))`, `--fix` rewrote the 298 places, and the check behind it printed no user line. `--out` with the path of the release file ended with exit code 2, `Nothing was exported` and no request; its sentence said `Pass --out to write it somewhere else` although `--out` was passed, and says `Name another file with --out.` in that case now. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13: exit code 2, the sentence that ends `Name another file with --out. Nothing was exported.`, no request and no file.
- **`mdb package-check`: a commit id is one in full.** The check of the commit `release.json` names took 4 to 64 hexadecimal digits while its message said git names a commit with 40: the first seven digits of an ancestor, in a `release.json` edited by hand, passed `--release`. Only what git writes in full - 40 hexadecimal digits, 64 in a SHA-256 repository - is a commit id now; anything else, an abbreviation included, fails both forms with `which is no commit id in full (git writes one with 40 hexadecimal digits, 64 in a SHA-256 repository)`. `mdb` itself writes the full id. A test runs the whole command in a real scratch repository. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with a copy of an export of a test content root whose `release.json` was cut to the first seven digits of its commit: both forms printed that sentence for the file.
- **`mdb package-check`: only the id of a commit, as it stands, is a commit.** In a `release.json` edited by hand, a full id with a blank in front or a line break behind passed as a commit id; the id of an annotated tag passed as an ancestor and then failed on a sentence about its date; the id of a tree was said to be a commit `which this clone does not hold`. The first is now no commit id in full - the sentence shows the text as Python writes it, so that the blank or the line break is seen -, and the other two get `names the commit <id>, which is the id of another object of this clone - a tag, a tree or the content of a file - and not of a commit`, in both forms of the check. `mdb` writes none of these. In a clone that does not hold the object, such an id is still a commit the clone does not hold: without the object nothing says what an id names. Tested in scratch git repositories.
- **A wrong password costs one sign-in, and the refusal says what SAS Logon answered.** Measured on 2026-10-13: `/SASLogon/oauth/token` answers a password grant with a wrong password with status 401, `Content-Type: application/json`, the 64-byte body `{"error":"invalid_client","error_description":"Bad credentials"}` and the header `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="UAA/client", error="unauthorized", error_description="Bad credentials"`. `mdb` asked every refused grant three times. A grant that is answered `Bad credentials` is now not asked again: a wrong password costs one failed sign-in per command, whatever `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS` says. Any other 401 of the token address is asked up to three times as before - but for one that says `Unauthorized grant type`, see below -, and for those `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS` keeps its meaning. The one line of a refusal names the sign-ins that were made and the `error` / `error_description` of the answer: `... refused the sign-in of <user> with SAS_VIYA_USER + SAS_VIYA_PASSWORD (1 sign-in made): Authentication failed for user '<user>' - sasctl says so for an answer whose error_description says "Bad credentials". ...`, or for another 401 `... (3 sign-ins made): HTTP 401 from /SASLogon/oauth/token with error "<error>" and error_description "<text>". ...`. Read from sasctl 1.11.8: it raises its `AuthenticationError`, without the response, where the 401 carries `bad credentials` in the `error_description`, and passes every other 401 on with the response. Not recorded: the body of the 401 seen while sign-ins overlapped on 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12. The output saved on 2026-10-12 shows that sasctl 1.11.8 passed that refusal on as the `HTTPError` of requests, which it does for a 401 whose `error_description` does not say `Bad credentials`; a refusal like it is asked up to three times, as before. That is why a second sign-in for `Bad credentials`, which the command made as first built for this release, protected against nothing that was observed. On 2026-10-13 twelve and then 24 correct password grants sent at the same moment were all answered 200 (2.6 to 3.5 s): the refusal did not come back, and the 124 sign-ins of the second pass of that day were each answered 200. Tested with the measured body and with sasctl itself over a replaced transport. In the third pass of 2026-10-13 a wrong password went to a stand-in on the workstation that answers with that body, and not to SAS Logon: one request, exit code 2 and the line with `(1 sign-in made)`, also with `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS=3`. No wrong password was sent to SAS Logon with the command as it is now.
