- **Every 401 of the token address ends with the one line.** A 401 of `/SASLogon/oauth/token` whose JSON body is no object, whose `error_description` is no text, or says `Unauthorized grant type`, leaves sasctl 1.11.8 as `AttributeError` or `RuntimeError`, and the command ended with a traceback after one sign-in. `mdb` now finds the answer in the traceback, asks again like for any 401 that is not final - an answer `Unauthorized grant type` is asked once, see below - and ends with `... (3 sign-ins made): HTTP 401 from /SASLogon/oauth/token with error "<error>" and error_description "<text>". ...` - the two fields where the answer has them - and exit code 2. Tested with the installed sasctl and made-up bodies; no such answer was seen on a system. In the third pass of 2026-10-13 a stand-in on the workstation gave such answers: a body that is no object ended with `(no error_description could be read from the answer)`, and an `error_description` with a bracket and a line break was printed as text on one line - each one line and exit code 2.
- **The sign-in sentence is printed as SAS Logon answered.** It was printed with markup on: a `[/red]` in `error` or `error_description` ended the command with a traceback and exit code 1. It is printed as it is, on one line whatever the width of the terminal, with both fields whole (they were cut at 200 characters). The sentence for a missing sasctl keeps the `[viya]` of `cli[viya]`. The same held for about forty other lines of `mdb` that print an error between colour tags - errors that carry what SAS Viya, CAS, the transfer service or a model provider answered: a closing tag in such an answer, for example a path in brackets like `[/folders/...]`, ended the command with a `MarkupError`. Those lines now print a bracket of the answer as text. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **A port in `SAS_VIYA_URL` is used.** `mdb` handed the URL to sasctl, which takes the port from an argument of its own: `http://127.0.0.1:4981` was asked at port 80. Tested with the installed sasctl; not against a system that listens on another port. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with `:443` in `SAS_VIYA_URL`: a dry run of `mdb content-deploy` sent the 73 requests of the same run without the port, in the same order, and printed the same lines but for the first, which shows the address as it was given. The address has to carry its scheme for this: see *An address of SAS Viya that cannot be used is refused before any request* below.
- **`mdb package-export`: the last line of a release export says what the commit holds and which commit is tagged.** It said `commit this file - and nothing else with it` and `Tag the commit that adds the file`; the commit of release 2.3.0 also removes the package files of the earlier releases and takes `--allow-missing` out of the workflow, and after a squash merge the commit that adds the file is a commit of a branch. The line now says: a commit that changes neither the changelog nor a source the manifest names; while a package file of an earlier release lies beside the release file, that the same commit removes them and takes `--allow-missing` out of the workflow; and that the tag goes on the commit of `main` that holds the file, with the commit `release.json` names in its history.
- **The release procedure says which commit is deployed from and which one is tagged.** With the final texts and the package in one pull request, deployed from its branch, the check of the pull request passed and the gate of the tag failed. Where `main` only takes squash-merged pull requests a release is two of them: the release work with its final texts first; the folder is deployed and exported from the commit that merge made on `main`; the package file goes into a second pull request from that commit; the tag names the commit the second merge made on `main`, not the commit of the branch. `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the guide say so. A test follows these steps in scratch git repositories - a bare repository as the host, squash merges, deleted branches, a fresh clone of the tag - with `mdb package-check --release` at each place the workflow runs it, and a second test holds the order that fails the tag. The gate itself did not change. The workflow now also starts on a change to `CONTRIBUTING.md`. No GitHub workflow has run with this.
- **The workflow fails a release tag that is not on `main`.** A tag on the commit of the branch that added the package file passed `mdb package-check --release` in a fresh clone of the tag, although that commit is not on `main` after a squash merge: the gate cannot tell, because that checkout equals its package as well. On a tag the workflow now asks git, in a step in front of the strict gate: `git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD origin/main`. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says so in step 4. Followed in scratch git repositories with both tags; the workflow has not run on GitHub.
- **`mdb package-export`: the command for a test folder writes nothing but the folder.** Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13: the line the export of a test content root printed, `mdb builders-import <file> --content-root <folder> --no-reports`, said in its dry run, with a folder filled in, `Release table: would load Public.ACCELERATOR_RELEASES on cas-shared-default from Data/ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv of <file>` - run as printed for a test folder it would have replaced the release table of the site; it was not run. The line was `mdb builders-import <file> --content-root "<folder>" --no-releases --no-reports --no-files` then. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with that line: with a second test root filled in, its dry run said `Release table: not reloaded (--no-releases)` and `70 entries: 70 created.`, and the run created the 70 entries in place - 11 folders, 37 files, 18 custom steps and four job definitions - without a request to the transfer service; a dry run behind it found the 70 `equal`. The line is `mdb builders-import <file> --content-root "<folder>" --no-releases --no-reports --create --no-files` now - with `--create`, because run as printed for a test folder that is not there the command ended with exit code 1 and the sentence that says to pass it - and says what it brings: the files, custom steps and job definitions into a test folder, `which it creates where it is not there, and nothing else: no report, no release table, no commands file`. The line with `--create` is not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb package-export --help` no longer says what was not measured.** That is said here and in the guide; a test reads every help text of `mdb` for such a sentence.
- **`mdb content-deploy`: a run of a part does not write `release.json`.** "Written last, and only when every entry in front of it was written" held only within the entries a run selected: `mdb content-deploy --only release.json`, or `--only RAG --only release.json`, wrote the record of a complete release - the release, the commit and every hash - into a folder that held nothing else, and Start Here then showed `release.json | ok`. A run that does not cover every object of the manifest and names `release.json` is now refused before the command signs in - one line, exit code 1, not even the content root is created: `release.json is the record of the whole folder and is written only by a run that covers every entry of the manifest: this run covers a part of it (--only), so nothing was written - leave release.json out of --only, or run without --only`. `--only release.json --to <a file>` is refused with the same sentence. A part that does not name it is deployed as before and leaves the folder's `release.json` as it is. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with the command as first built for this: `--only release.json` and `--only RAG --only release.json` with `--create` for a content root that was not there, and `--only release.json` and `--only Data --only release.json` for an empty one, each ended with the sentence and exit code 1 in the dry run and in the run, and no folder was asked for or created; `--only release.json --to <the release.json of a test root>` ended with a `FAILED` line that held the sentence, `Nothing was updated.` and exit code 1. That build signed in first and printed the header of a run in front of the sentence, so the refusal was two lines behind one request. The check now stands in front of the sign-in for all of these, and the sentence is the only line. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 on the workstation, with every sign-in refused by the harness of the pass: `--only release.json` in the dry run and in the run, `--only RAG --only release.json` and `--only release.json --to <the release.json of a test root>` each ended with exit code 1 and the sentence as the only line, and none sent a request.
- **`mdb content-deploy --only <folder> --to <target>`: the job definitions read the content root the target belongs to.** Measured on 2026-10-13: `--only Data --to <root>/Data --create` wrote `contentRoot = /SAS Agentic AI Accelerator`, the default of the manifest, into `Load-Accelerator-Tables`; run as deployed, the job read the `Data` folder of the real content root. The content root is now the target without the folder's own path at its end - `--only Data --to "/Team/Tables/Data"` writes `/Team/Tables`. Where a job definition of the folder has the parameter `contentRoot` and the target does not end with that path, the command ends with exit code 2 before it signs in and says what to give. The RAG runtime, which has no such job definition, goes into a folder of any name as before, and one object named with `--to` keeps the `contentRoot` the site gave it. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13: `--only Data --to <root>/Data --create` wrote `contentRoot` = `<root>` into the job definition and `--to <root>/zz_loader/Data --create` wrote `<root>/zz_loader`, each behind a dry run that listed the same five entries, and the job run from the second folder looked for its files below `<root>/zz_loader`; `--to <root>/zz_tables` ended with exit code 2 and its sentence without a sign-in, in the dry run and in the run. The command that sentence names held its placeholder without quotation marks - `--content-root <content root>`, which a shell reads as a redirection - and was not run as printed; the placeholder is quoted now. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 on the workstation: `--only Data --to <root>/zz_tables` ended with exit code 2 and no request, in the dry run and in the run, with the sentence that ends `Give the folder as <content root>/Data, or run mdb content-deploy --content-root "<content root>" --only Data.`
- **One name, one object: a reference of the name of an entry fails the entry, alone or beside the object.** Measured on 2026-10-13 in a test content root: with a folder member of type reference at `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py`, `mdb content-deploy` said `FAILED` for the entry and listed the same path under `Not in the manifest and left alone`; with the file and a reference of its name in the folder, `mdb builders-import` said `equal`, listed the reference as left alone and ended with exit code 0. Both commands now follow one rule: a reference of the name and kind of an entry fails the entry whether the object is there or not, its line names every object and every reference of that name, and nothing is written. A reference that the line of its entry names is not listed again; where no line names it - beside a report, or at an entry `--only` left out - the list says `reference to a file - not the file that is listed under this name`. The rule holds for a folder of the manifest: nothing is created beside a reference of its name, two folders of one name fail the same way, and nothing goes into a folder that FAILED - which a dry run now says too, where it said `created` for what a run failed. `--to <path>` that names the object and a reference of its name is refused, and the object is named by its id. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13, in two test content roots. With the file replaced by a reference of its name to a file elsewhere, `mdb content-deploy` said `FAILED ... (a reference of that name is in the folder and names /files/files/<id>, an object that lives elsewhere: it is not followed, and nothing was written - ...)` in the dry run and in the run, left `release.json` `not attempted`, did not list the reference again and ended with exit code 1. With the file and the reference both there, `mdb content-deploy` and `mdb builders-import` said `FAILED ... (the file of that name (id <id>) and a reference of that name, which names /files/files/<id>, an object that lives elsewhere, are in the folder: none was changed and the reference was not followed, because nothing says which one is meant - remove the reference)` and ended with exit code 1; `--to <path>` of that file ended with `... name the file by its id`, and `--to <its id>` said `equal`. With a reference named `Prompt Builder` to a folder elsewhere, `--only "Prompt Builder"` said `FAILED` for the folder and `its folder FAILED (the line of the folder says why): nothing was written into it` for the three entries in it, in the dry run as in the run. Two folders of one name were not staged.
- **`mdb content-deploy`: a reference of another kind is said to be what it is.** The help of the command said that a reference "of that name" fails an entry; the code fails it for a reference of that name to an object of the entry's kind, which is what says that the entry is meant. A reference of that name to an object of another kind fails nothing and is listed at the end with the entry it is not: `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (reference to a report - not the file that is listed under this name)`, where the list said `(reference to a report)` under `Not in the manifest and left alone` for a path the manifest lists. The help of both commands and `SAS-Viya-Integrations/README.md` say "of that kind". Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with a reference named `tokens.py` to a folder, beside the file: `mdb content-deploy` said `equal` for `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py` in the dry run and in the run, listed `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (reference to a folder - not the file that is listed under this name)` under `Not in the manifest and left alone`, sent no write and ended with exit code 0. A reference to a report was not built. The words of that list for the other kinds were changed after the pass: see *what is left alone is called by the word the other lines use* below.
- **The hints of two `FAILED` lines work as printed.** The line for two objects of one name named `mdb content-deploy --only ... --to <id>` without saying that the command writes the content of a checkout - `mdb builders-import` prints the line too -, and the line for a reference alone said `update the object it names with --to`, which is refused as it stands where that object has another name. Both say `from a checkout with mdb content-deploy --only "<path>" --to ...` now; the line for a reference adds `(with --force where that object has another name)`, and for a folder of the manifest it names `--to <the SAS Content path of that folder>`. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with a reference alone at `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py` that named a file of another name: the line ended `... remove the reference, or update the object it names from a checkout with mdb content-deploy --only "RAG/rag_core/tokens.py" --to <its URI> (with --force where that object has another name)`; run as printed with that URI the command was refused (`... names the file '<other name>' in <its folder>, not 'tokens.py': nothing was written - pass --force to update it all the same`, exit code 1, no write), and with `--force` it said `updated file RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (content replaced in place; id <id> kept ...)` with one write, answered 200. The folder form of the hint was printed for a reference named `Prompt Builder` and not run. Three hints still did not run as printed: see *Every hint of a `FAILED` line runs as it is printed* below.
- **`mdb builders-import`: the command a failed run names is the run's own, and is not refused again.** Measured on 2026-10-13: a run with `--no-files` named a repeat without it. And after the reports were refused for another content root the repeat was the same command, refused for the same reason. The line is now built from every option of the command but `--dry-run`; `--out` and `--no-files` are in it. Where the reports were refused for the content root and no package's reports went to the transfer service, the repeat carries `--no-reports` and says that it was added and why; where the reports of one package went through and those of another were refused, the repeat is two commands: the run without that package, and that package with `--no-reports`. As first built for this release it was the one command again with a sentence that it ends with exit code 1 - which it did every time it was run as printed, importing the reports of the other package again on each run. The release table is loaded again only by the line with the package the run took it from; the other carries `--no-releases`. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with one package and `--no-reports --no-releases --no-files`: a run that failed one entry - a reference beside its file, and a write refused with HTTP 412 - named `mdb builders-import <file> --content-root "<root>" --no-releases --no-reports --no-files`, and that line run as printed, once the cause was gone, ended with exit code 0. The repeat for refused reports is not run against SAS Viya: the pass imported no report.
- **The line of a refused write holds the whole answer, and a folder that is created for the content gets a line.** Measured on 2026-10-13: the `FAILED` line of a write that SAS Viya refused with HTTP 412 ended in the middle of the JSON it answered (`"remediation":"Get the latest re))`), and `mdb content-deploy --content-root <root> --create` created the root folder without a line for it, in the dry run and in the run. A refusal now shows `message`, `remediation` and `errorCode` of a JSON error body whole, and a body of another shape up to 300 characters, ended at a word with `...`. Where the folder the content goes into is created, the first line says so - `created folder <path> (the folder the content goes into; created above it: ...)`, in a dry run `... with every folder above it that is missing`, because a dry run does not look up the folders above it. The folder is no entry and is not counted in `N entries`; `Stopped at the first write that failed` names it beside its counts. A sentence that ends `mdb content-deploy` before its first entry is one line at any width of the terminal. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13: a write that SAS Viya refused with HTTP 412 - staged by changing the file between the read and the write of the command - gave `FAILED file RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (the file was left as it is: the update of its content was refused (HTTP 412 The values for the request header field "If-Match" ("<tag>") and the resource's entity tag ("<tag>") are not the same. - Get the latest representation of the resource, and apply the desired changes. Retry the operation with the modified representation and the entity tag from the response header field "ETag". (errorCode 0)))` with `mdb content-deploy` and with `mdb builders-import`, and the run stopped there; `--content-root <root> --create` for a root that was not there printed `created folder <root> (the folder the content goes into)` as its first line and `72 entries: 70 created, 2 not deployed.` at its end, and its dry run `... (the folder the content goes into, with every folder above it that is missing)`.
- **No message of `mdb` cuts an answer of SAS Viya in the middle, and an answer is one line.** The line of a refused write was the only one that showed the answer whole: eleven other messages - of the release table and the fact sheets in CAS, of the model repository, of the reports and of the transfer service - still cut it at 200 or 300 characters. They now show what the deploy line shows, through one function: `message`, `remediation` and `errorCode` of a JSON error whole, any other body up to 300 characters and then `...` at a word. And a line break or a tab in an answer made the line more than one, and a `message` that is an object was printed as Python writes a dict: every run of blanks, tabs and line breaks is one blank now, and an object is shown as JSON. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 for the line of a refused write, with an HTTP 412 staged as in the second pass: the `FAILED` line was one line of the output, written at a width of 2,000 columns, and ended `(errorCode 0)))`. The eleven other messages are not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb builders-import` says in one sentence which job definitions get the compute context of the options.** The line read `given to a job definition this run creates; one the site has keeps its _contextName`, while the code also gives it to a job definition that is on the site without the parameter. The line, the help of the command and of `--compute-context`, and the README now carry one sentence: `given to a job definition the import creates and to one on the site that has no _contextName; a _contextName a job definition on the site holds is kept`. What the import does is unchanged. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with the ingestion compute context in the options of the import: the sentence was printed, the job definition of the test root whose `_contextName` had been taken away got that context (`updated jobDefinition Start Here (parameters; id <id> kept)`, `70 entries: 69 equal, 1 updated.`), and the one that had been given a context of another name kept it.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.7: a response can no longer end a part of the file.** A line in a model's response that had the shape of the collection script's line was taken for it: it ended the part in the middle of the response, and with a later time of collection every line of the script behind it was read as text, so the containers behind it were one part. The time of collection decides nothing any more, the script escapes such a line and the parser reads it back as the text it was. In lines collected without the script of this release such a line cannot be told from the line of the script - two files that are the same byte for byte need different rows, and a test holds them -: it ends the response it stands in, and its request is counted as cut off. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 in SAS, with the collection script of the guide as it was then - with `escape_llm_log` - and `Log-Parser-Code.sas` of this version: the script collected nine containers of the test system into a file of 30,395 lines, nine of them its own; in a copy, four lines of the shape of the script's line were staged inside one response - without a backslash, with one, with two, and behind a carriage return - and sent through the pipe of the script, which put one backslash more in front of each; and the parser gave 890 rows for the file and 890 for the copy, the same in every column but the response of that one request, which held the four staged texts without the backslash the script had added. The pass ran the code file; the step *LLM - Log Parser* 2.7 is not run in SAS.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.7: lines of the old script in front of lines of the new one.** A file that is appended to across the upgrade was read without the time rule from its first line on, and requests of its old part lost their token counts, run time and response. The old part is read by the time of its lines and ends at the first line of the script. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 in SAS with the code file: the collected file without the nine lines of the script, with the file that has them appended behind it, gave 1,780 rows - the 890 rows of the one and the 890 of the other, compared as whole rows. A file that an earlier script wrote was not at hand: the first half was the collected file with the lines taken out.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.7: `failed` and the two NOTEs.** In a file without a single line of a transaction, a request with all its first lines was `failed` = 1 when the lines of its container ended on the request behind it; it is cut off as well now. Every request whose `failed` is missing is counted once: in the NOTE of the requests the lines of their container end on - also where another request ran beside it -, or in `failed is missing for <n> request(s)` of the NOTE of the requests that ran at the same time. Measured with the Python outside of SAS, files with the lines of the script, five seeds a generator: the 24,000 files of the second generator of one reading of the parser gave no cut-off request with `failed` = 1 and no row with a value of another request; the 18,000 of its first gave no row with a value of another request and no answered request with `failed` = 1; and the 2,000 of the generator of a second reading gave no value of another request and no cut-off request with `failed` = 1 unless its part holds an ERROR line of it (the release before: 13 files of the first two with such a row, 1 with such a request); 42 files have one row fewer than request lines, the request line in a response that nothing decides; the numbers of the two NOTEs are the rows in every other file of the second and of the third generator. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 in SAS with the code file: the collected file gave 890 rows and 36 columns with and without the lines of the script, no row different in any column, `failed` 0 for 559 rows, 1 for 331 and missing for none, `SYSCC` 0 and no NOTE of the parser. That file holds no request whose `failed` is missing, so the numbers of the two NOTEs are not measured in SAS.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.7: fewer rows with a value of another request.** Three rules: where the first lines of a request broke off and no request since has a status or an error when the lines end, the requests since keep their request line only; a second line of a kind from the score code for one request leaves it none of the score code's values; a `Response:` line without its `Response Data:` line is given to the one running transaction only behind the line that follows its score code, or with a status of 400 and above. In files WITHOUT the lines of the script, 319 of 12,000 generated files that end a part on a running request still give a row a value of another request (seed 2026: 62 of 2,400, before 90). All are of three classes that no rule decides; a test holds two byte-identical files for each. The three rules are not measured in SAS: the file the second pass of 2026-10-13 parsed gave the same rows with and without the lines of the script.
- ***LLM - Log Parser* 2.7: what `failed` says wrong in a file WITHOUT the lines of the script, kind by kind.** The guide gave `failed` = 1 for a request that was cut off as a limit of such files and named one case; the generated files had not been looked at one by one. Every `failed` that is not what the part of its request says was now put to its kind file by file, for the files of the three generators read without the lines: 32,000 files, 3,400 of them with such a value, 27 more left out because they have a row fewer than request lines; no value was left over. `failed` = 1 for a request whose part holds neither its `Response:` line nor an error (3,954 rows); `failed` = 1 for a request whose `Response:` line is in its part and whose `Response Data:` line is not (47 rows); `failed` = 0 for a request the lines end on, by such a `Response:` line of another request (none in these files, one row in 1,200 files of a sixth seed); and `failed` = 1 with the text of an error for a request that wrote none, by the ERROR line of the score code of another request (235 rows, all of the one generator whose requests fail). No code decides them: for each kind a test holds two files that are the same byte for byte and need different rows, and `test_log_parser_generated.py` sorts the values of its own files in every run of the tests and fails on a value of no kind. The parser is unchanged.
- **The collection script redacts a value that holds a byte that is no UTF-8.** `redact_llm_log` ran `sed` in the locale of the shell. In a UTF-8 locale GNU sed matches such a byte with neither `.` nor a bracket expression, so the redaction ended there. Measured on 2026-10-13 with GNU sed 4.9 of Git for Windows in `en_US.UTF-8` and in `C.UTF-8`: `Cookie: abc<FF>secret-value` became `Cookie: REDACTED<FF>secret-value` and `x API_KEY:sk-abc<FF>secret, y` became `x API_KEY:REDACTED<FF>secret, y` (`<FF>` being the byte 0xFF); in the C locale both were redacted whole. The function now runs `LC_ALL=C sed -E`. The tests run the redaction and the whole script with such bytes. The script without the locale ran in the second pass of 2026-10-13 against the model pods of the test system - its 441 `API_KEY:` values and 45 `Cookie` headers were all `REDACTED` -; with the locale it ran once in the third pass of that day, from Git Bash with GNU sed 4.9 against the model pods of the test system: nine containers, 5,767 lines, 82 `API_KEY:` values, all `REDACTED`. That collection held no `Cookie`, `Authorization` or `X-CSRF-TOKEN` header and no byte that is no UTF-8, so the redaction of such a byte ran through the function of the script on staged lines only: `Cookie: abc<FF>secret` came out as `Cookie: REDACTED` and `x API_KEY:sk-abc<FF>secret, y` as `x API_KEY:REDACTED, y`. The script is not run on Linux.
- **LLM - Get Options 1.2: *Save the code to a filename?* without a path no longer ends with an error.** Measured on 2026-10-13 with version 1.1, *Save the code to a filename?* ticked and no path: the step wrote its code file (34 records) and then ended with `ERROR 180-322`, `SYSCC` 3000 and a failed job. Its DATA step had put the statement `filename _slo_LLM temp;` into a macro variable with the semicolon, and the closing NOTE resolved that variable, so the semicolon ended the NOTE. Version 1.2 keeps the statement without the semicolon, as *LLM - Create LLM Call* does, and the NOTE reads it through `%SUPERQ`. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with version 1.2, the box ticked, no path and a model entered by hand, as a program and through the code SAS Studio generates for the step: the code file (34 lines), the NOTE `The filename _slo_LLM has been assigned, the source is available at filename _slo_LLM temp - use %include _slo_LLM to run the code` on one line, and `SYSCC` 0. *LLM - Create LLM Call* 1.3 gave its 34 lines both ways and, as a program for a second model, 36, and both steps wrote their file to a path in SAS Content. What the two steps did as they come - the selector on a table - is the entry *a run without a model says so* below.
- **An apostrophe in the path of the code file (LLM - Create LLM Call 1.3, LLM - Get Options 1.2).** Both steps wrote the path between two apostrophes into the statement that assigns the file, so an apostrophe in a folder or file name ended that text early. The statement is made with the QUOTE function, which doubles it; for a path without one it is the statement of version 1.0. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with versions 1.3 and 1.2 and a path of the form `sasserver:/tmp/<folder>_o'brien/call.sas`: both steps wrote the file there (34 lines) and ended with `SYSCC` 0, and the NOTE of *LLM - Get Options* named the statement with the apostrophe doubled, on one line. A quotation mark in a path reaches the DATA step whole. Measured in that pass with the code SAS Studio 2026.09 generates for a path control that holds `sasserver:/tmp/zz_verify_r20a_p;q'r"s&t%u/call.sas`: SAS Studio writes the value masked (`%nrquote(%nrstr(...))`, a percent sign in front of each quotation mark), and a DATA step that read it as `"&_slo_fl_path."`, the way both steps read it, held the 50 characters of the path; `SYMGET` gave the same 50. The steps were not run with such a path.
- **LLM - Create LLM Call 1.4 and LLM - Get Options 1.3: a run without a model says so.** Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with versions 1.3 and 1.2 through the code SAS Studio generates for the steps as they come - *Select name from a table*, no table connected, no model name selected: SAS Studio wrote no macro variable for the table and the model name, the program read both (`WARNING: Apparent symbolic reference _SLO_MOD_NM_TAB_1 not resolved.`, `ERROR 22-322` and `ERROR 76-322` in PROC SQL) and then ended with `no code was written, because SAS Viya did not answer the request for the options file  with HTTP 200 (status: 404)` and `SYSCC` 1012. Both steps now ask with `%SYMEXIST` for the macro variable of a control before they read it. A run without a model name or without an options file - the state the steps come with, a model whose row holds no `optionsFileURI`, manual entry without a name or without a URI - asks SAS Viya for nothing and ends with one ERROR line: `no code was written, because no model with an options file was chosen - with Select name from a table the step needs a model table that has the columns name and optionsFileURI and a model name selected from it, otherwise a model name and an options file URI.` Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with versions 1.4 and 1.3 through the code SAS Studio generates for the steps as they come: no `Apparent symbolic reference` and no other WARNING, PROC SQL and PROC HTTP did not run, the one ERROR line - which the SAS log breaks over two lines - and `SYSCC` 1012.
- **LLM - Create LLM Call 1.4 and LLM - Get Options 1.3: the `optionsFileURI` of a model table without the blanks of its column.** Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with a model table whose column `optionsFileURI` was 200 characters wide: both steps asked for `<uri><blanks>/content`, got HTTP 404, wrote no code and ended with `SYSCC` 1012, in 3 of 3 runs; the same runs with a column of 49 characters, which is what *LLM - List Available LLMs* writes, completed. In these runs the pass set the macro variables of the table selector itself; the code SAS Studio generates for a connected table was not used. `select optionsFileURI into :_slo_optionsFileURI` kept the blanks of the column; it is `into ... trimmed` now. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13 with a model table whose column `optionsFileURI` is 200 characters wide, the macro variables of the table selector again set by the pass: both steps wrote their code file for two models (34 and 36 lines) and ended with `SYSCC` 0, in 4 of 4 runs; manual entry of a name and a URI through the code SAS Studio generates gave 34 lines and `SYSCC` 0.
- **Every `%put`, `%let` and macro call of the LLM and MM steps was read.** They were read for a macro variable the program makes while it runs and then resolves with an ampersand, which is the kind of the defect above: 18 `%put`, 85 `%let` and 36 macro calls in nine steps (all but *LLM - Log Parser*). The NOTE of *LLM - Get Options* was the one that could carry a semicolon or a quotation mark. A test holds the reading.
- **`Create-LLM-Call.sas` keeps the fileref its comment names.** With its default `_slo_fl_path = temp` the script wrote the code to a temporary file, cleared the fileref `_slo_LLM` - which deletes a temporary file - and wrote `NOTE: The script is available at temp`. The fileref is no longer cleared and the NOTE names it. The comment of the setting says so in plain words, and the header no longer names an output table the script never made. Not run in SAS.
- **MM - Get Model Information 1.1.** The program ended with a statement that deleted the macro of *MM - Get Models in Project*, which this step does not define, and its header comment was the one of that step. The statement is gone and the comment names this step. Not run in SAS; what SAS wrote for the statement was not measured.
- **Start Here: a reference is no entry of the content folder.** Measured on 2026-10-13: with a file of the folder replaced by a reference of its name that pointed at a file of another folder, the page read `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (file) | ok | present` and `70 of 72 entries`, while `mdb content-deploy` reported that entry as FAILED. The page read the kind of a member from its `contentType`, which for a reference is the kind of the object it names. A member whose type says `reference` is now taken for no file, step, job definition, report or folder and is not followed. An entry whose name only a reference to an object of its kind has is `missing`, with the advice to remove the reference. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with the page as it was then and the file replaced by a reference of its name to a file elsewhere: `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (file) | missing | a reference of that name is in its folder, and the object it names lives elsewhere | Remove the reference from the folder. Then: Import the package of the accelerator again, or run mdb content-deploy.` and `69 of 72 entries of release.json are present`.
- **Start Here judges a reference by its name and kind, as `mdb` does.** Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with a file and a reference of its name in one folder: `mdb content-deploy` and `mdb builders-import` reported the entry as FAILED (`... nothing says which one is meant - remove the reference`, exit code 1), and the page said `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (file) | ok | present` for the same folder. The page now has the rule of `mdb` for a reference: a reference that has the name of an entry and names an object of the kind of the entry stands in the way of that entry, alone or beside the object - but for a report that is there, which stays `ok | present`: `mdb` writes no report and fails none over a reference. Beside the object the line is `missing | present, and a reference of that name is in its folder, which names an object that lives elsewhere | Remove the reference from the folder: while it is there, the import and mdb content-deploy write nothing for this entry.`; the entry is still counted in `N of M entries of release.json are present`, and its line is shown above the folded list of every entry. A reference to an object of another kind is no part of the check: `mdb` writes the object beside it, and the page had said `Remove the reference`. The line of `release.json` is judged the same way. Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13, with the file `tokens.py` and a reference of its name to a `.csv` file in one folder: the row `RAG/rag_core/tokens.py (file) | missing | present, and a reference of that name is in its folder, which names an object that lives elsewhere | Remove the reference from the folder: ...`, `70 of 72 entries of release.json are present` as without the reference, and 97 `ok` and 7 `missing` against 98 and 6; with a reference to a folder under that name the row was `ok | present`. With a `.py` file as the target of the reference SAS Viya answered HTTP 409 (errorCode 11552, `An item named ... of type "File" already exists in the folder`), in 4 of 4 attempts with four file names, and it accepted the reference to the `.csv` file; why the target decides was not determined. A report with a reference beside it was not staged: no test root held a report. What still differed from `mdb` - the name of a step, two objects of one name, and how the kind of a member is told - is the entry *Start Here and `mdb content-deploy` have one rule* below.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables and `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`: a folder that is not there writes no ERROR line of the FILENAME statement.** Measured on 2026-10-13 with a content root without `Logging and Monitoring`: the FILENAME statement for the program of that folder wrote `ERROR: Folder denoted by path ... was not found.` and `ERROR: Error in the FILENAME statement.` in front of the WARNING the job means. Both programs now ask the Folders service for a folder first (`GET /folders/folders/@item?path=`, the request *Start Here* asks with) and assign no fileref where it answers 404; every other answer assigns it as before. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 with the job as changed and a content root without `Logging and Monitoring`: PROC HTTP wrote `NOTE: 404 Not Found` and no ERROR line, no ERROR line of a FILENAME statement followed, the log held the WARNING that `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` was not found, and the release table was loaded (781 rows). The job still ended as failed: none of the three fact sheet tables was in the CAS library of the run, so the check at its end wrote `ERROR: Load-Accelerator-Tables: not in the CAS library casuser at the end of the run: LLM_FACT_SHEET EMBEDDING_FACT_SHEET DECISION_FACT_SHEET.` and `SYSCC` was 1012. That is what the job does whenever one of its four tables is not in the CAS library at its end; its header and the README now say so for this case in plain words. A copy of `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` with test table names, pointed at a folder that is not there, wrote `NOTE: 404 Not Found`, one WARNING per sheet and no ERROR line, and ended with `SYSCC` 0.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables: `releaseTable` and a table that is only its saved file.** The job replaces a table of that name only where it is a release table, and looked for it in memory only. A table that is only the file it was saved to - `LLM_LOGS` after a restart of the CAS server - was not looked at, and the save of the release table wrote over its file. Where no table of that name is in memory, the job now lists the files of the CAS library, loads `<name>.sashdat` as a table of its session, reads its columns and drops it. A file that lacks the columns of a release table or cannot be loaded is left as it is, with an ERROR line and `SYSCC` 1012; a saved release table is loaded again. Where CAS refuses to list the files, the job drops the upload again and loads no table, with an ERROR line of its own and `SYSCC` 1012 (see the entry on a listing that CAS refused). Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13 in `casuser`, which held files of other tables: with a table of 19 rows that was only its saved file and is no release table, the job loaded the file as `_LAT_SAVED`, wrote `ERROR: Load-Accelerator-Tables: no table casuser.<name> is in memory, and the file a table of that name was saved to in the CAS library casuser could not be loaded or lacks columns of a release table. The file was left as it is and nothing was loaded: ...` (the wording of that build; the line now ends `the upload of ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv was dropped again and no table <caslib>.<table> was loaded: ...`), and the file had the size and the time of before; with a release table that was only its saved file, the job loaded the file as `_LAT_SAVED` and then loaded, promoted and saved the table (781 rows). What `table.fileInfo` answers for a CAS library without any file is still not measured.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables and `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`: a table that has the name of a table of the session is not touched.** Both programs load a table of their CAS session under a fixed name into the CAS library of the site and drop or promote it: the upload as `<table>_NEW`, and the job the saved file of a release table as `_LAT_SAVED`. A site can have a global table of such a name; whether a drop then takes the table of the session rests on what CAS does with a session table that has the name of a global one, which was measured once, behind this change (below). Both programs now ask `table.tableExists` before they load under the name and load only where the answer is 0, so the table they drop or promote is the one they loaded. Any other answer leaves both tables as they are: a WARNING that says to rename or drop `<table>_NEW`, and for `_LAT_SAVED` an ERROR line of its own (see *a table named `_LAT_SAVED` has an ERROR line of its own* below). Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13, in `casuser` with test table names. With a global table `<releaseTable>_NEW` of 19 rows made first: the WARNING with `tableExists said 2`, no upload, and both tables with the rows and the created time of before. `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas`, from a copy with three test table names: the same WARNING for `<table>_NEW`, that sheet not loaded, the other two loaded. A name a session table and a global table both had, in one run: `tableExists` said 1 in the session that held both and 2 in another session; the first `table.dropTable` took the table of the session, the second the global one. Not measured: `_LAT_SAVED` as a global table, `Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` with the table names it ships with, and the condition code of a run whose only message is the WARNING - every run of the pass ended with `SYSCC` 1012 for the three fact sheet tables its test root lacked. A table of such a name that the session may not see is not found by the question.
- **`Load-Fact-Sheets.sas` says which outcome a swap had.** Where the drop of the table was refused, the promote and the load from the saved file were refused as well, and the program wrote that the table could not be loaded again from its file while the table of before was still in memory. Behind a refused promote it now asks whether a table of that name is in memory: if one is, nothing is loaded and the WARNING says the table was left as it is; if none is, the table is loaded again from its file, and the WARNING says whether that worked. Not run in SAS: the passes of 2026-10-13 ran the swap without a refusal - the second and the third from a copy of the program with test table names, 30, 16 and 5 rows.
- **A custom step names no user: in its `.step` file, and as `mdb content-deploy` registers it.** The ten `.step` files that were saved from SAS Studio held the user id of the person who saved them in `createdBy` and `modifiedBy`, and `mdb content-deploy` registered a step by sending the bytes of its file, while the release package names no user. Both fields of the ten files now hold the text `mdb package-export` writes in the place of a user id (`sas-agentic-ai-accelerator`); the nine RAG steps have neither field. And the deploy leaves out of a step what belongs to the system it was saved on - the user and the time stamps, the links, the version, the eTag and the local texts -, the keys the import of a package leaves out, so a step reaches a site in one shape from either. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-13: `mdb content-deploy` registered the 18 custom steps of a test content root that way (`created step ...`), a second run found each `equal`, and the export of that root named the user who had signed in for the deploy, and no other, in `createdBy` and `modifiedBy` of every object it held - the dataFlows service stores the user who sends a step. Two fixtures of the `mdb` tests named a user of a system as well and name a made-up one now.
- **The credentials guide and the example manifest equip one group, the one whose members open the report.** *Managing Credentials* equipped `LLMConsumers` in its script example and `PromptEngineers` in its manifest examples, and `credentials.example.yaml` equipped `PromptEngineers`. A site wrote its manifest for `PromptEngineers` while the people who open the report were members of `LLMConsumers`: the apply succeeded, and the models stayed disabled with the note `No OpenRouter key available in the agentic-ai-keys credential domain`. Every example now names `LLMConsumers`, the group `mdb setup` writes the create commands for, and the page says that a credential has to go to a group the users are members of, with that note as the symptom. Its section on deleting no longer says that deleting is for the standard tools only.
- **`mdb` prints a value as it is, in every line, table cell, title and prompt.** The round before handed about forty values - exceptions and details of a smoke test - to one function before they are printed between colour tags. What was left: the messages and the state of an export job that ended failed (`The folder [/folders/folders/abc] could not be exported.` ended `mdb package-export` with a `MarkupError`, `error [0] in task [export]: timeout` was printed without `[export]`), the content root in three lines of `mdb package-export` (`/Teams/zz [two]/root` was printed as `/Teams/zz /root`), the response of a model in the table of `mdb test` (a response with `[/INST]` ended the command), a value of an options file, and the identity and `--only` names typed for `mdb credentials-add` and `mdb credentials-remove`. Every value of every text that is printed with markup on now goes through that function, also one `mdb` made itself; a test reads the syntax tree of the command line and fails on a value that does not. The function is exact now: it lost a backslash in front of a bracket that opens no tag, and one of two backslashes at the end of a value. A name between colons is no longer printed as a picture (`urn:id:1` lost `:id:`). What rich takes out of every text it prints stays out of a value as well - a carriage return, a bell, a backspace, a vertical tab and a form feed; a tab is printed as blanks. Since a later commit of this release, mdb also takes out every other control character before rich reads the text: ESC, NUL, DEL and the C1 range of an answer of a server or of a model reached the terminal, which acted on an escape sequence in it. The `--json` output keeps them as JSON escapes, and now writes every character outside ASCII as an escape. A value mdb names in a sentence of its own stands between single quotes as it is: the credentials and sign-in sentences used Python's repr, which doubled a backslash. Where the whitespace of a value is the point, it is still shown as Python writes a text, with `\n` for a line break: the commit `release.json` names, the version, a kind and a generator of the content manifest, an endpoint in a refusal of `mdb generate`, and the first 60 characters of a model's answer in a smoke test. Two brackets of `mdb`'s own had never been printed: `[reasoning]`, `[embedding]` and `[decision]` behind a model in the list of `mdb add`, and `[y/N]` in a question of the hf-selfhosted adapter. Tested with made-up answers, and with rich 13.0.0, 13.9.4, 14.3.3 and 15.0.0 - the package takes rich from 13.0 on -, the removal of the other control characters and the quotes with rich 15.0.0 only. Measured on 2026-10-14 in a test content root, before those two: a folder named `zz [two]`, a content root that is not there (`.../not [/there]`), an identity with brackets, and a write and an export that the harness of the pass refused with a reason of its own (`[bold]no markup[/bold] [/red] [two] a\b :warning:`) were each printed as text, on one line. An export job that the transfer service itself ends as failed was not seen. In the second pass of 2026-10-14, on the workstation, `mdb credentials-add` with the identity `ZZ<ESC>[31mX` printed `ZZ[31mX`, and its output - read from a file, not watched on a terminal - held no ESC byte. The other control characters and the quotes have not been looked at in a pass.
- **`mdb content-deploy`: every hint of a `FAILED` line runs as it is printed.** Three did not. For a reference in the place of the folder `Data` the line named `--only "Data" --to <the SAS Content path of that folder>`, which ended with exit code 2 where that folder is not called `Data`: the job definition `Data/Load-Accelerator-Tables` reads the content root it sits in. The line says so for such a folder: `- a path that ends with /Data, with the content root in front of it, because the job definition Data/Load-Accelerator-Tables reads the content root it sits in`. In a run of a part that goes somewhere else - `--only RAG --to <folder>`, which the `deploy-rag-content` scripts run - the hints named an entry by its path below that part (`--only "rag_core/tokens.py"`), which `--only` does not take; they name the path the manifest lists. And for `release.json` the two lines named `--only "release.json" --to ...`, a run the command refuses; they name no command and say `release.json is written only by a run that covers every entry of the manifest`. Tests run the hints as printed on the manifest of the repository: for every folder, every job definition, two custom steps and two files, in a content root and in three parts. The hints spell the path of an entry in double quotes; a test holds that no path of the manifest holds a character double quotes do not keep. Measured on 2026-10-14 in a test content root, each hint run as printed: for a reference in the place of the folder `Data`, the hint with a path that ends with `/Data` gave a dry run of 5 entries, all `equal`, and a folder of another name ended with exit code 2 before the sign-in; in `--only RAG --to <folder>`, with a reference in the place of `tokens.py`, the hint `--only "RAG/rag_core/tokens.py" --to <its URI>` was refused without `--force` and with it updated the object the reference names, with its id kept; and the line of a reference named `release.json` named no command.
- **`mdb content-deploy`: what is left alone is called by the word the other lines use.** The list `Not in the manifest and left alone` said `(jobDefinition)`, `reference to a jobDefinition` and, for an object of no kind of the manifest, the first part of its address (`reference to a casManagement`). It says `(job definition)`, `(registered step)`, `(reference to a registered step - not the job definition that is listed under this name)`; an object of no kind of the manifest is called by the content type the Folders service gives it, and where it gives none by its address. `(file)`, `(folder)` and `(report)` read as before. Measured on 2026-10-14 in a test content root: `(registered step)`, `(job definition)`, `(reference to a folder - not the file that is listed under this name)` and `(reference to a registered step - not the job definition that is listed under this name)`; a reference to a CAS table, which the Folders service took, was listed as `(reference to a casTable)`.
- **An empty `--only`, `--content-root` or `--domain` is refused.** `mdb content-deploy --only ""` - what `--only "$PART"` gives where the variable is not set - and `--only /` deployed every entry of the manifest, `release.json` with them. An empty `--content-root` was read as not given, the content root of the release, by `mdb content-deploy`, `builders-import`, `package-export`, `options-save` and `options-restore`; an empty `--domain` made `mdb credentials-init` write a manifest that names no domain, which is read as one for `agentic-ai-keys`. Each ends the command with exit code 2 and one line, before the sign-in and before a file is written. Run on 2026-10-14 with the address of the test system set: `--only ""`, `--only /`, an empty `--content-root` for each of the five commands and an empty `--domain` each ended with exit code 2 and that line, sent no request and wrote no file.
- **A command a line names carries the file and the folder of the run.** `mdb options-save --file <file> --content-root <root>` named `mdb options-restore`; `mdb package-export --content-root <root>`, for an export that differs from the checkout, named `mdb content-deploy`, which deploys into the content root of the release; `mdb credentials-apply --manifest <file>` named `mdb credentials-report`. Eight lines of this kind now name the command with what the run was given, spelled as PowerShell and bash take it; a run with the defaults names the command alone, as before. Tests run the commands as printed. On 2026-10-14 the `mdb credentials-report --manifest <file>` that `mdb credentials-apply` named was run as printed; the other lines of this kind have not run against SAS Viya.
- **An address of SAS Viya that cannot be used is refused before any request.** `SAS_VIYA_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4983x` - a port that is no number - was asked at the default port and ended with a `ConnectionError`; `SAS_VIYA_URL=viya.example.com:8443` - a port and no scheme - made sasctl ask `viya.example.com://viya.example.com:8443/...`. A colon or a slash without a scheme in front of the host, a port that is no number from 1 to 65535, a scheme that is neither https nor http, a second `//` behind the scheme (`http://http://host`, which made sasctl ask the host `http`), a blank inside the address, an `@` as a user or a password in front of the host has one, no host, and an IPv6 address in brackets each end the command with exit code 2 and one line that names the value and says what it has to look like: `https://<host>`, or `https://<host>:<port>`. An empty port - `SAS_VIYA_URL=http://127.0.0.1:`, a colon with nothing behind it - was asked at the default port as well; it now ends the command with the line of a port that is no number and exit code 2, with and without a path behind it. Tested on the workstation, `mdb list` with a token and with the password: exit code 2, the line, no request; not run against SAS Viya. A user and a password are shown as `***@` in that line. A host alone is still taken as https. Blanks, tabs and line breaks at either end of the value are taken out, as of every variable `mdb` reads for the sign-in. Tested with the installed sasctl. Measured on 2026-10-14 with the host of the test system: `<host>:443` and `https://<host>:443x` each ended with exit code 2, the line and no request, and `https://<host>:443` and the host alone made the dry run they make without a port. The forms added after that pass were measured in the second pass of 2026-10-14: `https://https://<host>`, `https://u:x@<host>` and `https:// <host>` each ended with exit code 2, one line and no request, the second shown as `https://***@<host>`. That the ends of the value are taken out has not run against SAS Viya.
- **Another status of the token address is one line, and `Unauthorized grant type` is asked once.** A 500 of `/SASLogon/oauth/token` - and a 400, 403, 429 or 503 - ended the command with the traceback of requests and exit code 1. It ends with `SAS Viya at <url> did not answer the sign-in of <user> ... (1 sign-in made) with a token: HTTP 500 from /SASLogon/oauth/token ... mdb reads only HTTP 401 as a refusal of the credentials, and asks no other answer again: ...` and exit code 2. An answer `Unauthorized grant type` was asked three times with 7 seconds of waiting - seen in the third pass of 2026-10-13 at a stand-in on the workstation, which is no answer of SAS Logon: 3 requests, `(3 sign-ins made)` -; it is asked once, like `Bad credentials`. `MDB_LOGIN_ATTEMPTS` is as it was. Tested with the installed sasctl 1.11.8 and made-up answers; no pass got one of these answers from SAS Logon. On 2026-10-14 a stand-in on the workstation answered once with HTTP 500 and once with `Unauthorized grant type`: one request each and exit code 2, with this line for the 500 and the line of a refused sign-in, `(1 sign-in made)`, for the other. Three more answers ended with a traceback and end with that line now: a redirect whose target answers an error (`HTTP 404 from /elsewhere, where /SASLogon/oauth/token sent the sign-in on`), and a 200 whose body is no JSON or carries no `access_token` (`HTTP 200 from /SASLogon/oauth/token, without an access token mdb could read`). Tested with sasctl 1.11.8 and the transport of requests replaced. A 200 whose `access_token` is null, no text or empty, a redirect to a page that answers 200 and an address where nothing answers: see *Every outcome of the sign-in is one line*.
- **`mdb credentials-report`: the row of a credential that cannot be read shows the HTTP status.** The row showed the first 40 characters of `reading the credential for <type> '<id>' failed: HTTP <status>`, which ends in front of the status. It shows the sentence whole. Tested with a stand-in for the credentials service; not run against SAS Viya.
- **The credentials commands: one name for the manifest, and two things an edit no longer does without a word.** `mdb credentials-init` took its file as `--file` / `-f`, the four other commands as `--manifest` / `-m`; all five take `--manifest` / `-m`, and `credentials-init` still reads the earlier name. After the first `mdb credentials-add` on a manifest of `credentials-init` the comment `The first mdb credentials-add replaces this placeholder.` stood above a real identity; it leaves with the placeholder. An `only:` list with a comment between its entries was rewritten without the comment; such an edit is refused with a sentence and the file is as it was. `mdb credentials-add` takes `--domain` for a manifest it writes. The commands these commands print take a path with a blank. Run: the test suite, in which the credentials service is a stand-in in memory. Measured on 2026-10-14: `credentials-add --domain` wrote a manifest with that domain and without the comment about the placeholder, and an `only:` list with a comment was refused with the file unchanged, the same sha256 before and after.
- **The five decision definitions name the accelerator as their modeler.** `clef_flash`, `jev_1_13`, `liquid_d1`, `pplx_decider_27b` and `von_1_2` carried the user id of the person who ran `mdb add`, which takes the modeler from `SAS_RESPONSIBLE_PARTY` and, where that is not set, from the user of the machine. They carry `SAS Agentic AI Accelerator` like every other definition; per definition `definition.yaml`, `modelConfiguration.json` and the two hashes in `.mdb-lock.json` changed, and no score code. Not registered again in this round.
- **Start Here and `mdb content-deploy` have one rule for what a folder holds under the name of an entry.** Two differences were left. For a step the page looked for objects and references under the name with and without the ending `.step`, where `mdb` takes the name of the entry as it is: a reference named like a step without its ending made the page say `missing` while `mdb` found the step `equal`. And for two objects of the name and kind of an entry `mdb` says `FAILED`, while the page said `ok | present`. The page now tells a member the way `mdb` does - by the name of the entry to the letter and by what its uri starts with, for an object as for a reference. Two objects of the name and kind of an entry give `missing | 2 objects of that name and kind are in its folder, and nothing says which one is meant | Remove or rename all but one of them: while they are there, the import and mdb content-deploy write nothing for this entry.`; the entry is counted in `N of M entries of release.json are present`, as it is with a reference beside the object. Below a name two folders have the page reads nothing - the order of the listing would decide which folder is read - and the entries there are `not checked`; of two files named `release.json` neither is read, and the page then lists no entry. The line of a folder with a second folder or a reference of its name beside it ends `Nothing is written below the folder either.` A step that is registered under its name without `.step` is no longer the step of the entry: the manifest and every step file name a step with the ending. One difference stays: below a folder with a reference to a folder beside it the page reads that folder, where `mdb` looks at nothing below a folder it fails and lists its entries as `FAILED`. `tests/test_start_here_one_rule.py` holds the rule as one table and asks both for each row - the Python of the page, and `deploy` of `mdb` in a dry run - on the same made-up folder. Measured on 2026-10-14 in two test content roots, the page against a dry run of `mdb content-deploy`: beside a reference named `LLM - Log Parser`, without the ending, to the registered step, the step was `ok | present` and `equal`; beside a reference named `LLM - Log Parser.step` to a `.csv` file the step was `ok | present` and `equal`, and the reference was listed as `(reference to a file - not the registered step that is listed under this name)`; and with the folder `RAG/rag_core` replaced by a reference to a folder elsewhere the page said `missing | a reference of that name is in its folder, and the object it names lives elsewhere` and `missing | its folder is not in the content root` for the entries below it, and `mdb` said `FAILED` for the folder and below it. SAS Viya refused with HTTP 409 every attempt to put two objects of one name and kind into one folder - a second file, a second folder, a step registered twice, a second `release.json` - and a reference beside an object of its name and kind, in both orders: two objects of one name and kind, a name two folders have and the difference that stays were not reached.
- **Start Here: the advice for a report that is missing names what brings a report.** For a report that is not in its folder the page advised `Import the package of the accelerator again, or run mdb content-deploy.`, and `mdb content-deploy` writes no report (`not deployed report ... (not on the site - it comes with the package)`). The line now says `Import the package of the accelerator again, or run mdb builders-import: a report comes with the package, and mdb content-deploy writes none.` - also behind `Remove the reference from the folder. Then: ` and where the folder of the report is not in the content root. Measured on 2026-10-14 for two test content roots without the reports: both report rows read `missing | not in its folder` with that line.
- **Load-Accelerator-Tables: a table named `_LAT_SAVED` has an ERROR line of its own.** Measured in the third pass of 2026-10-13, with a table `_LAT_SAVED` of the session (`tableExists` said 1) and a release table that was only its saved file: the job wrote the ERROR line of a file that `could not be loaded or lacks columns of a release table`, with the remedies `the parameter releaseTable names another table, or that file is to be removed first` - and the next run, without that table, loaded 796 rows from the same file. The line also said `nothing was loaded` behind the upload of the CSV file, and not what CAS had answered. The job now writes `ERROR: Load-Accelerator-Tables: no table <caslib>.<table> is in memory, and the file a table of that name was saved to in the CAS library <caslib> was not looked at. The job loads that file as a table _LAT_SAVED of its session to read its columns, and CAS did not answer that there is no table <caslib>._LAT_SAVED - tableExists said <n>, and 0 is no table. The job loads over and drops no table it did not load: where a table of that name is there, rename or drop it and run the job again. The file was left as it is, the upload of ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv was dropped again and no table <caslib>.<table> was loaded.` The job ends with `SYSCC` 1012 as before. The line of a file that could not be loaded or lacks columns stays, and ends `the upload of ACCELERATOR_RELEASES.csv was dropped again and no table <caslib>.<table> was loaded: ...` where it said `nothing was loaded`. Measured on 2026-10-14 in `casuser`, with a test release table that was only its saved file and a table `_LAT_SAVED` of the session with 19 rows: the line with `tableExists said 1`, `SYSCC` 1012, `_LAT_SAVED` with its 19 rows, and the saved file with its size and its time; without that table the job loaded 822 rows from the file. Each run of that test root also wrote the ERROR line for the three fact sheets the root lacks. `_LAT_SAVED` as a global table is not measured.
- **`Create-LLM-Call.sas`: without a model name and an options file it asks for nothing.** The script is the third copy of the program of *LLM - Create LLM Call* and *LLM - Get Options*. It comes with `_slo_LLM_name` and `_slo_optionsFileURI` empty and sent its request all the same, for the address `<host>/content`, and then named an empty options file. It has the three lines the steps got in 1.4 and 1.3: a run without a value for both sends no request and ends with `ERROR: Create-LLM-Call.sas: no code was written, because no model with an options file was chosen - the script needs a model name in _slo_LLM_name and the URI of its options file in _slo_optionsFileURI.` and `SYSCC` 1012. A URI without a name is such a run as well; the script wrote code for it that calls `<endpoint>//`. A test holds the part of the program the three copies share equal in the three. Measured on 2026-10-14 as a program: as it comes, with a model name alone and with a URI alone, no PROC HTTP, no `Apparent symbolic reference`, that ERROR line as the only one of the script and `SYSCC` 1012; with both, for gpt_41_mini, one `NOTE: 200 OK`, 36 lines of code and `SYSCC` 0. In front of the first line of the program every log holds the two messages of the start of the session that could not connect to CAS, on three log lines of the type error.
- **The credentials commands.** `mdb credentials-report` counts an identity whose credential could not be read neither as holding one nor as gone, and says how many could not be read; before, an identity marked `state: absent` whose read was refused was counted as gone. A refused read, removal or write names the reason the service gave (message and errorCode), where before it showed only `HTTP <status>`. A write shows the reason only for a 404, and not where the answer holds a value the write carried (compared as the line prints the answer since the entry *The answer of a refused credential write is compared as the line prints it*). The id and the domain are percent-encoded as one segment of the address of a credential: an id with `#` or `?` was cut there, and one with `/../` reached another path; `.` and `..` alone are refused. A manifest that YAML cannot read is refused with the line and column where the reader stopped and nothing of its text, because `--manifest .env` printed a line of the key file. A key added with `--only` where the last line of a file without a line end is cut leaves no empty line. The lines print ` - ` as the other commands do, and `1 entry stored.` Tested in the suite with a stand-in for the service; measured in the second pass of 2026-10-14: a write for a group that does not exist printed `storing the credential for group '<group>' failed: HTTP 404 The credential could not be created because the group identity "<group>" does not exist. (errorCode 12310)` and ended with exit code 1, and the dry run of a manifest that marks the identity `ZZ#X` absent asked for `.../groups/ZZ%23X`, which SAS Viya answered with 404 (errorCode 12302), and planned `remove | no credential - already gone`. The count of a credential that could not be read and a refusal with another status have not run against SAS Viya.
- **An empty file or folder option is refused.** `--manifest`, `--file`, `--env-file`, `--source`, `--keys-file` and `--out` were read as not given when empty, like `--only` before. For example, `mdb credentials-add group G --manifest ""` wrote `./credentials.yaml`, an apply read it and removed what it marks absent, and `mdb options-restore --file ""` ended with a traceback. Each now ends the command with exit code 2 and one line, before anything is read, written or asked - since the entry *Every option and argument of `mdb` that takes a path or a name refuses an empty value* for every command. `options-restore --file .` says that it names a folder, with exit code 2 like `options-save`. None of these runs sends a request. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-14 with the address of the test system set: `mdb credentials-apply --manifest ""` ended with exit code 2 and `--manifest is empty - what a variable of the shell that is not set gives - and names no file. Nothing was done.`, and sent no request.
- **`--content-root /` is refused for what it is.** `mdb content-deploy` and `mdb builders-import` said that the option must be a SAS Content path starting with a slash; they now say that it must name a folder and not the root of SAS Content. Measured in the second pass of 2026-10-14: `mdb content-deploy --content-root / --dry-run` ended with exit code 2 and `--content-root must name a SAS Content folder, starting with a slash - and not / itself, the root of SAS Content.`, and sent no request.
- **No traceback of `mdb` shows the values of a credential, whatever typer is installed.** typer 0.12.0, which `pyproject.toml` allows (`typer>=0.12`), prints the locals of every frame of a traceback by default. A request of `mdb credentials-apply` that raised - a connection reset, a timeout - ended in such a traceback, and the frame of the write held every value of the credential in plain text and in base64. Both Typer apps of `mdb` set `pretty_exceptions_show_locals=False`. The lower bound stays: the setting exists in 0.12.0 and overrides its default. A request of the credentials commands that raises ends with one line that names nothing of the request: `storing the credential for group 'G' failed: no answer (ReadTimeout)`, and likewise `reading` / `removing the credential ...` and `creating or updating the domain '<domain>' failed: no answer (...)`. Measured with typer 0.12.0 and click 8.1.7 in a scratch environment, against a stand-in for the service: at the earlier tip a raising write printed both made-up values and their base64, and a defect inside the write printed both values twice; with the change neither printed any. The whole `mdb` suite passes with typer 0.12.0 as with 0.27.0. Not run against SAS Viya.
- **The answer of a refused credential write is compared as the line prints it.** A write shows the answer of a 404 only where it holds no value the write carried. The values were compared as they are, with a line in which every run of blanks, tabs and line breaks is one blank, and in which an answer that is no JSON ends at 300 characters. So a value with two blanks, a tab or a line break that the answer echoed was printed collapsed, and a value the cut split was printed in part. The values, plain and in base64, are now compared the same way, with the line and with the whole answer. Tested with stand-in answers; not run against SAS Viya.
- **The default a prompt of `mdb add` shows loses its control characters.** rich prints the default of a prompt as a text of its own, outside the console of `mdb`, so an ESC, an OSC sequence, the C1 CSI byte, NUL, DEL, SO or NEL in a default - `metadata.description`, `release_date` and `knowledge_cutoff` come from the catalog of a provider - reached the terminal. The default is now cleaned like every other text; the value the prompt returns is unchanged. Tested; not run in a terminal against a provider.
- **Every outcome of the sign-in is one line.** Each of these ended with a traceback or with a misleading line, and now ends with exit code 2 and one line:
  - a 200 of the token address whose `access_token` is null, a number, a list or empty: `... did not answer the sign-in of <user> with SAS_VIYA_USER + SAS_VIYA_PASSWORD with a token: the answer to the sign-in holds no access token mdb could read. ...`; before, the first request ended with exit code 1 and a TypeError on the line of that request;
  - a redirect of the token address to a page that answers 200 - a login page in HTML, or JSON without a token: `... HTTP 200 from /SASLogon/login, where /SASLogon/oauth/token sent the sign-in on, without an access token mdb could read. ...`;
  - an address where nothing answers - a port nothing listens on, a certificate, a timeout - with SAS_VIYA_USER + SAS_VIYA_PASSWORD: `SAS Viya at <url> could not be asked for the sign-in of <user> with SAS_VIYA_USER + SAS_VIYA_PASSWORD: ConnectionError (<what requests says>). Check SAS_VIYA_URL and that SAS Viya can be reached from here.` With SAS_VIYA_TOKEN or the SAS Viya CLI login the line says `could not be asked with the access token (SAS_VIYA_TOKEN)` - or `(the SAS Viya CLI login)` -: see *With a token or a CLI login, an address where nothing answers is one line*.

  Tested with the installed sasctl (1.11.8) and the transport of requests replaced; not run against SAS Viya.
- **Every option and argument of `mdb` that takes a path or a name refuses an empty value.** The class was closed option by option, and some were left:
  - `mdb builders-import --options ""` read `.` and named `mdb options-save --file .`, which is refused;
  - an empty `--caslib` of `load-facts`, `load-releases` and `builders-import` loaded into Public;
  - an empty `--compute-context` or `--name` fell back to the default;
  - `mdb content-deploy --only` with a tab was taken as a path.

  One mechanism now refuses a value that is empty or blanks alone for every parameter that takes a text or a path - every option and argument of every command, `mdb provider` included - with exit code 2 and the one line each command printed before (`<option> is empty - what a variable of the shell that is not set gives - and names no <what>. Nothing was done.`), before the command does anything. Only `mdb test --prompt` and `--system` take an empty value: it is the text that is sent. `builders-import --options` refuses a folder, as `options-restore --file` does. A test takes the parameters from the command line interface itself: 85, each with an empty value, a blank and a tab. None of these runs sends a request.
- **The credentials commands refuse what they cannot use:**
  - A `--manifest` that names a folder was reported as 'No credentials manifest' with a `credentials-init` command that ends with 'already exists' as printed, and `credentials-add` printed `[Errno 13] Permission denied`. All five commands now end with exit code 2 and `<folder> is a folder; --manifest names the credentials manifest, a file. Nothing was done.` (`--manifest / --file` for `credentials-init`).
  - `--env-file`, `--keys-file` and `--identity-type` were ignored without a word with a manifest: `mdb credentials-apply -m m.yaml --env-file prod.env` wrote the values of the manifest's source and said `Done.`. Each is refused with a manifest, as a `--domain` of another domain is: `<option> is read only with --identity: <manifest> names the key file of each identity. Nothing was asked of SAS Viya.`, with `the type of each identity` for `--identity-type`.`--identity-type` shows no literal default in `--help` any more; `group` is still the default with `--identity`.
  - `--identity G --identity G` wrote the credential twice and the report counted it twice; it is refused like an identity a manifest lists twice.
  - A `--domain` with blanks around it is refused: see *An identity id and a credential domain are printable ASCII*.
  - An identity id with a line break - which was printed over two lines, and with U+0085 made `credentials-add` refuse a manifest it writes itself - is refused, and so is any id with a character outside printable ASCII or a blank at either end: every line names an id unquoted, and `' G1'` was added beside `G1` as a second identity that every line called `G1`. See *An identity id and a credential domain are printable ASCII*.

  Tested with a stand-in for the credentials service; not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb retire` names what kept it from SAS Viya.** Every sign-in that failed and every address `mdb` refuses - an address where nothing answers, a sign-in SAS Logon refused, a blank inside `SAS_VIYA_URL` - was printed as `SAS Viya is not configured - the registered model's status was not updated.`, and the reason was dropped. The line is now `The registered model's status was not updated: <the sentence the other commands end with>`. `mdb retire` still tags and regenerates the definition and ends with exit code 0. The audit script of the skill `model-audit` printed `SAS Viya is not configured: <reason>` in the same cases and now prints `SAS Viya could not be used: <reason>`; a test takes every handler of these errors from the code and fails on one that says *not configured*. Tested with a stand-in for sasctl; not run against SAS Viya.
- **`mdb package-export` says which differences of a test folder stay.** For a folder other than the content root of the release, the line of the differences advised `mdb content-deploy --content-root <folder>` to bring the folder to the checkout.
